AG Grid is recommended because of the large column count and enterprise grid interaction requirements.

## 6. Filter Row

Each filterable column has a filter textbox directly below that column's header.

Rules:

- Filter input width equals the current column width.
- Filters are aligned with their columns.
- Filtering starts only after more than two characters are entered.
- Clearing the input removes the filter.
- Multiple filters combine using logical AND.
- Filtering occurs before pagination.
- Header controls such as Action, Comment, and Documents may use specialized filtering instead of free text if required by the approved screen.

## 7. Default Period

The page initially uses the previous calendar month.

Example:

```text
Current month: August 2026
Default selected period: 07/2026
```

Changing the global period reloads the progress dataset and resets the page number to the first page. Column visibility and column widths may remain unchanged during the same session.

## 8. Action Column

The Action column displays exactly one navigation link based on the centralized action-resolution service:

- `Edit` when the journal is unassigned and the current user is authorized to edit or assign it.
- `Cert` when the journal is in an actionable Preparer or Approver bucket for the current user.
- `View` when the journal has moved to another role bucket, is closed, or is otherwise read-only for the current role.

The grid does not directly execute Certify, Approve, Reject, or Decertify. Those actions exist inside the journal detail page.

## 9. Row Navigation

### Edit

```text
Edit -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=edit
```

The detail API independently validates that the journal is unassigned and the current role may edit it.

### Cert

```text
Cert -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=action
```

The detail page resolves the valid button set:

- Preparer: Certify.
- Approver: Approve and Reject.

### View

```text
View -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=view
```

The detail page is read-only except for the specific Preparer Decertify case while the journal is pending Approver.

## 10. Status and Indicators

The progress grid presents workflow and supporting-data indicators, including:

- Status.
- Comment availability.
- Document availability.
- Final Certification State.
- Completed flag.
- Posting Status.
- Header Status display.

Indicator values are view-model fields derived by the backend. The frontend must not infer financial status from unrelated fields.

## 11. Currency and Totals

The grid displays source and USD values where available:

- Total Debit.
- Total Credit.
- Conversion rate to USD.
- Total Debit USD.
- Total Credit USD.

Currency values use decimal-safe backend calculations and locale-consistent display formatting. The UI does not recalculate authoritative financial totals.

## 12. API Response

Example shape:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "period": "07/2026",
    "rows": [
      {
        "journal_id": "JRN001",
        "status": "PENDING_PREPARER",
        "action": "CERT",
        "action_label": "Cert",
        "is_read_only": false
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

The backend returns the resolved action code and label. The UI does not duplicate the workflow rules in multiple grid components.

## 13. Empty State

```text
No journals were found for the selected period and filters.
```

If no period data exists before local filters are applied, use an informational empty state. Empty results are not errors.

## 14. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_PROGRESS_LOAD_FAILED
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
SESSION_EXPIRED
```

The shared notification module presents a proper popup or error state. Raw exceptions and stack traces are prohibited.

## 15. Accessibility

- Grid supports keyboard navigation.
- Action links have descriptive accessible labels including the journal identifier.
- Filter inputs have accessible names based on their column.
- Status icons include text or accessible labels.
- Horizontal scrolling does not prevent keyboard access.

## 16. Performance

- Retrieve only fields needed by the grid.
- Use indexed period, status, assignment-user, and journal identifiers.
- Avoid loading document binary content in the progress response.
- Return document and comment counts/flags instead.
- Use memoized column definitions.
- Debounce filter input after the minimum character rule.

## 17. Audit

Viewing, filtering, sorting, or paging does not create journal audit events. Opening a row may be application logged for diagnostics but does not change workflow status.

## 18. Acceptance Criteria

- Previous month is selected by default.
- Complete approved column set is available.
- Filter textboxes align below their columns.
- Filtering starts after more than two characters.
- Edit, Cert, and View resolve correctly.
- Detail actions do not execute from the grid.
- Unauthorized journals cannot be opened through URL manipulation.
- Grid errors use centralized messages and correlation IDs.


# View-Journal-Progress-Column-Matrix

# View Journal Progress Column Matrix

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the complete business column inventory supplied for the View Journal Progress grid. It distinguishes displayed operational fields, JDE/source fields, derived fields, indicators, financial values, and duplicate business labels.

## 2. Implementation Rule

The source CSV contains duplicate labels such as `Team` and `Preparer`. The API and frontend must use unique technical field names while preserving the approved user-facing labels.

Example:

```text
team_assignment       -> display label Team
team_source           -> display label Team
preparer_display_name -> display label Preparer
preparer_source_id    -> display label Preparer
```

Duplicate labels must not produce duplicate JSON keys.

## 3. Column Matrix

### 3.1 Workflow and Operational Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Batch ID | `batch_id` | Journal/header or source record | Text | Yes | Yes | Business batch identifier shown by the progress view |
| Status | `status_display` | Workflow status mapping | Enum/Text | Yes | Yes | User-facing label derived from internal status |
| Comment | `has_comments` | Journal comment count | Boolean/Icon | Yes | Yes | Display indicator, do not load comment text in grid |
| Documents | `has_documents` | Journal document count | Boolean/Icon | Yes | Yes | Display indicator, do not load binary content |
| ID | `journal_display_id` | Journal business ID | Text/Number | Yes | Yes | Distinct from internal database primary key |
| Template | `template_name` | Journal header | Text | Yes | Yes | Default may be Generic |
| Team | `team_assignment` | Assignment/team mapping | Text | Yes | Yes | First Team occurrence |
| Preparer | `preparer_display_name` | Assigned Preparer user | Text | Yes | Yes | User-friendly name |
| Approver | `approver_display_name` | Assigned Approver user | Text | Yes | Yes | May show Unassigned |
| Reviewer | `reviewer_display_name` | Reviewer assignment if present | Text | Yes | Yes | Reviewer workflow remains undefined |
| Final Certification State | `final_certification_state` | Workflow/certification view model | Text | Yes | Yes | Display-only state summary |
| Completed | `is_completed` | Derived from terminal state | Boolean | Yes | Yes | Do not infer solely in UI |
| Posting Status | `posting_status` | Journal header/source | Text | Yes | Yes | Display status from supplied data |
| Document ID | `source_document_id` | Source or journal header | Text | Yes | Yes | Not the supporting document table ID |
| Journal Master | `journal_master` | Source/progress data | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve source semantics |
| Automated Journals | `automated_journal_flag` | Source/progress data | Boolean/Text | Yes | Yes | Display per approved screen |
| Matched Variance | `matched_variance` | Source/derived | Decimal/Text | Yes | Yes | Confirm formatting from source data |

### 3.2 Financial Summary Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Total Debit | `total_debit` | Aggregated journal lines or source total | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| Total Credit | `total_credit` | Aggregated journal lines or source total | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| BL Currency Rate to USD | `currency_rate_to_usd` | Currency-rate lookup | Decimal | Yes | Yes | UI label may be revised to remove external product terminology in final delivery |
| Total Debit - USD | `total_debit_usd` | Authoritative backend calculation | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| Total Credit - USD | `total_credit_usd` | Authoritative backend calculation | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |

### 3.3 Journal Source and Batch Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Team | `team_source` | Source/JDE | Text | Yes | Yes | Second Team occurrence |
| Company Code | `company_code` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve leading zeros |
| Batch Type | `batch_type` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Text, not enum unless catalog supplied |
| Document Type | `document_type` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve source code |
| Document Number | `document_number` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve leading zeros |
| GL Date (Old) | `gl_date_old` | Original source field | Date/Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve source date and normalized date if needed |
| Exchange Rate | `source_exchange_rate` | JDE source | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Distinct from USD conversion rate |
| Currency | `currency_code` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | ISO-like code where valid |

| Fiscal Year | `fiscal_year` | JDE source | Integer | Yes | Yes | Source format retained |
| Batch Number | `batch_number` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve leading zeros |

| Reverse/Void | `reverse_void_flag` | JDE source | Text/Boolean | Yes | Yes | Preserve source semantics |
| BlackLine ID | `external_journal_id` | Source external ID | Text | Yes | Yes | Final UI label must use neutral approved terminology |
| Preparer | `preparer_source_id` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Second Preparer occurrence; may be source ADID/value |
| GL Date | `gl_date` | Normalized/display date | Date | Yes | Yes | Use approved format |
| Batch Date | `batch_date` | Normalized/display date | Date | Yes | Yes | Use approved format |
| Original Variance Amount (Display) | `original_variance_amount_display` | Source/derived | Decimal/Text | Yes | Yes | Keep display formatting rule explicit |
| Explanation 1 | `explanation_1` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Truncate in grid with tooltip if needed |
| Total Credit (Auto-populated) | `total_credit_auto` | Backend/source derived | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Authoritative value |

## 4. Additional Action Column

The UI also requires the role-resolved `Action` column even though it is not listed as a source-data field in the CSV.

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Action | `resolved_action` | Backend action resolver | Enum/Link | Optional | Optional | Edit, Cert, or View |

## 5. Column Groups

Recommended column selector groupings:

```text
Workflow
Assignments
Financial Summary
Source Journal
Batch and Ledger
Invoice
Intercompany
Regional
System/Derived
```

Grouping improves usability without removing any approved columns.

## 6. Data Formatting

- IDs and codes are strings to preserve leading zeros.
- Monetary values use decimal types, not binary floating point.
- Rates use sufficient decimal scale.
- Dates are normalized in API responses and formatted for display.
- Boolean indicators use approved labels/icons and accessible text.
- Empty source values display blank, not misleading zero, unless zero is a valid numeric value.

## 7. Branding Cleanup

Source labels containing an external product name must be replaced with neutral, approved labels in the delivered UI while retaining neutral technical fields such as `external_journal_id` and `currency_rate_to_usd`.

## 8. Acceptance Criteria

- Every supplied CSV column has a unique technical field.
- Duplicate display labels do not create duplicate JSON keys.
- Codes preserve leading zeros.
- Numeric and date fields sort correctly.
- Action is derived, not read from source data.
- The UI can expose the full column inventory through horizontal scrolling and/or a column selector.


# Action-Resolution-Rules

# Action Resolution Rules

## 1. Purpose

The action resolver determines the link shown in the View Journal Progress Action column and the valid workflow buttons shown inside Journal Detail.

The rule is centralized in backend code. It must not be duplicated independently across pages.

## 2. Action Types

```text
EDIT
CERT
VIEW
```

User-facing labels:

```text
EDIT -> Edit
CERT -> Cert
VIEW -> View
```

## 3. Inputs

The resolver evaluates:

- Authenticated user ID.
- Active role.
- User's active role assignments.
- Journal status.
- Journal assignments.
- Assignment status.
- Whether the journal is unassigned.
- Whether the current user owns the actionable assignment.
- Whether the journal has moved to another role bucket.
- Whether the journal is closed.
- Preparer/Approver same-user restriction.

## 4. Edit Rule

Return `EDIT` when:

- Journal is unassigned.
- Current active role is authorized to edit/assign the unassigned journal.
- Journal is not closed or otherwise locked.

`Edit` opens the journal in assignment/edit mode. Edit authorization must be confirmed again by the Journal Detail API.

## 5. Cert Rule

Return `CERT` when:

- Current user owns the actionable assignment.
- Active role is Preparer or Approver.
- Journal is in an actionable status for that role.

Examples:

```text
Assigned Preparer + PENDING_PREPARER -> Cert
Assigned Preparer + REJECTED -> Cert
Assigned Approver + PENDING_APPROVER -> Cert
```

`Cert` is a navigation label. Inside Journal Detail:

- Preparer receives `Certify`.
- Approver receives `Approve` and `Reject`.

## 6. View Rule

Return `VIEW` when:

- Journal belongs to another role bucket.
- Current user does not own the actionable assignment but has permitted read access.
- Journal is closed.
- Current role has historical/read-only visibility.
- An assigned Preparer views a journal currently pending Approver.

Special case:

```text
Preparer + PENDING_APPROVER -> View detail with Decertify available
```

The row link remains `View`, because the primary ownership bucket is Approver, while the detail page may expose the controlled Decertify action to the original Preparer.

## 7. Priority

Recommended resolution order:

```text
1. Validate page visibility
2. If unassigned and editable -> EDIT
3. If current user owns actionable Preparer/Approver assignment -> CERT
4. If read access exists -> VIEW
5. Otherwise -> no action / forbidden
```

## 8. Detail Button Resolution

Separate resolver output:

```text
can_edit
can_certify
can_approve
can_reject
can_decertify
is_read_only
```

Example:

```json
{
  "grid_action": "VIEW",
  "can_certify": false,
  "can_approve": false,
  "can_reject": false,
  "can_decertify": true,
  "is_read_only": false
}
```

## 9. Same-User Restriction

The same user cannot be both Preparer and Approver for the same journal. If invalid data exists, the resolver must not expose Approve. The backend returns a business-rule error and logs the issue.

## 10. Reviewer

Reviewer action resolution is undefined. Do not return CERT or workflow buttons for Reviewer until the business flow is provided.

## 11. System Admin and Local Admin

Admin roles do not receive workflow actions solely because of admin status. Read-only access, if enabled, returns View.

## 12. Backend Interface

```text
IJournalActionResolver
JournalActionResolverImpl
```

Recommended method contract:

```text
resolve(user_context, journal, assignments) -> JournalActionAccessDto
```

## 13. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_ACTION_NOT_ALLOWED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
```

## 14. Audit

Action resolution itself does not create audit events. Successful workflow actions do.

## 15. Acceptance Tests

- Unassigned authorized journal returns Edit.
- Assigned Preparer journal returns Cert.
- Assigned Approver journal returns Cert.
- Journal in another role bucket returns View.
- Preparer viewing pending Approver journal receives View plus Decertify in detail.
- Closed journal returns View.
- Reviewer receives no invented workflow action.
- Unauthorized user receives no action and cannot open the journal.


# Journal-Detail

# Journal Detail

## 1. Purpose

Journal Detail displays all information required to review a journal and perform authorized workflow actions. The page is opened from View Journal Progress, To Do, or a supported notification.

## 2. Route

```text
/journals/<journalId>
```

Optional UI mode query:

```text
mode=edit
action
view
```

The backend remains authoritative and does not trust the requested mode.

## 3. Page Sections

The detail page includes approved sections such as:

- Journal summary/header.
- Journal line grid.
- Documents.
- Comments.
- Hyperlinks where included in the approved page.
- Certification/action panel.
- Audit Trail or Certification Details.

Sections may be tabs, panels, or a two-column layout according to the supplied screenshots.

## 4. Header Information

Display authoritative journal values, including identifiers, status, period, source details, assignments, totals, currency, and posting/header status where applicable.

## 5. Journal Lines

The line grid uses journal-line data from the parsed source file. It is read-only for workflow review unless a confirmed edit requirement exists.

- Preserve source row number.
- Use decimal-safe amount values.
- Show source and USD amount where available.
- Support sorting and filtering if shown.
- Do not change parsed source data during certification.

## 6. Role-Aware Actions

### Preparer

Valid actions by status:

```text
PENDING_PREPARER -> Certify
REJECTED -> Certify
PENDING_APPROVER -> Decertify
CLOSED -> none, View only
```

### Approver

```text
PENDING_APPROVER -> Approve, Reject
Other states -> View only
```

### Reviewer

No workflow actions are defined.

### Admin

No workflow actions solely due to admin role.

## 7. Certification Panel

The panel shows the applicable certification statement from configuration, comments/reason fields when required, and authorized action buttons.

Buttons are disabled while an API call is in progress.

## 8. Data API

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}
```

Response includes:

```text
journal header
lines or line endpoint reference
assignments
resolved access/actions
document summary
comment summary
certification history
audit summary
```

Large line data may use:

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/lines
```

## 9. Read-Only Mode

Read-only mode:

- Disables editable controls.
- Hides unauthorized workflow buttons.
- Allows permitted document download/preview.
- Allows audit viewing.
- Does not rely on disabled HTML controls as the only security layer.

## 10. Success Handling

After a successful workflow action:

- Show centralized success popup/toast.
- Refresh journal status and resolved access.
- Refresh Audit Trail.
- Update or invalidate View Journal Progress cache.
- Navigate only when the approved workflow calls for it.

## 11. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

Display user-friendly messages with correlation ID. No raw exception is rendered.

## 12. Concurrency

Workflow commands must lock or version-check the journal before state transition. If another user has already acted, return a conflict/business error and refresh the page.

Recommended response status:

```text
409 Conflict
```

## 13. Accessibility

- Page heading identifies the journal.
- Tabs/panels are keyboard accessible.
- Action buttons have clear labels.
- Confirmation dialogs manage focus.
- Validation messages are associated with fields.
- Status is conveyed through text and not color alone.

## 14. Audit

View does not create a journal audit event. Certify, Approve, Reject, Decertify, document upload, comment addition, and hyperlink addition create the applicable audit records.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Detail displays authoritative journal data.
- Buttons reflect active role, ownership, and status.
- Preparer can Certify or Decertify only in valid states.
- Approver can Approve or Reject only in valid state.
- Reviewer actions are absent.
- Unauthorized URL access is denied.
- Workflow updates and audit records are atomic.


# Documents-Module

# Documents Module

## 1. Purpose

The Documents module manages supporting evidence attached to a journal and makes authorized documents available for workflow review.

## 2. Capabilities

- Upload supporting document.
- List documents.
- Download document.
- Preview supported document types where available.
- Display metadata.
- Mark inactive/remove only if explicitly approved.

## 3. Authorization

Document access requires:

- Valid bearer token and active session.
- Access to the parent journal.
- Current role permission for the requested operation.

A document ID alone never grants access.

## 4. Upload Flow

```text
Select file
  -> client validates basic constraints
  -> upload API validates journal access
  -> validate name, size, MIME type and extension
  -> calculate hash
  -> persist metadata and content
  -> create audit record
  -> flush and commit
  -> return success message
```

## 5. Storage

Current schema stores file content in PostgreSQL `BYTEA`. The repository/service boundary must allow future replacement with Amazon S3 without changing journal workflow code.

## 6. Metadata

Required fields:

```text
document_id
journal_id
original_file_name
file_extension
mime_type
file_size_bytes
file_hash
description
uploaded_by
uploaded_role
uploaded_at
status
```

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents/{documentId}/download
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents/{documentId}/preview
```

## 8. Validation

- Empty file rejected.
- File size must be within configured limit.
- Unsupported extension/MIME combination rejected.
- File name sanitized for display and response headers.
- Hash may be used for duplicate detection.
- Journal must exist and be accessible.
- Upload must be allowed in the current state/role.

## 9. UI

Document panel shows:

- File name.
- Description.
- Size.
- Uploaded by.
- Uploaded role.
- Uploaded time.
- Download icon.
- Preview icon only when supported.

View Journal Progress receives only document count/indicator.

## 10. Audit Events

```text
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED, optional application/security log
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_PREVIEWED, optional application/security log
```

Upload audit is part of the business transaction.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
DOCUMENT_UPLOAD_FAILED
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_TYPE_NOT_SUPPORTED
DOCUMENT_SIZE_EXCEEDED
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
DOCUMENT_PREVIEW_NOT_AVAILABLE
```

## 12. Security

- Do not expose storage paths.
- Use safe content-disposition headers.
- Prevent path traversal.
- Validate MIME type.
- Consider malware scanning before production.
- Log download/preview failures with correlation ID.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Authorized user can upload permitted document.
- Document list refreshes after commit.
- Approver can review permitted evidence.
- Unauthorized document access returns 403/404 appropriately.
- Upload creates audit event.
- Raw storage details are never exposed.


# Comments-Module

# Comments Module

## 1. Purpose

The Comments module records journal-specific collaboration and workflow context. Comments are visible to authorized users across workflow stages.

## 2. Capabilities

- Add comment.
- List comments chronologically or newest first according to approved UI.
- Display author, active role, and timestamp.
- Retain prior comments after status transitions.

Editing or deleting posted comments is not included unless explicitly approved.

## 3. Authorization

- User must have access to the journal.
- Active role is recorded with the comment.
- Read-only journal access may allow comment reading but not necessarily comment creation.
- Reviewer comment behavior remains undefined unless the approved screen expressly shows it.

## 4. Data Model

```text
comment_id
journal_id
comment_text
role_code
created_by
created_at
updated_at
```

## 5. Add Flow

```text
Enter comment
  -> validate non-empty and length
  -> validate journal access
  -> save comment
  -> create audit event
  -> flush and commit
  -> show success message
```

## 6. API

```http
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/comments
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/comments
```

Request:

```json
{
  "comment_text": "<comment>"
}
```

The backend derives author and active role from request context.

## 7. Rejection Comments

Approver rejection uses the workflow action contract and records rejection reason/comments in the certification/action record. It may also create a visible journal comment if the business design requires that behavior. Do not create duplicate comments unless explicitly intended.

## 8. Decertification Comments

Decertification requires the configured reason/comment policy. The workflow action stores this in certification/audit metadata. A separate comment row is optional only when approved.

## 9. UI

- Multiline comment input.
- Character limit/help text.
- Add button.
- Busy state during save.
- Comment history with author, role, and timestamp.
- Safe text rendering.
- No raw HTML execution.

## 10. Audit Event

```text
JOURNAL_COMMENT_ADDED
```

The audit event includes comment ID and metadata but should not duplicate sensitive text unnecessarily.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
COMMENT_REQUIRED
COMMENT_TOO_LONG
COMMENT_ADD_FAILED
COMMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 12. Security

- Escape/render as text.
- Apply length limit.
- Prevent cross-site scripting.
- Do not trust client-supplied author or role.
- Preserve immutable authorship.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Authorized user can add a valid comment.
- Comment persists with user and active role.
- Comment is visible to authorized subsequent roles.
- Audit event is created.
- Empty/oversized comments show proper field errors.
- Rejection workflow does not create accidental duplicate messages.


# Audit-Trail

# Audit Trail

## 1. Purpose

The Audit Trail provides immutable traceability for journal workflow and supporting actions. Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify must always be recorded.

## 2. Mandatory Workflow Events

```text
PREPARER_CERTIFIED
APPROVER_APPROVED
APPROVER_REJECTED
PREPARER_DECERTIFIED
```

Additional journal events:

```text
JOURNAL_FILE_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_PARSE_STARTED
JOURNAL_PARSE_SUCCEEDED
JOURNAL_PARSE_FAILED
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_COMMENT_ADDED
JOURNAL_HYPERLINK_ADDED
```

## 3. Required Audit Fields

```text
audit_id
journal_id
file_load_id
action_type
old_status
new_status
performed_by
performed_role
message
metadata_json
created_at
```

## 4. Audit Questions

Every workflow event must answer:

- Who performed the action?
- Which active role was used?
- Which journal was affected?
- What action occurred?
- What was the previous status?
- What is the new status?
- When did it occur?
- What reason/comment metadata was supplied?

## 5. Atomicity Rule

```text
No workflow action is complete unless the audit record is successfully written.
```

Required transaction sequence:

```text
Lock/load journal
  -> validate ownership and transition
  -> update journal and assignment
  -> create certification/action record
  -> create audit record
  -> flush all changes
  -> commit
```

If audit insert or flush fails, rollback the full action.

## 6. Event Details

### Certify

```text
Old: PENDING_PREPARER or REJECTED
New: PENDING_APPROVER
Role: PREPARER_RECONCILER
```

### Approve

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: CLOSED
Role: APPROVER
```

### Reject

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: REJECTED
Role: APPROVER
Metadata: reason code, reason text, comments
```

### Decertify

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: PENDING_PREPARER
Role: PREPARER_RECONCILER
Metadata: reason/comments and assignment changes
```

## 7. Audit UI

Journal Detail includes an Audit Trail or Certification Details section.

Columns:

```text
Action
Status From
Status To
Performed By
Performed Role
Performed At
Reason
Comments
```

Default order is newest first. Use pagination when needed.

## 8. API

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/audit
```

Access requires authorization to the journal.

## 9. Immutability

- No normal update/delete API.
- Corrections are represented by new events.
- Database permissions should prevent application users from modifying history.
- Retention follows corporate requirements when supplied.

## 10. Metadata

Use structured JSON for supplementary values. Do not hide essential searchable fields only inside JSON.

Example:

```json
{
  "assignment_id": "ASN001",
  "certification_id": "CRT001",
  "reason_code": "MISSING_SUPPORT",
  "screen_id": "journal-detail",
  "action_source": "UI"
}
```

## 11. Error Handling

If audit logging fails:

```text
Action could not be completed because audit logging failed. Please retry or contact support.
```

API code:

```text
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
```

## 12. Security

- Audit access is journal-authorized.
- Sensitive metadata is minimized.
- Correlation IDs link application logs to API failures but need not replace audit IDs.
- Audit records do not contain bearer tokens or password data.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Every mandatory action produces one audit event.
- Event contains before/after status.
- Event identifies user and active role.
- Audit failure rolls back the workflow action.
- UI shows the event after success.
- Audit history cannot be edited through the application.


# Journal-Status-Matrix

# Journal Status Matrix

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the Phase 1 journal status model and allowed transitions. Status rules are implemented in code using enums.

## 2. Status Enum

```text
UPLOADED
PARSING
PARSE_FAILED
PENDING_PREPARER
PENDING_APPROVER
REJECTED
CLOSED
```

File-load status and journal status should remain separate enums. `UPLOADED`, `PARSING`, and `PARSE_FAILED` primarily belong to the file-load lifecycle, while journal business records begin after successful parse.

## 3. Journal Business Statuses

### PENDING_PREPARER

Journal is assigned to Preparer and awaits certification.

### PENDING_APPROVER

Preparer certified the journal; Approver owns the active decision. Original Preparer may View and Decertify.

### REJECTED

Approver rejected the journal. It is returned to the Preparer for correction/review and re-certification.

### CLOSED

Approver approved the journal. Workflow is complete for Phase 1 and the journal is read-only.

## 4. Transition Matrix

| From | Action | Role | To |
|---|---|---|---|
| PENDING_PREPARER | Certify | Assigned Preparer | PENDING_APPROVER |
| REJECTED | Certify | Assigned Preparer | PENDING_APPROVER |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Approve | Assigned Approver | CLOSED |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Reject | Assigned Approver | REJECTED |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Decertify | Original Assigned Preparer | PENDING_PREPARER |

## 5. Grid Action Matrix

| Status / Ownership | Grid Action |
|---|---|
| Unassigned and editable | Edit |
| Assigned actionable Preparer | Cert |
| Assigned actionable Approver | Cert |
| Moved to another role bucket | View |
| Closed | View |

## 6. Detail Action Matrix

| Status | Preparer | Approver | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|
| PENDING_PREPARER | Certify | View if authorized | Undefined |
| REJECTED | Certify | View | Undefined |
| PENDING_APPROVER | View, Decertify | Approve, Reject | Undefined |
| CLOSED | View | View | Undefined |

## 7. Assignment Effects

### Certify

- Complete/deactivate Preparer actionable assignment as appropriate.
- Activate/create Approver assignment.

### Reject

- Return/activate Preparer assignment.
- Complete/deactivate Approver actionable assignment.

### Approve

- Complete Approver assignment.
- Close journal.

### Decertify

- Reactivate Preparer assignment.
- Remove/deactivate pending Approver action.

Exact assignment status enum values must remain consistent across code and schema.

## 8. Invalid Transitions

Examples:

- Approve from PENDING_PREPARER.
- Reject from CLOSED.
- Decertify from REJECTED.
- Certify from CLOSED.
- Approver decision by an unassigned user.

Return:

```text
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
```

## 9. Concurrency

Status transitions require row locking or optimistic version validation. A stale client receives 409 Conflict and refreshes the current status.

## 10. Reviewer

No Reviewer transition is included. A future Reviewer flow requires a separate architecture update.

## 11. Acceptance Criteria

- Only listed transitions succeed.
- Assignment changes match the transition.
- Grid and detail actions match status and ownership.
- Invalid transitions return structured errors.
- Audit records capture old and new status.


# Certification-Workflow

# Certification Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Certification workflow allows the assigned Preparer to certify a journal and move it to the Approver bucket.

## 2. Eligible States

```text
PENDING_PREPARER
REJECTED
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- Current user owns the Preparer assignment.
- User is not the resolved Approver for the same journal.
- Session and token are valid.

## 4. Entry

View Journal Progress displays `Cert`. Selecting it opens Journal Detail. The detail page displays `Certify` when all rules pass.

## 5. Preconditions

- Journal exists.
- Correct current status.
- Preparer assignment is actionable.
- Required supporting documents exist if header validation requires them.
- Required comments/explanations are complete.
- Debit/credit and other configured validation rules pass.
- Approver assignment/resolution is valid.
- Preparer and Approver are different users.

## 6. Transaction

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> revalidate status and assignment
  -> validate certification prerequisites
  -> update status to PENDING_APPROVER
  -> update Preparer assignment
  -> activate/create Approver assignment
  -> insert journal_certification record
  -> insert PREPARER_CERTIFIED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

Any failure rolls back all changes.

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/certify
```

Request may contain:

```json
{
  "comments": "<optional-or-required-comment>",
  "certification_statement_id": "<statement-id>"
}
```

The backend derives user and role.

## 8. Success Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Certified successfully.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_APPROVER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 9. Post-Success UI

- Show success popup/toast.
- Refresh journal detail.
- Show View mode.
- Show Decertify to the original Preparer.
- Refresh Audit Trail.
- Invalidate progress and To Do data.
- Approver receives actionable work/notification after commit.

## 10. Audit

```text
Action: PREPARER_CERTIFIED
Old: PENDING_PREPARER or REJECTED
New: PENDING_APPROVER
```

Audit failure rolls back certification.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
REQUIRED_DOCUMENT_MISSING
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 12. Concurrency

A second Certify submission must not create duplicate certifications. Disable the button and enforce status/locking in the backend.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Only assigned Preparer can certify.
- PENDING_PREPARER and REJECTED can certify.
- Status becomes PENDING_APPROVER.
- Approver work becomes active.
- Audit event exists.
- Failure leaves journal unchanged.


# Approval-Workflow

# Approval Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Approval workflow enables the assigned Approver to approve or reject a journal currently in the Approver bucket.

## 2. Eligible State

```text
PENDING_APPROVER
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `APPROVER`.
- Current user owns the active Approver assignment.
- Current user is not the journal Preparer.
- Session and token are valid.

## 4. Entry

View Journal Progress displays `Cert` for an actionable Approver journal. Selecting it opens Journal Detail, which displays:

- Approve.
- Reject.

There is no separate confirmed Approver Decertify action.

## 5. Approve Flow

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate PENDING_APPROVER and assignment
  -> validate segregation of duties
  -> update status to CLOSED
  -> complete Approver assignment
  -> insert approval certification/action record
  -> insert APPROVER_APPROVED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

### Success

```text
Approved successfully.
```

The journal becomes read-only.

## 6. Reject Flow

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate PENDING_APPROVER and assignment
  -> validate rejection reason/comments
  -> update status to REJECTED
  -> complete/deactivate Approver assignment
  -> reactivate Preparer assignment
  -> insert rejection certification/action record
  -> insert APPROVER_REJECTED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

### Success

```text
Rejected successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

## 7. APIs

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/approve
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/reject
```

Reject request:

```json
{
  "reason_code": "MISSING_SUPPORT",
  "comments": "Please attach the required support."
}
```

## 8. Audit

Approve:

```text
APPROVER_APPROVED
PENDING_APPROVER -> CLOSED
```

Reject:

```text
APPROVER_REJECTED
PENDING_APPROVER -> REJECTED
```

Audit failure rolls back the decision.

## 9. Notifications and To Do

After commit:

- Approve removes active work from Approver and closes the journal.
- Reject removes active Approver work and creates/reactivates Preparer work.
- Relevant users receive notifications according to the notification module.

## 10. Error Codes

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
REJECTION_REASON_REQUIRED
REJECTION_COMMENT_REQUIRED
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 11. Concurrency

Approve and Reject are mutually exclusive. The first committed action changes the status. A stale second action receives 409 Conflict.

## 12. UI Confirmation

Approve and Reject should use confirmation dialogs where shown/appropriate. Reject dialog contains reason and comments fields. The UI never displays raw backend errors.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Only assigned Approver can act.
- Approve closes journal.
- Reject returns journal to Preparer.
- Reason/comments are validated.
- Audit record is mandatory.
- Same person cannot prepare and approve.
- Failure rolls back all updates.


# Decertification-Workflow

# Decertification Workflow

## 1. Purpose

Decertification allows the original assigned Preparer to pull a certified journal back from the Approver bucket before the Approver has approved or rejected it.

## 2. Eligible State

```text
PENDING_APPROVER
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- Current user is the journal's assigned/original Preparer.
- Approver has not completed a decision.
- Journal is not CLOSED or REJECTED.

## 4. Entry

In View Journal Progress, the journal is in another role bucket, so the row action is `View` for the Preparer. Journal Detail shows `Decertify` when the rule is satisfied.

## 5. Preconditions

- Journal exists.
- Status remains PENDING_APPROVER.
- Preparer ownership is valid.
- Approver assignment is still pending/actionable.
- Required reason/comment policy is satisfied.

## 6. Transaction

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate Preparer and PENDING_APPROVER
  -> update status to PENDING_PREPARER
  -> reactivate Preparer assignment
  -> deactivate/cancel pending Approver assignment
  -> insert decertification action record
  -> insert PREPARER_DECERTIFIED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

Any failure rolls back the status and assignment changes.

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/decertify
```

Request:

```json
{
  "reason_code": "<configured-code>",
  "comments": "<reason for pulling back>"
}
```

## 8. Success Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_DECERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Decertified successfully and returned to Preparer queue.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_PREPARER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 9. Post-Success UI

- Show success popup.
- Refresh journal detail.
- Show Certify again when prerequisites are met.
- Remove journal from active Approver work.
- Add/reactivate Preparer To Do item.
- Refresh Audit Trail.

## 10. Audit

```text
Action: PREPARER_DECERTIFIED
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: PENDING_PREPARER
```

Metadata includes reason/comments and assignment-status changes.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
DECERTIFICATION_REASON_REQUIRED
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 12. Race Condition

If the Approver approves or rejects before the Decertify transaction obtains the lock, Decertify fails with 409 Conflict and the UI refreshes the latest state.

## 13. Restrictions

- Approver cannot decertify in Phase 1.
- Reviewer decertification is undefined.
- Decertification after CLOSED is prohibited.
- Decertification does not delete previous certification history.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Original Preparer can decertify only while pending Approver.
- Grid link remains View before opening detail.
- Status returns to PENDING_PREPARER.
- Approver work is removed/deactivated.
- Audit event is mandatory.
- Previous certification remains historically visible.
- Failure leaves all state unchanged.


# Currency-Architecture

# Currency Architecture

## 1. Purpose

The Currency module provides System Admin access to currency configuration and period-based conversion-rate information. The architecture is based on the supplied currency screenshots, the currency-conversion workbook used as seed data, and the existing PostgreSQL currency tables.

## 2. Confirmed Scope

Phase 1 contains:

- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Cross-currency display.
- Historical-rate display.
- Show Prior 12 Periods.
- SQL/workbook-based seed loading.

The module must not add unsupported pages or speculative rate-maintenance workflows.

## 3. Role and Authorization

The confirmed role is `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.

Required controls:

- Valid bearer token.
- Active server-side session.
- Active role `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.
- Explicit permission for currency screens.

Other roles do not gain access because the user can view journal rates elsewhere. Direct access by an unauthorized role returns 403.

## 4. Routes

```text
/admin/currency/settings
/admin/currency/rates
```

Unsupported currency routes return the standard 404 page.

## 5. Currency Area Layout

The supplied Currency Setup screenshot shows a left-panel pattern for currency configuration. The delivered UI should preserve the approved left-panel/content relationship while using neutral component and CSS names.

Recommended structure:

```text
Currency Area
  -> Currency Left Navigation
  -> Currency Settings
  -> Currency Rates Information
```

Only supported entries are displayed.

## 6. Frontend Modules

```text
modules/currency/
  pages/
    CurrencySettingsPage.tsx
    CurrencyRatesInformationPage.tsx
  components/
    CurrencySidePanel.tsx
    CurrencyRateGrid.tsx
    CrossCurrencyToggle.tsx
    PriorPeriodsToggle.tsx
    CurrencyFilters.tsx
  hooks/
    useCurrencySettings.ts
    useCurrencyRates.ts
  services/
    currencyService.ts
  types/
    currency.types.ts
  styles/
```

## 7. Backend Modules

```text
api/v1/controllers/admin_currency_controller.py
application/services/currency/
  get_currency_settings_service.py
  get_currency_rates_service.py
  import_currency_rates_service.py
  impl/
domain/services/currency_rate_service.py
infrastructure/database/repositories/currency_conversion_repository_impl.py
```

Controllers handle HTTP only. Application-service implementations orchestrate authorization, validation, repository access, and audit behavior.

## 8. Database Model

The existing Phase 1 model includes:

```text
currency_conversion_rate
currency_rate_load
```

Core rate fields:

```text
rate_id
from_currency
to_currency
conversion_rate
effective_from
effective_to
source_system
source_file_name
load_batch_id
is_active
created_at
updated_at
```

The model supports direct and cross-currency pairs through `from_currency` and `to_currency`.

## 9. Period Model

Currency screens use the global `MM/YYYY` period. The default is the previous month. The selected period is the anchor for current-rate and historical-rate retrieval.

## 10. Rate Precision

- Store rates using fixed decimal precision.
- Current schema uses `NUMERIC(18,8)`.
- Do not use binary floating-point values for authoritative rate calculations.
- Display precision follows the workbook/screenshot and must not silently truncate significant digits.

## 11. Rate Selection

For a requested pair and period, select the active rate whose effective date range covers the period anchor date.

```text
from_currency = requested source
and to_currency = requested target
and effective_from <= period end/anchor
and (effective_to is null or effective_to >= period start/anchor)
and is_active = true
```

If multiple rates overlap, treat the configuration as invalid and return a controlled error rather than choosing arbitrarily.

## 12. API Overview

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=MM/YYYY
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/import
```

The import endpoint is included only for controlled administration or deployment use. If no upload control is approved in the screen, seed/import remains an operational process.

## 13. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_RATE_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_RATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_FAILED
```

## 14. Audit and Logging

Viewing rates is application logged when needed but does not create journal audit events. Import or configuration changes create currency administration audit records. Journal conversions retain the rate used on the journal so later rate changes do not rewrite historical financial values.

## 15. Non-Functional Requirements

- Rate retrieval must be deterministic.
- Large historical grids remain responsive.
- All rate values are decimal safe.
- APIs include correlation IDs.
- No spreadsheet formula is treated as authoritative unless imported as a resolved value and validated.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- System Admin can open both confirmed currency screens.
- Global period defaults to previous month.
- Direct rates display correctly.
- Cross-currency mode displays the approved pair view.
- Prior 12 periods displays the approved history structure.
- Unauthorized roles receive 403.
- Missing or overlapping rates produce structured errors.


# Currency-Settings

# Currency Settings

## 1. Purpose

Currency Settings defines the supported currency configuration displayed through the confirmed currency setup screen and left-panel navigation.

## 2. Scope Control

The screenshot confirms the Currency Settings area, but not every reference-system setting is automatically in scope. Implement only fields and controls visible in the approved screen or required for the Phase 1 rate model.

## 3. Access

- Active role: `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.
- Other roles: denied.
- Direct navigation without authorization: 403.

## 4. Route

```text
/admin/currency/settings
```

## 5. Page Layout

```text
Global application shell
  -> Currency left panel
  -> Currency Settings content
  -> Save/Cancel controls only when edit behavior is approved
```

The left panel must match the supplied visual hierarchy and highlight the active currency page.

## 6. Configuration Concepts

Supported configuration may include:

- Currency code.
- Display name.
- Active/inactive indicator.
- Default target/reporting currency.
- Rate type or frequency when present in approved data.
- Decimal/display precision when supplied.

Do not invent settlement, hedging, consolidation, or accounting-policy settings.

## 7. Currency Code Rules

- Required.
- Uppercase.
- Normally three characters when using standard currency codes.
- Unique in the active currency catalog.
- Preserve approved non-standard codes if they exist in source data.
- Cannot be changed in a way that breaks historical rate references.

## 8. Active Status

Deactivation prevents new configuration use but does not delete historical rates or journal conversion history.

```text
Deactivate != Delete
```

## 9. API

If Phase 1 is view-only:

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
```

If approved editing is implemented:

```http
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
PUT  /api/v1/admin/currency/settings/{currencyCode}
```

The architecture must not expose write endpoints before the screen's edit controls and permissions are approved.

## 10. Validation

- Currency code required.
- Duplicate code rejected.
- Default target currency must exist and be active.
- Precision values must be within configured bounds.
- Deactivation must not remove historical records.

## 11. UI Behavior

- Dense enterprise form or grid consistent with screenshot.
- Labels above or beside controls as shown.
- Save disabled when no changes exist.
- Cancel restores server values.
- Loading and error states use shared UI components.
- Success and error messages use centralized enums/catalog.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_SETTING_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_CODE_REQUIRED
CURRENCY_CODE_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_TARGET_INVALID
CURRENCY_SETTING_UPDATE_FAILED
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 13. Audit

When writes are enabled, record:

```text
CURRENCY_SETTING_CREATED
CURRENCY_SETTING_UPDATED
CURRENCY_SETTING_DEACTIVATED
```

Audit metadata includes before/after values, actor, role, and timestamp. Viewing settings does not create a business audit event.

## 14. Database Direction

If a dedicated currency-master table is introduced, keep it separate from rate history.

Recommended fields:

```text
currency_code
currency_name
is_active
display_precision
created_at
updated_at
```

The current Phase 1 schema can derive available currencies from rate data until a dedicated master is required.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Active currency navigation is clear.
- Only approved fields appear.
- No unsupported settings are invented.
- Historical rates survive deactivation.
- Unauthorized roles cannot view or modify settings.
- Errors include user-friendly messages and correlation IDs.


# Currency-Rates-Information

# Currency Rates Information

## 1. Purpose

Currency Rates Information displays period-based currency conversion rates using the layout and controls shown in the supplied screenshots.

## 2. Route

```text
/admin/currency/rates
```

## 3. Access

System Admin only for the confirmed administration screen.

## 4. Screen Structure

The page contains:

- Currency-area left panel.
- Page title/information area.
- Period context.
- Rate filters.
- `Show Cross Currency` selection.
- `Show Prior 12 Periods` selection.
- Rate information grid.

Labels and layout follow the screenshots rather than a generic form design.

## 5. Current-Period View

Default behavior:

- Global period is previous month.
- Cross-currency option is off unless the approved screenshot shows another default.
- Prior-12-period option is off.
- Display current/selected period rates.

## 6. Grid Columns

The exact labels follow the screenshot and workbook. The logical fields include:

```text
rate_type
from_currency
to_currency
selected_period
conversion_rate
source
status
```

Historical mode adds period columns or period rows according to the approved screenshot.

## 7. Filters

Confirmed logical filters may include:

- Rate type.
- Currency/from currency.
- Convert to/to currency.
- Period.
- Custom frequency only if visible and supported.

Do not add unsupported filter controls.

## 8. API

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_cross_currency=false&show_prior_periods=false
```

Optional pair filters:

```text
fromCurrency=USD
toCurrency=INR
rateType=<type>
```

## 9. Response Model

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "period": "07/2026",
    "show_cross_currency": false,
    "show_prior_periods": false,
    "periods": ["07/2026"],
    "rates": [
      {
        "from_currency": "EUR",
        "to_currency": "USD",
        "rate": "1.09000000"
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

Rate values are serialized as decimal-safe strings or JSON decimal-compatible values.

## 10. Missing Rates

A missing rate is displayed as blank or an approved missing-data indicator, not zero. Zero is a valid number only if the business source explicitly supplies it.

The API may return warnings:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
```

## 11. Sorting and Pagination

- Sort by rate type, source currency, and target currency by default.
- Use pagination when rate volume requires it.
- Prior-period columns remain aligned during horizontal scrolling.
- Currency codes remain visible through pinned columns if the approved grid supports pinning.

## 12. Precision and Formatting

- Preserve up to eight decimal places from the database.
- Do not apply currency monetary symbols to conversion rates.
- Do not use percentage formatting.
- Trim only insignificant trailing zeros when consistent with the screenshot.

## 13. Loading and Error States

- Changing period or options shows grid loading.
- Failed retrieval shows centralized error state.
- Last valid selection remains visible when practical.

Error codes:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 14. Accessibility

- Toggle controls have explicit labels.
- Grid headers identify period and pair.
- Missing rate indicators include accessible text.
- Horizontal grid remains keyboard accessible.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Page matches the confirmed left-panel and rate-grid structure.
- Selected period is displayed.
- Direct rates load by default.
- Cross-currency and prior-period options alter the view independently.
- Missing values are not shown as zero.
- Rate precision is preserved.


# Cross-Currency-Rules

# Cross-Currency Rules

## 1. Purpose

Cross-currency mode displays conversion relationships between currencies beyond a simple source-to-reporting-currency list. The supplied cross-currency screenshot is the UI source of truth.

## 2. Terms

```text
Direct rate: A -> B stored explicitly.
Inverse rate: B -> A calculated as 1 / direct rate.
Cross rate: A -> C calculated through an approved pivot currency B.
```

## 3. Phase 1 Pivot

The existing seed data primarily contains currency-to-USD rates. Therefore USD is the natural Phase 1 pivot unless the workbook/configuration explicitly defines another reporting currency.

Do not hardcode USD inside UI components. Treat the pivot/reporting currency as configuration.

## 4. Calculation

Assume rates are stored as:

```text
1 unit of FROM currency = rate units of TO currency
```

If both currencies have rates to the same pivot:

```text
A -> Pivot = rate_A
C -> Pivot = rate_C
A -> C = rate_A / rate_C
```

Example:

```text
EUR -> USD = 1.09
INR -> USD = 0.012
EUR -> INR = 1.09 / 0.012
```

All calculations use decimal arithmetic.

## 5. Direct-Rate Priority

Resolution order:

```text
1. Explicit direct rate for pair and period
2. Approved inverse of explicit reverse pair
3. Cross rate through configured pivot
4. Missing-rate result
```

This order avoids replacing an approved direct rate with a calculated value.

## 6. Same-Currency Rule

```text
A -> A = 1
```

Same-currency rates may be stored or derived. Display exactly `1` using approved precision.

## 7. Inverse Rule

```text
inverse_rate = 1 / direct_rate
```

A direct rate of zero cannot be inverted and is a configuration error.

## 8. Period Consistency

All component rates used in one cross-rate calculation must belong to the same selected period/effective date context. Never combine rates from different periods.

## 9. Rounding

- Calculate with database/application decimal precision.
- Round only for final display or persisted derived rate.
- Do not round component rates before division.
- Display precision follows configured rate precision, up to the schema scale.

## 10. Missing Component Rate

If either pivot component is missing:

- Do not calculate the cross rate.
- Display missing/blank indicator.
- Include a warning code.
- Do not substitute a previous period unless an explicit fallback rule is approved.

## 11. API

Cross mode request:

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_cross_currency=true
```

The response identifies whether each rate is:

```text
DIRECT
INVERSE
CROSS
IDENTITY
MISSING
```

## 12. Auditability

For journal calculations, store the actual rate used and its source/method so historical journal totals remain explainable.

Recommended metadata:

```text
rate_method
pivot_currency
component_rate_ids
calculated_at
```

The information screen may calculate cross rates on retrieval without persisting every matrix cell.

## 13. Error Codes

```text
CROSS_CURRENCY_COMPONENT_MISSING
CROSS_CURRENCY_ZERO_RATE
CROSS_CURRENCY_INVALID_PAIR
CROSS_CURRENCY_CALCULATION_FAILED
```

## 14. Security

Only authorized System Admin users access the administration cross-currency screen. Journal-processing services may call the rate service internally without exposing admin endpoints.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Explicit direct rate has priority.
- Same-currency result equals one.
- Inverse uses decimal division.
- Cross rate uses one configured pivot and same-period components.
- Missing components do not produce fabricated values.
- Response identifies calculation method.


# Historical-Rates-And-Prior-12-Periods

# Historical Rates and Prior 12 Periods

## 1. Purpose

This document defines historical currency-rate retrieval and the `Show Prior 12 Periods` behavior shown in the supplied screenshot.

## 2. Anchor Period

The globally selected period is the anchor period.

Example:

```text
Selected period: 07/2026
```

## 3. Period Sequence

The UI displays the selected period and prior periods in descending order.

Example sequence:

```text
07/2026
06/2026
05/2026
04/2026
03/2026
02/2026
01/2026
12/2025
11/2025
10/2025
09/2025
08/2025
07/2025
```

The supplied screenshot determines whether the selected period plus 12 prior periods, or 12 total periods including the selected period, is displayed. The implementation must match the screenshot exactly.

## 4. Year Rollover

Period generation must correctly cross calendar years.

```text
01/2027 -> 12/2026 -> 11/2026 ...
```

## 5. Data Retrieval

For each pair and each displayed period:

- Resolve the effective rate for that period.
- Preserve the rate source and method.
- Return missing rather than carrying forward unless a fallback rule is approved.

## 6. API

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_prior_periods=true
```

Response includes an ordered periods array and rate values keyed by period.

Example:

```json
{
  "periods": ["07/2026", "06/2026", "05/2026"],
  "rates": [
    {
      "from_currency": "EUR",
      "to_currency": "USD",
      "period_values": {
        "07/2026": "1.09000000",
        "06/2026": "1.08000000",
        "05/2026": null
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

## 7. Grid Presentation

The screenshot indicates an expanded period view. Recommended behavior:

- Currency-pair columns remain pinned.
- Period columns scroll horizontally.
- Newest/anchor period appears first.
- Header labels use `MM/YYYY`.
- Missing values remain blank or show an approved unavailable indicator.
- Tooltips can display rate source and effective dates.

## 8. Cross-Currency Combination

`Show Cross Currency` and `Show Prior 12 Periods` may be selected together if the approved UI permits it.

In combined mode:

- Calculate/resolve every pair independently for every period.
- Do not reuse the anchor-period cross rate for older periods.
- Return rate method per period.

## 9. Performance

- Query the full effective-date range in one repository call where practical.
- Index currency pair and effective dates.
- Build the period matrix in the application service.
- Avoid one database query per cell.
- Cache read-only period results where safe and invalidate after rate import.

## 10. Historical Integrity

Changing a current rate must not overwrite historical effective periods. Import should insert new effective-dated records or close previous ranges according to approved rules.

## 11. Missing and Duplicate Rates

- Missing rate: null plus warning metadata.
- Overlapping active rates: configuration error.
- Duplicate pair/effective start: rejected by unique constraint or validation.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
HISTORICAL_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
```

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Period sequence matches screenshot.
- January rollover works.
- Pair columns remain readable during horizontal scroll.
- Historical values use their own period rates.
- Missing values are not carried forward without approval.
- Combined cross/historical mode is period correct.


# Currency-Import-Workflow

# Currency Import Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Currency Import workflow loads validated conversion-rate seed or operational data into PostgreSQL. The supplied currency workbook is the Phase 1 source for seed data.

## 2. Deployment Versus UI Import

Two modes are distinguished:

### Seed/Deployment Import

- Workbook is converted into reviewed SQL seed data.
- SQL file is version controlled.
- Loaded using `psql` during database setup.
- This is the required Phase 1 baseline.

### Administrative UI Import

- Implement only if a visible upload control and business requirement are approved.
- Uses the same validation and transaction service.

Do not expose a speculative upload screen solely because the database has a load table.

## 3. Recommended Files

```text
backend/db/seed/003_seed_currency_rates.sql
backend/db/source/currency_conversion_source.xls
backend/db/README.md
```

The source workbook may be retained outside the deployable repository if licensing, confidentiality, or size policies require it. The SQL seed remains the executable artifact.

## 4. Import Data Contract

Each rate row resolves to:

```text
rate_id
rate_type, if supplied
from_currency
to_currency
conversion_rate
effective_from
effective_to
source_system
source_file_name
load_batch_id
is_active
```

## 5. Validation Rules

### File-Level

- Expected workbook/sheet exists.
- Required columns exist.
- File is readable.
- No unsupported formula/error cells in required fields.

### Row-Level

- Source currency required.
- Target currency required.
- Currency codes normalized to uppercase.
- Conversion rate required and greater than zero unless a documented exception exists.
- Effective-from date required.
- Effective-to is optional but cannot precede effective-from.
- Duplicate pair/effective-from rejected.
- Overlapping active ranges rejected.
- Same-currency rate must equal one.

## 6. Transaction Behavior

A controlled import is atomic for the batch:

```text
Begin transaction
  -> create currency_rate_load record
  -> validate all rows
  -> insert/update rate records according to approved mode
  -> update valid/invalid counts
  -> mark load successful
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

If any blocking error occurs:

```text
Rollback rate changes
Persist failure/load-error summary separately
Show/import-log error result
```

Do not partially load business rate data when atomic mode is selected.

## 7. Import Modes

Recommended modes:

```text
INSERT_ONLY
REPLACE_BATCH
```

`UPSERT` should not be used casually because it can overwrite historical data. Any update mode must preserve prior values and effective history.

## 8. SQL Seed Execution

The deployment guide uses direct `psql` execution. Example final path:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification -f .\db\seed\003_seed_currency_rates.sql
```

Paths must match the repository exactly.

## 9. Administrative API

Only when approved:

```http
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/import
GET  /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/imports/{loadBatchId}
```

## 10. Load Tracking

The `currency_rate_load` record stores:

```text
load_batch_id
source_type
source_file_name
file_content, optional
total_rows
valid_rows
invalid_rows
status
error_message
loaded_by
loaded_at
processed_at
```

For AWS deployment, source files may move to S3 while metadata remains in PostgreSQL.

## 11. Audit

Administration audit events:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_STARTED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_SUCCEEDED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_FAILED
```

Audit metadata includes batch ID, source file, row counts, and actor. Do not store sensitive workbook content in logs.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_IMPORT_FILE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_COLUMN_MISSING
CURRENCY_IMPORT_RATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_DATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_IMPORT_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_IMPORT_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 13. Post-Import Behavior

After successful import:

- Invalidate currency-rate caches.
- Refresh Currency Rates Information.
- Preserve historical journal rates already stored on journals.
- Show success message with batch counts.

## 14. Security

- Import requires System Admin or deployment-level database authorization.
- Uploaded workbook type and size are validated.
- No macro execution.
- No formula execution by backend import code.
- Store secrets outside files and source control.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Seed SQL can build currency rates from scratch.
- Invalid rows block an atomic import.
- No partial rate data remains after failure.
- Duplicate and overlapping rates are rejected.
- Same-currency rates equal one.
- Import produces a load record and administration audit event.
- Currency screens show new rates only after commit.


# Dependency-Validation

# Dependency Validation

## Purpose
Dependency Validation is the authoritative routing configuration used during journal parsing and assignment resolution.

## Business Objective
- Resolve Preparer ownership
- Resolve Approver ownership
- Support journal routing
- Prevent invalid assignments
- Enforce segregation of duties

## Source System
The first key value originates from the JDE Preparer identifier in the uploaded file.

## Core Data
- Preparer Identifier
- Preparer User
- Approver User
- Effective Dates
- Active Flag

## Processing
Upload -> Validate -> Store Mapping -> Journal Parse Uses Mapping

## Validation Rules
- Preparer required
- Approver required
- Active mapping required
- Same-user Preparer/Approver prohibited
- Duplicate active mappings prohibited

## Security
System Admin access only.

## Acceptance Criteria
Every parsed journal resolves to a valid Preparer and Approver mapping.


# Dependency-Mapping-Upload

# Dependency Mapping Upload

## Purpose
Provides controlled loading of dependency mapping data.

## Supported Inputs
- Excel
- CSV

## Upload Lifecycle
Select File -> Validate Structure -> Validate Rows -> Save Batch -> Activate Mapping

## Mandatory Columns
- Preparer ID
- Preparer Name
- Approver ID
- Approver Name
- Status

## Row Validations
- Required values present
- Approver exists
- Preparer exists
- No duplicate active mapping
- Preparer != Approver

## Errors
- Missing Column
- Invalid User
- Duplicate Mapping
- Invalid Status

## Audit
- Upload Started
- Upload Completed
- Upload Failed


# Journal-Assignment-Engine

# Journal Assignment Engine

## Purpose
The Assignment Engine determines ownership during parsing and workflow transitions.

## Inputs
- Parsed JDE Journal
- Dependency Validation Data
- User Master
- Role Assignments

## Primary Flow
Read Journal -> Resolve Preparer -> Resolve Approver -> Create Assignments -> Persist Journal

## Assignment Rules
### Preparer Assignment
Resolved from dependency mapping.

### Approver Assignment
Resolved from dependency mapping.

### Same User Check
A user cannot be both Preparer and Approver.

## Workflow Ownership
PENDING_PREPARER -> Preparer Assignment Active
PENDING_APPROVER -> Approver Assignment Active
REJECTED -> Preparer Assignment Active
CLOSED -> No Active Assignment

## Failure Handling
Any resolution failure causes parse rollback.

## Audit
Assignment creation is logged.


# Approver-Resolution-Logic

# Approver Resolution Logic

## Purpose
Defines how an Approver is identified for a journal.

## Resolution Sequence
1. Read JDE Preparer Identifier
2. Lookup Dependency Mapping
3. Locate Active Mapping
4. Resolve Approver User
5. Validate User Role
6. Create Assignment

## Validation Rules
- Mapping must exist
- Approver must be active
- Approver role must be assigned
- Approver cannot equal Preparer

## Errors
- APPROVER_NOT_FOUND
- DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
- INVALID_APPROVER_ROLE
- PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER

## Parse Impact
Unable to resolve Approver = Parse Failure = Transaction Rollback

## Acceptance Criteria
Every journal has one valid Approver before commit.


# JDE-Integration-Model

# JDE Integration Model

## Purpose
Defines inbound integration between JDE journal extracts and the Journal Certification Platform.

## Source
JDE generated journal file.

## Flow
JDE Export -> Upload -> Parse -> Validate -> Assignment Resolution -> Persist -> Workflow

## Import Stages
### Upload
Stores file metadata and content.

### Parse
Validates entire file.

### Persistence
Creates journal headers, journal lines, assignments and audit records.

## Atomic Processing Rule
If any row fails a critical validation:
- Rollback transaction
- No journal created
- No assignments created

## Dependency Validation Usage
JDE Preparer Identifier is used to resolve Preparer and Approver ownership.

## Future Enhancements
- S3 ingestion
- API integration
- Scheduled imports

## Acceptance Criteria
Uploaded JDE data can be parsed and routed to the correct users through dependency mappings.


# Security-Architecture

# Security Architecture

## Purpose
Defines authentication, authorization, session control, RBAC, auditability, and API protection.

## Principles
- Zero trust authorization
- JWT-based authentication
- Server-side session validation
- RBAC authorization
- Journal ownership enforcement
- Least privilege access

## Layers
Frontend Route Guards -> API Authorization -> Business Authorization -> Data Access Authorization

## Controls
- HTTPS only
- JWT bearer tokens
- Refresh token support
- Password hashing
- Correlation IDs
- Audit logging

## Security Events
Login, Logout, Role Switch, Certify, Approve, Reject, Decertify, Upload, Parse.


# Authentication-Flow

# Authentication Flow

## Login
User -> Login API -> Credential Validation -> Session Creation -> JWT Issued.

## Flow
1. Submit credentials
2. Validate user
3. Validate active account
4. Create session
5. Issue access token
6. Return user roles

## Logout
Invalidate session and refresh token.

## Failure Codes
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_ACCOUNT_DISABLED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED


# JWT-And-Session-Management

# JWT and Session Management

## Access Token
Contains:
- user_id
- username
- active_role
- session_id
- issued_at
- expiry

## Session Table
Tracks active sessions.

## Validation
Every API validates:
- JWT signature
- Expiration
- Session existence
- Session status

## Expiration
Expired token -> 401 Unauthorized.

## Security
Never store passwords in JWT.


# Role-Switching

# Role Switching

## Purpose
Users may own multiple roles but only one active role.

## Flow
Select Role -> API Call -> Validate Ownership -> Update Session -> Issue New JWT.

## Rules
- Role must exist in user_roles.
- Frontend cannot grant roles.
- Backend is authoritative.

## Result
Navigation, Dashboard and Permissions refresh.
