# Journal-Ownership-Security

# Journal Ownership Security

## Principle
Authentication does not equal ownership.

## Ownership Checks
User must:
- Hold role
- Own assignment
- Be in valid status

## Preparer Rules
Can act only on assigned journals.

## Approver Rules
Can approve/reject only assigned journals.

## Segregation of Duties
Preparer cannot approve same journal.

## Errors
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION

## Audit
All workflow changes are audited.


# Error-Handling-Standards

# Error Handling Standards

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the mandatory error-handling standard for the frontend, FastAPI backend, database operations, workflow transactions, file parsing, authentication, and administration modules.

The application must never display raw exceptions, stack traces, SQL errors, framework messages, or unhandled promise rejections to users. Every failure is translated into a stable application error code and a user-friendly message.

## 2. Core Principles

```text
Technical details belong in protected server logs.
User-facing responses contain safe and actionable messages.
Business failures are not treated as unexpected system failures.
Every failed API response follows one response contract.
Every error response contains a correlation ID.
Workflow and parse failures must not leave partial business data.
```

## 3. Error Categories

### 3.1 Validation Errors

Used when request fields, uploaded rows, periods, identifiers, comments, files, or business inputs are invalid.

Examples:

```text
VALIDATION_ERROR
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
FILE_INVALID_COLUMN_COUNT
REJECTION_REASON_REQUIRED
COMMENT_REQUIRED
```

Recommended HTTP status: `400 Bad Request` or `422 Unprocessable Entity`, applied consistently.

### 3.2 Authentication Errors

Used when credentials, access tokens, refresh tokens, or sessions are invalid.

```text
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED
AUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN_INVALID
```

Recommended HTTP status: `401 Unauthorized`.

### 3.3 Authorization Errors

Used when the user is authenticated but lacks the required active role, permission, assignment, or ownership.

```text
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

Recommended HTTP status: `403 Forbidden`.

### 3.4 Not Found Errors

Used when a supported resource does not exist.

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_SETTING_NOT_FOUND
DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
```

Recommended HTTP status: `404 Not Found`.

Unknown or unsupported frontend routes use the standard Page Not Found screen and must not be confused with resource-level API failures.

### 3.5 Business Rule Errors

Used when the request is structurally valid but violates workflow or financial rules.

```text
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
```

Recommended HTTP status: `409 Conflict` for state conflicts, or `422 Unprocessable Entity` for other business constraints.

### 3.6 Concurrency Errors

Used when the record changed after the client loaded it or another user completed the action first.

```text
JOURNAL_CONCURRENT_UPDATE
RESOURCE_VERSION_CONFLICT
```

Recommended HTTP status: `409 Conflict`.

The UI must refresh the latest record and explain that the data changed.

### 3.7 Infrastructure and Transaction Errors

Used for database, storage, network, or unexpected server failures.

```text
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
FILE_STORAGE_FAILED
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR
```

Recommended HTTP status: `500 Internal Server Error` or `503 Service Unavailable` when a dependency is temporarily unavailable.

## 4. Backend Exception Hierarchy

Recommended structure:

```text
ApplicationException
  -> ValidationException
  -> AuthenticationException
  -> AuthorizationException
  -> NotFoundException
  -> BusinessRuleException
  -> ConflictException
  -> ParseException
  -> TransactionException
  -> InfrastructureException
```

Every application exception carries:

```text
error_code
safe_message
severity
http_status
details
```

Technical exceptions are logged and translated by global exception handlers.

## 5. Global FastAPI Exception Handling

Recommended file:

```text
backend/app/api/exception_handlers.py
```

Handlers must cover:

- Application exceptions.
- Pydantic/request validation errors.
- Authentication and authorization failures.
- SQLAlchemy/database exceptions.
- File and storage exceptions.
- Unhandled exceptions.

Unknown exceptions are converted to `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`. The response must not expose the exception class, SQL statement, file path, or stack trace.

## 6. Transaction Error Handling

Workflow commands and parsing use explicit transaction boundaries.

```text
Begin transaction
  -> validate
  -> modify business records
  -> create required audit event
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

On any failure:

```text
rollback
log technical failure with correlation ID
return structured API error
show proper UI popup/dialog
```

Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify must roll back when audit logging fails.

## 7. Parse Error Handling

Journal parsing validates the complete file before committing business data.

A critical error causes:

- Parse batch status `PARSE_FAILED`.
- No journal headers committed.
- No journal lines committed.
- No assignments committed.
- Row-level validation details returned or stored in the parse-error area.
- Error popup explaining that no journal data was created.

Example summary:

```text
Parse failed. No journal data was created.
```

## 8. Frontend Error Handling

All API calls use a shared API client and shared error mapper.

The frontend must not use:

```text
window.alert
raw response dumps
component-specific copies of common error messages
```

The frontend must:

- Map the standardized API error response.
- Show the correct notification severity.
- Display field-level errors beside the associated fields.
- Display row-level parse errors in the parse-error panel.
- Display correlation ID for support-worthy failures.
- Redirect to Login for expired sessions.
- Display Access Denied for 403.
- Preserve valid user input when retry is possible.

## 9. Popup and Message Behavior

### Success

Use an auto-dismiss toast for routine completion.

### Information

Use a toast, banner, or inline callout.

### Warning

Use a persistent notification when user attention is required.

### Error

Use a sticky toast or dialog. Critical transaction, parse, and authorization errors should remain visible until dismissed.

## 10. Retry Rules

Automatic retry may be used for idempotent GET requests and transient network failures. Do not automatically retry workflow commands such as Approve, Reject, Certify, Decertify, Parse, or Upload without an idempotency design.

## 11. Error Details

`details` may include safe structured information:

```text
field
row_number
error_code
message
rejected_value, only when safe
```

Do not include credentials, tokens, password hashes, SQL, local paths, or confidential payloads.

## 12. Logging Rule

Every server failure is logged with:

- Correlation ID.
- Timestamp.
- Endpoint.
- User ID where available.
- Active role where available.
- Safe entity identifiers.
- Error code.
- Technical exception and stack trace in protected logs.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- All failed APIs use the standard contract.
- Raw exceptions never reach the UI.
- Every error has an enum code.
- Every error response has a correlation ID.
- Workflow failures roll back.
- Parse failures create no journal business data.
- 401, 403, 404, 409, 422, and 500 behaviors are consistent.
- Shared notification components render user-friendly messages.


# API-Response-Contracts

# API Response Contracts

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the common JSON envelopes returned by all REST APIs. Consistent envelopes allow the frontend API client, notification framework, error mapper, and logging tools to behave uniformly.

## 2. General Rules

- JSON property naming uses `snake_case` unless the frontend contract later standardizes on camelCase through a serialization layer.
- Success and failure envelopes are mutually exclusive.
- Every response includes `correlation_id`.
- Timestamps use ISO 8601 UTC.
- Financial decimals are serialized without binary floating-point loss.
- Error codes, message codes, and severity values are enums.

## 3. Success Envelope

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Certified successfully.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_APPROVER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000001"
}
```

`message` may be omitted for read-only GET operations when no user notification is required.

## 4. Error Envelope

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION",
    "message": "This journal action is not allowed in the current status.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [],
    "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000002"
  }
}
```

The top-level and nested correlation-ID convention must be chosen once. Recommended implementation keeps `correlation_id` at the top level for both success and error responses. If retained inside `error` for compatibility, do not duplicate mismatched values.

## 5. Validation Error Details

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "VALIDATION_ERROR",
    "message": "Please correct the highlighted fields and try again.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [
      {
        "field": "period",
        "error_code": "INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT",
        "message": "Select a valid period in MM/YYYY format."
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000003"
}
```

## 6. Parse Error Response

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "FILE_PARSE_FAILED",
    "message": "Parse failed. No journal data was created.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [
      {
        "row_number": 12,
        "field": "preparer_adid",
        "error_code": "PREPARER_ADID_NOT_FOUND",
        "message": "The Preparer identifier was not found in the user master."
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000004"
}
```

For large error lists, return a summary and a paged parse-error endpoint rather than an oversized response.

## 7. Paged Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "items": [],
    "pagination": {
      "page": 1,
      "page_size": 25,
      "total_items": 143,
      "total_pages": 6,
      "has_previous": false,
      "has_next": true
    }
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000005"
}
```

Phase 1 local grid pagination may not require this contract for period-loaded datasets, but server-side administrative and error lists can use it.

## 8. HTTP Status Mapping

```text
200 OK                  Successful read or command response
201 Created             Resource created
202 Accepted            Asynchronous processing accepted, if used
204 No Content          Successful command with no response body, used sparingly
400 Bad Request         Invalid request syntax or field validation
401 Unauthorized        Authentication/token/session failure
403 Forbidden           Role, permission, or ownership failure
404 Not Found           Supported resource does not exist
409 Conflict            Status/concurrency/business-state conflict
413 Payload Too Large   Upload exceeds configured limit
415 Unsupported Media Type
422 Unprocessable Entity Business/input validation, if standardized
500 Internal Server Error
503 Service Unavailable Temporary dependency outage
```

## 9. Authentication Responses

Login success:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "access_token": "<token>",
    "token_type": "Bearer",
    "expires_in": 3600,
    "user": {},
    "roles": ["PREPARER_RECONCILER", "APPROVER"],
    "active_role": "PREPARER_RECONCILER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000006"
}
```

Refresh tokens should be returned/stored according to the secure cookie strategy described in the security architecture. Do not expose raw server-side token hashes.

## 10. File Upload Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "FILE_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "File uploaded successfully. Click Parse to process the file.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "file_load_id": "LOAD001",
    "status": "UPLOADED"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000007"
}
```

## 11. Request Correlation Header

The server returns:

```http
X-Correlation-ID: CORR-20260820-000007
```

The response-body value and header value must match.

## 12. Schema Organization

Recommended backend schemas:

```text
ApiSuccessResponse[T]
ApiErrorResponse
ApiMessage
ApiError
ApiErrorDetail
PaginationMetadata
```

## 13. Frontend Behavior

The shared API client:

- Reads HTTP status.
- Parses the standard envelope.
- Maps error code and severity.
- Captures correlation ID.
- Triggers centralized session handling for 401.
- Throws/returns a typed application error, not an arbitrary transport exception.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Success and error shapes are consistent across controllers.
- Correlation header and body match.
- Validation details are structured.
- Decimals and dates are serialized safely.
- HTTP status matches the error category.
- Frontend does not need endpoint-specific parsing for common failures.


# Message-Catalog-And-Enums

# Message Catalog and Enums

## 1. Purpose

The message catalog centralizes all user-facing success, error, warning, and information messages. Enums provide stable codes, while display text can be maintained without changing business logic in multiple locations.

## 2. Design Principles

```text
Code decides which message code applies.
Catalog resolves the user-facing text.
UI renders the server-provided safe message and severity.
Repeated messages are not hardcoded in pages or services.
```

## 3. Severity Enum

```text
SUCCESS
ERROR
WARNING
INFO
```

## 4. Error Code Enum

Recommended groups:

### Authentication and Authorization

```text
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED
```

### Journal

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_CONCURRENT_UPDATE
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
```

### File and Parse

```text
FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED
FILE_PARSE_FAILED
FILE_INVALID_FORMAT
FILE_INVALID_COLUMN_COUNT
PREPARER_ADID_NOT_FOUND
APPROVER_ADID_NOT_FOUND
DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
```

### Documents and Comments

```text
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_TYPE_NOT_SUPPORTED
DOCUMENT_SIZE_EXCEEDED
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
COMMENT_REQUIRED
COMMENT_TOO_LONG
```

### Currency

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_IMPORT_FILE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

### Infrastructure

```text
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR
SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE
```

## 5. Success Code Enum

```text
LOGIN_SUCCESSFUL
ROLE_SWITCHED_SUCCESSFULLY
FILE_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY
FILE_PARSED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_APPROVED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_REJECTED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_DECERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY
DOCUMENT_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY
COMMENT_ADDED_SUCCESSFULLY
CURRENCY_IMPORT_SUCCESSFUL
```

## 6. Information Code Enum

```text
NO_RECORDS_FOUND
NO_TODO_ITEMS
NO_NOTIFICATIONS
PARSING_IN_PROGRESS
SESSION_EXPIRING_SOON
PREVIEW_NOT_AVAILABLE
FILTER_MINIMUM_CHARACTERS_REQUIRED
```

## 7. Warning Code Enum

```text
UNSAVED_CHANGES
MISSING_OPTIONAL_RATE
JOURNAL_STATUS_CHANGED
SESSION_EXPIRING_WARNING
```

## 8. Backend Catalog

Recommended file:

```text
backend/app/core/message_catalog.py
```

Example conceptual mapping:

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND -> Journal was not found.
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION -> This journal action is not allowed in the current status.
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED -> Action could not be completed because audit logging failed. Please retry or contact support.
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR -> Something went wrong. Please try again or contact support.
```

## 9. Message Parameters

Messages may use safe named parameters:

```text
File {file_name} uploaded successfully.
Showing {start}-{end} of {total}.
```

Do not format messages using untrusted HTML. Parameters are escaped and rendered as text.

## 10. Localization Readiness

Even if Phase 1 uses English only:

- Codes remain language neutral.
- Avoid concatenating sentence fragments in components.
- Keep display text in catalogs/resources.
- Dates and numbers use formatting utilities.

## 11. Frontend Message Mapping

The frontend may maintain a limited fallback catalog for network errors and cases where no API response is available. The server message is authoritative for business failures.

Recommended files:

```text
notification.types.ts
notificationMapper.ts
fallbackMessageCatalog.ts
```

## 12. Naming Standards

- Codes use uppercase `SNAKE_CASE`.
- Codes describe the condition, not the current UI component.
- Do not include external product names.
- Do not reuse one code for unrelated conditions.
- Codes are append-only where external clients may depend on them.

## 13. Governance

Adding a message requires:

1. Add/update enum.
2. Add catalog text.
3. Map HTTP status and severity.
4. Add tests.
5. Update API documentation where externally visible.

## 14. Testing

Tests validate:

- Every enum has a catalog entry.
- No duplicate codes.
- Required placeholders are supplied.
- No message exposes secrets or technical details.
- Error codes map to expected HTTP status.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Common messages are not duplicated across pages.
- Every API failure uses an enum code.
- Every success command can return a success code.
- Catalog text is safe and actionable.
- External product names are absent.
- Fallback messages exist for network and unknown failures.


# Notification-Framework

# Notification Framework

## 1. Purpose

The notification framework provides one modular way to display immediate success, error, warning, and information feedback throughout the application.

This framework is distinct from persistent header notifications. Immediate notifications describe the result of the action currently performed by the user.

## 2. Supported Presentation Types

### Toast

Used for routine success and information messages.

### Sticky Toast

Used for recoverable errors or warnings requiring attention.

### Modal Dialog

Used for critical transaction failures, parse failures, authorization issues requiring explanation, or detailed row-level errors.

### Inline Banner

Used for page-level information, warnings, or partial-data states.

### Field-Level Message

Used for request validation near the invalid control.

## 3. Severity Rules

```text
SUCCESS -> success toast, normally auto-dismiss
INFO    -> toast or inline banner
WARNING -> persistent toast or banner
ERROR   -> sticky toast or modal based on impact
```

## 4. Shared Frontend Structure

```text
frontend/modules/shared/notifications/
  components/
    AppNotificationProvider.tsx
    NotificationToast.tsx
    NotificationDialog.tsx
    NotificationBanner.tsx
    ValidationSummary.tsx
  hooks/
    useNotification.ts
  services/
    notificationMapper.ts
  types/
    notification.types.ts
  styles/
```

## 5. Notification Object

```text
code
message
severity
details
correlation_id
auto_dismiss
duration_ms
action_label
action_callback
```

## 6. API Integration

The shared API client maps standardized success/error envelopes to typed notification objects.

```text
API response
  -> parse response contract
  -> map code/severity/details
  -> return typed result
  -> page/service triggers appropriate presentation
```

Not every successful GET should show a toast. Command responses can request success feedback; background reads remain quiet unless they fail.

## 7. Workflow Messages

### Certify

```text
Certified successfully.
```

### Approve

```text
Approved successfully.
```

### Reject

```text
Rejected successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

### Decertify

```text
Decertified successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

### Action Failure

```text
Action failed. No changes were saved.
```

## 8. Parse Messages

### Upload

```text
File uploaded successfully. Click Parse to process the file.
```

### Parsing

```text
Parsing is in progress.
```

### Success

```text
File parsed successfully.
```

### Failure

```text
Parse failed. No journal data was created.
```

Parse failure uses a dialog or page panel with row-level details and correlation ID.

## 9. Session Messages

### Expired

```text
Your session has expired. Please log in again.
```

The notification is shown before redirecting to Login where technically practical.

### Forbidden

```text
You are not authorized to perform this action.
```

## 10. Deduplication

The provider prevents duplicate notifications caused by repeated renders or multiple interceptors. A notification may be deduplicated using:

```text
code + correlation_id + entity_id
```

## 11. Queue Behavior

- Limit visible toasts.
- Queue additional messages.
- Critical errors take priority.
- A modal is never hidden behind a toast.
- Role switch and navigation clear obsolete transient notifications where appropriate.

## 12. Accessibility

- Use suitable ARIA live regions.
- Errors remain visible long enough to read.
- Dialog focus is trapped and restored.
- Close buttons have labels.
- Severity is conveyed by text/icon, not color only.
- Correlation ID can be copied.

## 13. Styling

Notifications follow the enterprise theme and use neutral component names. Do not copy proprietary class names. Use consistent colors, typography, spacing, and iconography.

## 14. Persistent Header Notifications

Persistent workflow notifications are stored and retrieved through the separate Notifications module. The immediate framework may also show a toast when a persistent notification is generated.

## 15. Testing

- Success toast appears once.
- Error modal shows details and correlation ID.
- 401 triggers session handling.
- 403 triggers access-denied message.
- Field validation maps to controls.
- Notifications are keyboard accessible.
- No `window.alert` is used.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Every module uses the shared provider.
- Messages come from standard API contracts/catalogs.
- Raw exceptions never appear.
- Critical errors remain visible.
- Duplicate messages are controlled.
- Persistent and transient notifications are clearly separated.


# Correlation-Id-And-Logging

# Correlation ID and Logging

## 1. Purpose

Correlation IDs connect frontend errors, API requests, backend logs, database failures, and support investigations without exposing internal technical details to users.

## 2. Correlation ID Lifecycle

1. Accept a valid incoming `X-Correlation-ID` from a trusted gateway/client, or generate a new ID.
2. Store it in request context.
3. Include it in every log entry for that request.
4. Return it in the response header.
5. Return it in the standard response body.
6. Display it in support-worthy error notifications.

## 3. Format

Use a globally unique, non-sensitive value such as a UUID or approved platform format.

Example:

```text
CORR-20260820-8f17a84d
```

Do not encode user IDs, email addresses, tokens, or business-sensitive values in the correlation ID.

## 4. FastAPI Middleware

Recommended middleware responsibilities:

- Read/generate correlation ID.
- Attach ID to request state/context variable.
- Set response header.
- Record request start/end.
- Record duration and status code.
- Clear context after request completion.

## 5. Frontend Handling

The API client reads correlation ID from the body or `X-Correlation-ID` header and attaches it to the typed error.

Error dialog example:

```text
Something went wrong. Please try again or contact support.
Correlation ID: CORR-20260820-8f17a84d
```

Provide a copy action when practical.

## 6. Structured Logging

Use structured JSON logs in AWS environments. Recommended fields:

```text
timestamp
log_level
message
correlation_id
request_method
request_path
http_status
duration_ms
user_id
active_role
session_id, hashed or safe reference
entity_type
entity_id
error_code
exception_type
service_name
environment
```

## 7. Sensitive Data Rules

Never log:

- Passwords.
- Password hashes.
- Access tokens.
- Refresh tokens.
- Authorization headers.
- Full session secrets.
- File binary content.
- Sensitive document content.
- Entire request bodies by default.
- Database connection strings with passwords.

Mask or omit sensitive fields.

## 8. Log Levels

```text
DEBUG -> local diagnostics, disabled/restricted in production
INFO  -> successful lifecycle events and operational milestones
WARNING -> recoverable abnormal conditions
ERROR -> failed operation requiring investigation
CRITICAL -> broad outage or integrity threat
```

Business validation failures usually log at INFO or WARNING, not ERROR, unless they indicate invalid system configuration.

## 9. Workflow Logging

Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify produce:

- Immutable journal audit event for business history.
- Application log entry for technical operations.

Application logs do not replace audit records.

## 10. Parse Logging

Log:

- File load ID.
- File name where safe.
- Row counts.
- Parse start/end.
- Validation summary.
- Transaction commit/rollback.
- Error code and correlation ID.

Do not log every raw source row in production.

## 11. AWS CloudWatch Direction

For AWS deployment:

- Send backend structured logs to CloudWatch Logs.
- Configure retention according to corporate policy.
- Create metrics/alarms for 5xx rates, authentication failures, parse failures, and latency.
- Use correlation ID in CloudWatch search and support runbooks.

## 12. Frontend Logging

Frontend logging is limited and sanitized. Browser console output must not contain tokens or sensitive journal data. Production client errors may be sent to an approved monitoring platform with correlation ID and safe metadata.

## 13. Audit Versus Application Log

```text
Audit Trail -> Who changed business state, what changed, before/after status.
Application Log -> How the request executed and why a technical failure occurred.
```

Audit data is immutable and business-facing. Application logs are operational and access controlled.

## 14. Error Handler Integration

Global exception handlers:

- Log technical exception with stack trace server-side.
- Use the current correlation ID.
- Map to safe error code/message.
- Return the standard response contract.

## 15. Operational Search Examples

Support can search by:

```text
correlation_id
journal_id
file_load_id
error_code
user_id
request_path
```

Access to logs must be restricted and monitored.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Every API response contains one correlation ID.
- Header and body IDs match.
- Every request log contains the ID.
- Error popups show the ID for significant failures.
- Sensitive values are masked or omitted.
- Workflow audit and technical logs remain separate.
- AWS logging is structured and searchable.

# Deployment-And-Launch-Guide

# Deployment and Launch Guide

## 1. Purpose

This guide defines how to launch the application from scratch, prepare the PostgreSQL database, start the FastAPI backend, start the Next.js frontend, verify the application locally, and promote the solution to AWS.

The database lifecycle is SQL-script based. Alembic, Flyway, Liquibase, and Docker are not used in the current phase.

## 2. Repository Prerequisites

Install:

```text
Git
Python 3.11 or later
Node.js LTS
npm
PostgreSQL 18
psql command-line client
PowerShell
```

## 3. Expected Repository Layout

```text
repository/
├── backend/
│   ├── app/
│   ├── db/
│   │   ├── schema/
│   │   ├── seed/
│   │   ├── rollback/
│   │   ├── scripts/
│   │   └── README.md
│   ├── tests/
│   ├── requirements.txt
│   └── .env.example
├── frontend/
│   ├── app/
│   ├── modules/
│   ├── public/
│   ├── package.json
│   └── .env.example
└── docs/
```

## 4. Clone and Prepare

```powershell
git clone <repository-url>
cd <repository-folder>
```

Copy environment templates and provide local values. Never commit real credentials or JWT secrets.

## 5. Database Creation

Create the database using PostgreSQL administration tooling or a controlled SQL script.

Example:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\createdb.exe" `
  -U postgres `
  -h localhost `
  journal_certification
```

If the database already exists, do not recreate it unless following the documented reset procedure.

## 6. Run Schema and Seed Scripts

The currently supplied execution approach is:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" `
  -U postgres `
  -h localhost `
  -d journal_certification `
  -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 `
  -f .\seed\001_schema.sql

& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" `
  -U postgres `
  -h localhost `
  -d journal_certification `
  -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 `
  -f .\seed\002_seed_phase1.sql
```

If final repository paths are `backend/db/schema` and `backend/db/seed`, update the commands to match those checked-in locations. `ON_ERROR_STOP=1` is mandatory so PowerShell does not continue after a SQL failure.

## 7. Start Backend

```powershell
cd backend
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
python -m pip install --upgrade pip
pip install -r requirements.txt
Copy-Item .env.example .env
uvicorn app.main:app --reload --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000
```

Expected checks:

```text
GET http://localhost:8000/health/live
GET http://localhost:8000/health/ready
GET http://localhost:8000/docs, local environments only
```

## 8. Start Frontend

Open another PowerShell window:

```powershell
cd frontend
npm ci
Copy-Item .env.example .env.local
npm run dev
```

Expected URL:

```text
http://localhost:3000
```

## 9. Startup Order

```text
1. PostgreSQL
2. Database schema and seed verification
3. FastAPI backend
4. Next.js frontend
5. Login and smoke test
```

## 10. Smoke Test

Verify:

1. Login succeeds.
2. Baxter logo appears without an application title.
3. Clicking the logo opens the landing dashboard.
4. Default period is the previous month.
5. Role switching refreshes dashboard and navigation.
6. View Journal Progress loads.
7. Local Admin can upload and parse a valid file.
8. Preparer can Certify.
9. Approver can Approve or Reject.
10. Preparer can Decertify while pending Approver.
11. System Admin can open currency and dependent-validation screens.
12. Unsupported route displays 404.
13. Footer links are correct.

## 11. Production Build Verification

Backend:

```powershell
python -m pytest
```

Frontend:

```powershell
npm ci
npm run lint
npm run test --if-present
npm run build
```

Database:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification -c "SELECT 1;"
```

## 12. Rollback

Application rollback means redeploying the previously approved backend and frontend releases. Database rollback uses reviewed rollback SQL only when safe. Never automatically reverse a financial workflow or delete audit history.

## 13. Deployment Acceptance

Deployment is complete only when health checks pass, database scripts completed without error, smoke tests pass, CloudWatch logging is visible, no secrets are in source control, HTTPS is active, and a support rollback path is documented.


# Local-Development-Setup

# Local Development Setup

## 1. Purpose

This document standardizes developer workstation setup for Windows and the current PostgreSQL 18 environment.

## 2. Required Software

```text
Git
PowerShell 7 recommended
Python 3.11+
Node.js LTS
npm
PostgreSQL 18
Visual Studio Code or approved IDE
```

## 3. Verify Installations

```powershell
git --version
python --version
node --version
npm --version
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" --version
```

## 4. Backend Setup

```powershell
cd backend
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
pip install -r requirements.txt
Copy-Item .env.example .env
```

Configure `.env` with local-only values. Generate a development JWT secret rather than using a shared production secret.

## 5. Frontend Setup

```powershell
cd frontend
npm ci
Copy-Item .env.example .env.local
```

Configure:

```text
NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000/api/v1
```

## 6. Database Setup

Create `journal_certification`, run the schema script, then run seed scripts. Use `-v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`.

## 7. Run Services

Backend:

```powershell
uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000
```

Frontend:

```powershell
npm run dev
```

## 8. Recommended Helper Scripts

```text
backend/scripts/run_api.ps1
frontend/scripts/run_ui.ps1
backend/db/scripts/setup_local.ps1
backend/db/scripts/reset_local.ps1
```

Scripts must stop on errors and return a non-zero exit code.

## 9. Local URLs

```text
Frontend: http://localhost:3000
Backend:  http://localhost:8000
API:      http://localhost:8000/api/v1
Health:   http://localhost:8000/health/ready
```

## 10. Development Data

Use only approved seed data. Do not place real production journal files, credentials, or confidential supporting documents in the repository.

## 11. Testing

Backend:

```powershell
pytest
```

Frontend:

```powershell
npm run lint
npm run test --if-present
```

## 12. Troubleshooting

### PowerShell blocks venv activation

Use the approved local execution policy or activate through the Python interpreter. Do not weaken machine-wide security without authorization.

### Database authentication fails

Check host, port, database, user, password, PostgreSQL service status, and `pg_hba.conf` configuration.

### CORS error

Ensure backend allowed origins include `http://localhost:3000` exactly.

### Port already in use

Stop the conflicting process or use an approved alternate port and update environment values consistently.

## 13. Developer Security

- Never commit `.env` files.
- Never log tokens.
- Never use production credentials locally.
- Keep local upload files outside source control.
- Use least-privileged database users where practical.


# Database-Build-Process

# Database Build Process

## 1. Purpose

The database is built and maintained through ordered, version-controlled SQL files executed directly by `psql`. No migration framework is used.

## 2. Folder Structure

```text
backend/db/
├── schema/
│   ├── 001_schema.sql
│   ├── 002_views.sql
│   ├── 003_functions.sql
│   └── 004_indexes.sql
├── seed/
│   ├── 001_seed_phase1.sql
│   ├── 002_seed_currency_rates.sql
│   └── 003_seed_dependency_mapping.sql
├── rollback/
├── scripts/
└── README.md
```

If the current files remain under one `seed` folder, retain their existing names and document that path. Do not create conflicting sequences.

## 3. Execution Standard

```powershell
$Psql = "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe"

& $Psql -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification `
  -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 `
  -f .\seed\001_schema.sql

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Schema build failed." }

& $Psql -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification `
  -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 `
  -f .\seed\002_seed_phase1.sql

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Seed load failed." }
```

## 4. Script Rules

- UTF-8 encoding.
- Ordered numeric prefixes.
- `ON_ERROR_STOP` required.
- No environment passwords in SQL.
- Schema and seed changes are separated.
- Destructive operations require explicit review.
- Audit/history data is never silently deleted.
- Use transactions where supported and appropriate.

## 5. Idempotency

Baseline creation scripts may assume an empty database. Seed scripts should use stable keys and controlled conflict handling so approved reruns do not create duplicates.

Do not use `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` to hide invalid or changed seed data. Conflict behavior must be explicit.

## 6. Build Validation

After execution, verify:

```sql
SELECT current_database();
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM roles;
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users;
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM user_roles;
```

Also verify required tables, constraints, indexes, views, and seed rows using a controlled validation script.

## 7. Production Database User

Use separate identities:

```text
Deployment owner: executes approved DDL
Application user: DML on required tables only
Read-only support user: controlled query access
```

The application must not connect as `postgres` in production.

## 8. Release Packaging

Each release includes:

- New SQL files.
- Execution order.
- Prerequisites.
- Backup requirement.
- Expected duration.
- Validation queries.
- Rollback/forward-fix approach.

## 9. AWS RDS Execution

Run approved SQL against the RDS endpoint from a secured deployment runner or controlled administrative host with network access. Do not make RDS publicly accessible solely to run scripts.

## 10. Backup and Restore

Before destructive production changes, create or verify an approved RDS snapshot/backup. Restore testing must be documented separately from deployment success.

## 11. Failure Handling

If a script fails:

1. Stop execution.
2. Capture script name, statement context, and correlation/change reference.
3. Determine whether the transaction rolled back.
4. Do not continue to seed dependent objects.
5. Apply an approved corrected script or forward fix.

## 12. Acceptance Criteria

- Fresh database builds successfully.
- Scripts stop on first error.
- Seed rows are deterministic.
- Application user has least privilege.
- Production execution is private and auditable.
- Validation queries pass.


# AWS-Deployment-Architecture

# AWS Deployment Architecture

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the target AWS topology for the Next.js frontend, FastAPI backend, PostgreSQL database, secrets, logs, and deployment operations.

## 2. Recommended Logical Topology

```text
Users
  -> HTTPS DNS endpoint
  -> CloudFront / approved frontend hosting
  -> Next.js frontend
  -> Application Load Balancer or approved API endpoint
  -> FastAPI compute
  -> Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL

Supporting services:
  Secrets Manager or SSM Parameter Store
  CloudWatch Logs and Alarms
  S3 for future document/file storage
```

## 3. Network

Recommended VPC layout:

```text
Public subnets:
- Internet-facing load balancer, if required

Private application subnets:
- Backend compute

Private database subnets:
- RDS PostgreSQL
```

RDS should not be publicly accessible. Security groups allow database traffic only from approved backend/deployment sources.

## 4. Frontend Hosting Options

Choose one approved pattern:

### AWS Amplify Hosting

Suitable for managed Next.js build and hosting when supported by enterprise standards.

### S3 and CloudFront

Suitable only when the frontend can be deployed as a static export. Server-side Next.js features require compute-based hosting.

### Compute-Based Next.js

Use approved ECS, App Runner, Elastic Beanstalk, or EC2 architecture where server-side rendering is required.

The final choice depends on whether the implemented Next.js pages require server-side runtime features.

## 5. Backend Hosting Options

Preferred managed patterns:

- ECS Fargate for containerized production runtime, if containers are approved for AWS deployment.
- Elastic Beanstalk for managed application deployment.
- EC2 with systemd and reverse proxy for a simpler VM-based pattern.

Local development can remain non-Docker while AWS production uses an approved deployment artifact.

## 6. Database

Use Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL:

- Multi-AZ for production where required.
- Encryption at rest.
- Automated backups.
- Private subnet group.
- Parameter and maintenance windows.
- Performance and connection monitoring.

## 7. Documents and Uploaded Files

Phase 1 may store content in PostgreSQL. The design must keep a storage abstraction so future AWS deployment can move journal files and supporting documents to private S3 buckets with server-side encryption and controlled signed access.

## 8. Secrets

Store:

- Database credentials.
- JWT signing secret/private key.
- External integration secrets.

Use AWS Secrets Manager or Systems Manager Parameter Store. Never bake secrets into frontend builds, repository files, AMIs, or deployment scripts.

## 9. TLS and DNS

- HTTPS only.
- Use ACM-managed certificates where applicable.
- Redirect HTTP to HTTPS.
- Configure approved Route 53 or corporate DNS records.
- Restrict CORS to the production frontend origin.

## 10. Logging and Monitoring

- Backend structured logs to CloudWatch.
- Frontend hosting/access logs where supported.
- Load balancer logs to approved storage.
- RDS metrics and alarms.
- Correlation IDs across API requests.

Alarms should cover:

```text
5xx error rate
backend health-check failures
high latency
RDS CPU/storage/connections
parse failure spikes
authentication failure spikes
```

## 11. Deployment Flow

```text
Source control
  -> build and test
  -> package immutable frontend/backend artifacts
  -> database pre-deployment scripts
  -> deploy backend
  -> deploy frontend
  -> smoke test
  -> monitor
```

Database scripts run once under controlled release identity before application features depending on them are enabled.

## 12. Availability and Recovery

- Use multiple Availability Zones for production compute/database where required.
- Retain automated backups and snapshots.
- Document RTO and RPO when business requirements are approved.
- Test application rollback and database restore procedures.

## 13. Security

- Least-privilege IAM roles.
- No long-lived AWS keys in source control.
- Private database.
- Security-group restrictions.
- Encryption in transit and at rest.
- CloudTrail and approved audit logging.
- Patch management for VM-based deployments.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Public traffic uses HTTPS.
- RDS is private.
- Secrets come from managed secret storage.
- Logs reach CloudWatch with correlation IDs.
- Production database access is restricted.
- Deployment and rollback are documented and tested.


# Environment-Variables

# Environment Variables

## 1. Purpose

This document defines configuration values for local, test, and AWS environments. Real secrets must never be committed.

## 2. Backend Variables

```text
APP_ENV=local
APP_HOST=0.0.0.0
APP_PORT=8000
API_V1_PREFIX=/api/v1
DATABASE_URL=postgresql+<driver>://<user>:<password>@localhost:5432/journal_certification
JWT_SECRET_KEY=<secret>
JWT_ALGORITHM=HS256
ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES=60
REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRE_HOURS=12
CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3000
LOG_LEVEL=INFO
LOG_FORMAT=text
CORRELATION_ID_HEADER=X-Correlation-ID
MAX_UPLOAD_SIZE_BYTES=<approved-limit>
```

For production, prefer asymmetric JWT signing if approved and store keys in managed secrets.

## 3. Frontend Variables

```text
NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8000/api/v1
NEXT_PUBLIC_LOGO_URL=https://www.baxter.com/themes/custom/cosmos/logo.svg
NEXT_PUBLIC_TERMS_URL=https://www.baxter.com/terms-use
NEXT_PUBLIC_PRIVACY_URL=https://www.baxter.com/policies-positions/global-privacy-policy
```

Only values prefixed with `NEXT_PUBLIC_` are exposed to the browser. Never place secrets in public variables.

## 4. AWS Variables

Recommended non-secret runtime configuration:

```text
APP_ENV=production
LOG_LEVEL=INFO
LOG_FORMAT=json
CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=https://<frontend-host>
```

Secret values are injected from Secrets Manager or Parameter Store rather than a deployed `.env` file.

## 5. Validation

The backend validates required variables at startup using typed settings. Missing or invalid required configuration must fail readiness/startup with a clear protected log message.

The frontend build must fail when required public configuration is absent.

## 6. Environment Files

Allowed in repository:

```text
.env.example
.env.local.example
```

Not allowed:

```text
.env
.env.local
.env.production
files containing real credentials
```

Add secret files to `.gitignore`.

## 7. Database URL Characters

Passwords containing characters such as `@`, `:`, `/`, `#`, `%`, or `^` must be URL-encoded in a URI-style database URL, or passed through a safer structured configuration method.

Do not manually substitute special characters without encoding rules.

## 8. Configuration Ownership

- Application team owns variable definitions and validation.
- Deployment team owns environment-specific values.
- Security/operations owns production secret access and rotation.

## 9. Rotation

JWT and database credentials require documented rotation. Rotation must account for existing sessions and connection pools.

## 10. Acceptance Criteria

- No real secret is committed.
- Frontend public variables contain no secret.
- Startup validates configuration.
- Production secrets come from managed storage.
- CORS uses exact approved origins.


# Operations-And-Support-Guide

# Operations and Support Guide

## 1. Purpose

This guide defines normal operations, monitoring, incident triage, support evidence, routine checks, and recovery responsibilities.

## 2. Daily Health Checks

Verify:

- Frontend availability.
- Backend liveness and readiness.
- Database connectivity.
- Authentication success rate.
- API error rate.
- Parse success/failure counts.
- RDS CPU, storage, and connections.
- CloudWatch alarms.

## 3. Health Endpoints

```text
GET /health/live
GET /health/ready
```

Liveness checks process availability. Readiness checks required dependencies such as database connectivity without exposing sensitive details.

## 4. Support Information

Collect:

- UTC timestamp.
- Correlation ID.
- User ID or ADID, handled according to privacy policy.
- Active role.
- Browser and route.
- Journal ID or file-load ID.
- Error code.
- Screenshot without confidential content where possible.

Never request passwords or tokens.

## 5. Common Incidents

### Login Failure

Check account status, assigned roles, session records, JWT configuration, system clock, and authentication logs.

### Role Switch Failure

Check `user_roles`, active flag, session status, token refresh, and `ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED` logs.

### View Journal Progress Empty

Check selected period, assignments, journal status, user scope, and column filters.

### Parse Failure

Check file-load status, parse-error rows, exact 27-field structure where applicable, Preparer identity, dependent mapping, currency rates, and transaction rollback logs.

### Workflow Action Failure

Check ownership, active role, current status, version/concurrency, segregation of duties, and audit insert failure.

### Currency Rate Missing

Check selected period, pair, effective dates, active flag, overlap, and source load batch.

## 6. Log Search

Use correlation ID first. Then filter by:

```text
journal_id
file_load_id
error_code
user_id
request_path
```

Application logs and business audit records serve different purposes and must not be conflated.

## 7. Database Operations

- Use read-only access for routine support.
- Never update workflow status directly without approved recovery procedure.
- Never delete audit history.
- Back up before approved production data correction.
- Record change ticket, script, operator, timestamp, and validation result.

## 8. Restart Procedure

For backend compute:

1. Confirm incident scope.
2. Drain/stop traffic if required.
3. Restart using the approved AWS platform procedure.
4. Verify readiness.
5. Run smoke tests.
6. Monitor error and latency metrics.

Restarting must not be used to conceal recurring failures.

## 9. Deployment Verification

After every release:

- Health endpoints pass.
- Login works.
- Role switching works.
- Previous-month period is correct.
- Journal progress loads.
- One approved workflow smoke test passes.
- Currency/dependency screens are reachable for System Admin.
- 404 and access-denied behavior is correct.
- Logs and correlation IDs are present.

## 10. Backup and Recovery

Use RDS automated backups and snapshots according to the approved retention policy. Document restore-test results. File/document recovery must account for PostgreSQL or future S3 storage.

## 11. Patching and Maintenance

- Patch OS/runtime for VM-based hosting.
- Review Python and npm dependency updates.
- Test security updates before release.
- Schedule RDS maintenance.
- Notify users of planned downtime through approved channels.

## 12. Incident Severity

Suggested categories:

```text
Critical: application unavailable or data-integrity risk
High: core workflow blocked for many users
Medium: one module degraded with workaround
Low: cosmetic or minor issue
```

Final SLA and escalation times require business and operations approval.

## 13. Security Incident Handling

If tokens, secrets, or unauthorized access may be involved:

- Restrict access.
- Preserve logs.
- Rotate affected secrets through approved process.
- Invalidate compromised sessions.
- Engage security incident response.
- Do not place sensitive evidence in normal tickets.

## 14. Runbook Ownership

Each production module should name an application owner, operational owner, escalation contact, log location, dashboard, known dependencies, and rollback procedure.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Support can investigate using correlation ID.
- Health checks and alarms exist.
- Common incident runbooks are documented.
- Direct data changes are governed.
- Backup and restore are tested.
- Security incidents follow approved escalation.

# architecture

# Executive-Summary

# Executive Summary

## 1. Purpose

This document establishes the executive foundation for a role-based journal workflow application. The solution supports controlled journal ingestion, assignment, certification, approval, rejection, decertification, audit history, currency-rate visibility, dependent validation, notifications, and assigned-work visibility.

The architecture is based only on the requirements, screenshots, sample HTML/CSS, CSV column definitions, workbook data, SQL schema, SQL seed data, and workflow clarifications supplied for the project. Features without enough information are not to be invented. Unsupported routes must display the standard `404 - Page Not Found` experience.

## 2. Business Outcome

The solution is intended to provide:

- A consistent landing dashboard for authenticated users.
- Role-aware dashboard content and navigation.
- A global accounting period context, defaulted to the previous calendar month.
- A journal progress workbench with column-aligned filters and pagination.
- Controlled actions based on assignment ownership and workflow status.
- A two-step JDE journal-file process: Upload, then Parse.
- Atomic journal parsing with rollback on any validation or database failure.
- Preparer/Reconciler certification and decertification.
- Approver approval and rejection.
- Complete audit logging for Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify.
- System Admin access to dependent validation and currency screens.
- Centralized authentication, authorization, error handling, and notification messaging.

## 3. Architecture Principles

### 3.1 Hybrid Governance

```text
Workflow Logic = Code (Safe)
Business Configuration = Database (Flexible)
```

Workflow states, action eligibility, role authorization rules, and transaction behavior belong in code and are represented by enums. Users, role assignments, dependent validation, currency rates, rejection reasons, certification statements, and other business-maintained values belong in PostgreSQL.

### 3.2 Evidence-Based Scope

A screen or workflow is included only when supported by at least one supplied artifact or an explicit requirement. The application must not create speculative pages or workflows.

### 3.3 No Partial Financial Updates

Journal parsing and workflow actions are transactional. A transaction is committed only after all required changes, including the audit event, have flushed successfully. On validation failure, flush failure, or unexpected error, the transaction is rolled back.

### 3.4 Security at Every Layer

Frontend route guards improve usability, but backend authorization is authoritative. Every protected API validates the bearer token, active session, active role, required permission, and journal assignment when applicable.

### 3.5 Maintainability

The implementation uses modular frontend features and an enterprise backend structure based on controllers, service interfaces, service implementations, repository interfaces, and repository implementations.

## 4. Target Technology

### Frontend

- Next.js
- React
- TypeScript
- Ant Design for shell, menu, modal, form, popup, and common interactions
- AG Grid for data-intensive journal and currency grids
- TanStack Query for server state
- Redux Toolkit or a focused application context for authenticated user, active role, and selected period
- CSS Modules and neutral design tokens

### Backend

- Python 3.11 or later
- FastAPI
- Pydantic
- SQLAlchemy
- JWT bearer authentication
- PostgreSQL driver suitable for synchronous or asynchronous implementation

### Database

- PostgreSQL 18 for the current local environment
- Version-controlled SQL files
- No Alembic or migration framework

### Deployment Direction

- AWS-hosted deployment
- Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL
- Approved AWS compute option for FastAPI
- Approved AWS hosting option for Next.js
- AWS Secrets Manager or Systems Manager Parameter Store for secrets
- Amazon CloudWatch for logs and operational monitoring

## 5. Confirmed Roles

- Preparer/Reconciler, displayed as **Preparer** in the user role menu where required by the reference UI.
- Approver.
- Reviewer, with dashboard confirmed but business workflow actions intentionally left undefined.
- System Admin.
- Local Admin, retained for upload and parse responsibilities where the current implementation separates operational upload from system configuration.

## 6. Confirmed Primary Screens

- Login.
- Role-aware landing dashboard.
- To Do list.
- User profile and role selection.
- View Journal Progress.
- Journal detail and workflow-action view.
- Journal-file upload, parse result, and parse-error views.
- Dependent Validation.
- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information, including cross-currency and prior-12-period views.
- Standard 404 page.

## 7. Branding

The header displays only the Baxter logo. No application title is displayed beside the logo.

```text
https://www.baxter.com/themes/custom/cosmos/logo.svg
```

Clicking the logo navigates to the active role's landing dashboard.

The footer displays:

```text
©Copyright 2026 Baxter. All rights reserved.
Cookies | Terms & Conditions | Privacy Policy
```

Terms and Privacy links use the specified Baxter URLs. Cookies remains non-clickable until a URL or cookie-preference workflow is supplied.

## 8. Delivery Definition

Phase 1 is considered architecturally complete when the confirmed screens, workflow rules, security controls, atomic transactions, audit trail, centralized messages, SQL-based database setup, and AWS deployment notes are implemented and testable. Undefined modules remain unavailable and resolve to the standard 404 page.


# Solution-Overview

# Solution Overview

## 1. Solution Context

The solution is a browser-based, role-aware financial workflow application. It receives journal data originating from a JDE file, validates and persists that data, assigns journal responsibility, presents role-specific dashboards and journal progress views, and supports controlled workflow actions.

## 2. Logical Architecture

```text
Browser
  |
  v
Next.js Application
  |  - Application shell
  |  - Role-aware navigation
  |  - Dashboard tabs and widgets
  |  - AG Grid workbenches
  |  - Notifications and dialogs
  |
  v
FastAPI REST API
  |  - Controllers
  |  - Application service interfaces
  |  - Application service implementations
  |  - Domain services and workflow rules
  |  - Repository interfaces
  |  - Repository implementations
  |
  v
PostgreSQL
  |  - Users and sessions
  |  - Roles and assignments
  |  - Journal files, headers, and lines
  |  - Certifications and audit events
  |  - Dependent validation
  |  - Currency rates
```

## 3. Functional Modules

### 3.1 Authentication and Session

- Login endpoint returns an access token and refresh-token arrangement.
- Protected APIs require `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>`.
- The session record is validated for every protected request.
- Role switching is validated by the backend and returns a new access token containing the selected active role.

### 3.2 Application Shell

The common shell contains:

- Baxter logo.
- Global period selector.
- Notification control.
- To Do control.
- User profile and role selector.
- Role-aware left navigation.
- Main content area.
- Global footer.
- Shared success, error, warning, and information notification layer.

### 3.3 Dashboard

Dashboards use a shared shell and role-specific content.

- Preparer dashboard is confirmed from the supplied dashboard artifacts.
- Approver dashboard contains **My Work** and **Analytics** tabs.
- Reviewer dashboard contains **My Work** and **Analytics** tabs; reviewer business actions are not defined.
- System Admin dashboard content is limited to confirmed administration capabilities and must not invent unsupported widgets.

Phase 1 dashboards are fixed. Widget rearrangement, add/remove, and personalized layout editing observed in the reference artifacts are not included unless later approved.

### 3.4 Journal File Upload and Parse

The process is explicitly two-step:

```text
Upload -> status UPLOADED
Parse  -> status PARSING -> PARSED_SUCCESSFULLY or PARSE_FAILED
```

Upload stores the file and metadata only. Parse validates the entire file, resolves users and dependent validation, creates journals and assignments, records audit data, flushes all records, and commits only when every operation succeeds.

### 3.5 View Journal Progress

This is the primary journal operations workbench. The supplied CSV defines the complete set of available columns. The UI must support large horizontal datasets, column selection, sorting, pagination, and a filter textbox in the space directly below each filterable column header.

The action column resolves as follows:

- `Edit` when the journal is unassigned.
- `Cert` when the journal is assigned to an eligible Preparer or Approver context.
- `View` when the journal has moved to another role bucket or is otherwise read-only for the current role.

### 3.6 Journal Detail

Opening `Cert` or `View` navigates to journal detail. Actions inside the page are role and status controlled:

- Certify.
- Approve.
- Reject.
- Decertify.

The grid link and page action are separate concepts. For example, `Cert` opens the actionable detail view; the actionable button inside the page may be `Certify` for a Preparer or `Approve` and `Reject` for an Approver.

### 3.7 Dependent Validation

System Admin can view the dependent-validation setup. The first logical value is the Preparer identifier coming from JDE. Dependent validation resolves the configured Approver and supports assignment routing during parse.

### 3.8 Currency

The confirmed currency capability includes:

- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Cross-currency display.
- Show prior 12 periods.
- Seed data sourced from the supplied currency workbook.

### 3.9 Audit

Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify always produce audit events in the same database transaction as the workflow change. Audit failure causes the business action to roll back.

## 4. Response and Notification Model

All APIs return consistent response contracts. Failures include a stable enum-based code, a user-friendly message, severity, structured details where applicable, and a correlation ID. The frontend uses one shared notification module rather than hardcoded page messages.

## 5. Database Lifecycle

Database change management uses SQL files executed with `psql`. The currently supplied baseline commands are:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification -f .\seed\001_schema.sql
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification -f .\seed\002_seed_phase1.sql
```

The repository architecture may place those files under `backend/db/seed/`, but the documented commands must match the final checked-in paths exactly.

## 6. Quality Attributes

- Secure direct-route and API access.
- Atomic financial updates.
- Traceable audit history.
- Consistent messages and error handling.
- Maintainable enterprise naming.
- Keyboard-accessible, labelled controls.
- Dense enterprise grid behavior.
- AWS-ready configuration with no secrets in source control.


# Scope-And-Assumptions

# Scope and Assumptions

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the boundary of Phase 1 and prevents unsupported functionality from being inferred from reference screenshots or source HTML.

## 2. In Scope

### 2.1 Common Application Capabilities

- Login.
- JWT bearer authentication.
- Refresh-token/session handling.
- Logout and session invalidation.
- User profile menu.
- Backend-validated active-role switching.
- Baxter logo navigation to landing dashboard.
- Global previous-month default period.
- Notifications.
- To Do list showing pending or assigned actions for the active user.
- Central popup/toast/dialog notification handling.
- Standard access-denied and 404 pages.

### 2.2 Dashboard

- Preparer dashboard based on supplied artifacts.
- Approver dashboard with My Work and Analytics tabs.
- Reviewer dashboard with My Work and Analytics tabs.
- System Admin landing view limited to confirmed System Admin functions.
- Role-driven left-navigation changes.

Dashboard widget personalization is not in Phase 1, even though the reference HTML contains widget arrangement and customization controls.

### 2.3 Journal File Processing

- Local Admin upload of JDE journal file.
- Separate Parse button.
- File-level status and parse progress notification.
- Complete-file validation.
- Atomic database transaction.
- Zero journal business-data updates when parse fails.
- Row-level parse-error persistence outside journal business tables.
- Success/failure results and user notification.

### 2.4 Journal Workbench

- View Journal Progress.
- Complete column model based on the supplied CSV.
- Period load from backend.
- Column filters displayed under their own headers.
- Filter input width equal to column width.
- Filter activation after more than two characters.
- Client filtering, sorting, and pagination after the period dataset is loaded, subject to later performance review.
- Action-column resolution: Edit, Cert, View.

### 2.5 Journal Detail and Workflow

- Journal details.
- Journal lines.
- Supporting documents.
- Comments.
- Hyperlinks where present in the approved detail design.
- Certification details.
- Audit trail.
- Preparer Certify and Decertify.
- Approver Approve and Reject.
- Approval closes the journal.
- Rejection returns the journal to the Preparer queue.
- Preparer decertification pulls a pending journal back from the Approver queue.

### 2.6 System Administration

- View dependent validation.
- JDE Preparer identifier as the first logical dependency value.
- Currency Settings based on supplied screenshots.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Show Cross Currency.
- Show Past/Prior 12 Periods.
- Currency seed data based on the supplied workbook.

## 3. Explicitly Not Defined

- Reviewer workflow actions.
- Reviewer certification, approval, rejection, return, or decertification behavior.
- Any unsupported menu page for which no reliable requirement or artifact exists.
- Personalized dashboard layout editing.
- Enterprise SSO implementation.
- Automated emails or escalations.
- Period close/lock workflow.
- Advanced report authoring.

Reviewer screens may display dashboard information supported by the artifacts, but the implementation must not add reviewer workflow buttons until requirements are supplied.

## 4. Unsupported Route Policy

Unsupported functionality must not use a speculative placeholder business screen. Direct navigation to an unknown or unsupported route displays:

```text
404
Page Not Found
The requested page is not available for the current release.
```

The page provides:

- Go to Dashboard.
- Go Back.
- Correlation ID only when the 404 originated from an API or routing failure requiring support traceability.

## 5. Assumptions

### 5.1 Period

The default accounting period is the previous calendar month. If the current date is August 2026, the default is `07/2026`. If the current date is January 2027, the default is `12/2026`.

### 5.2 Role Naming

The internal code may retain `PREPARER_RECONCILER` while the user-facing profile displays `Preparer` when required by the approved UI. `BLPREPARER` and `BLAPPROVER` are valid external mappings. The mistyped external code previously discussed is not valid and must not appear anywhere.

### 5.3 System Admin and Local Admin

System Admin is responsible for the confirmed system configuration screens. Local Admin is retained for operational file upload and parse if the implemented role separation follows the current requirements. The two roles must not be silently merged.

### 5.4 UI Fidelity

The application should closely reproduce the supplied layout, density, typography, color behavior, header, navigation, grid, filters, pagination, and interaction patterns. Proprietary source code, copied third-party branding, and external product names are excluded.

### 5.5 Database

PostgreSQL is available locally. SQL files are executed directly with `psql`. No Alembic, Flyway, Liquibase, or Docker-based database setup is used in the current phase.

### 5.6 AWS

The application is intended for AWS deployment. Exact AWS hosting choices remain deployment decisions, but the application must externalize configuration and secrets and support Amazon RDS PostgreSQL.

## 6. Change-Control Rule

A new screen, role action, transition, dashboard widget, API, or database behavior must be added to this architecture before implementation. Until documented, the feature remains unsupported.


# Roles-And-Permissions

# Roles and Permissions

## 1. Purpose

This document defines role behavior, permissions, active-role handling, and the boundary between role possession and journal ownership.

## 2. Authorization Principles

```text
Authentication != Role Authorization != Journal Ownership
```

A valid login does not grant access to every page. Possessing a role does not grant access to every journal. A workflow action requires:

1. A valid bearer token.
2. An active server-side session.
3. The required active role.
4. The role assigned to the user.
5. Access to the requested page/API.
6. A valid journal assignment when the action is journal-specific.
7. A valid workflow state transition.

## 3. Preparer

### 3.1 Internal and Display Naming

- Internal role code: `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- User-facing display: `Preparer`, unless the final approved UI explicitly requires `Preparer/Reconciler`.
- External JDE mapping: `BLPREPARER`.

### 3.2 Confirmed Access

- Landing dashboard.
- To Do list for assigned work.
- View Journal Progress.
- Assigned journal detail.
- Documents, comments, hyperlinks, certification details, and audit trail for authorized journals.

### 3.3 Confirmed Actions

- Open `Edit` for an unassigned journal only when the corresponding assignment function is authorized.
- Open `Cert` for an actionable assigned journal.
- Certify a journal in a valid Preparer state.
- View and Decertify a journal awaiting Approver action.
- Re-certify a journal returned by Approver rejection.
- View a journal in another role bucket in read-only mode.

### 3.4 Restrictions

- Cannot approve.
- Cannot reject as Approver.
- Cannot use System Admin currency or dependent-validation administration.
- Cannot act on a journal assigned to another user.

## 4. Approver

### 4.1 Naming

- Internal role code: `APPROVER`.
- External JDE mapping: `BLAPPROVER`.

### 4.2 Confirmed Access

- Landing dashboard with `My Work` and `Analytics` tabs.
- To Do list for assigned approval work.
- View Journal Progress.
- Assigned journal detail.
- Documents, comments, certification details, and audit trail for authorized journals.

### 4.3 Confirmed Actions

The Approver has only these workflow decisions in Phase 1:

- Approve.
- Reject.

`Cert` in the progress-grid Action column opens the actionable certification/detail screen for an assigned Approver journal. The detail screen presents `Approve` and `Reject` rather than a generic workflow decision.

### 4.4 Transitions

- Approve: `PENDING_APPROVER -> CLOSED`.
- Reject: `PENDING_APPROVER -> REJECTED`, and the journal returns to the Preparer queue for review and re-certification.

### 4.5 Restrictions

- No Approver decertify in Phase 1.
- Cannot approve or reject an unassigned journal.
- Cannot approve a journal when the user is also the journal's Preparer.
- Cannot use System Admin configuration screens unless the user also holds and actively selects `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.

## 5. Reviewer

### 5.1 Confirmed Access

- Reviewer role exists.
- Reviewer dashboard exists.
- Reviewer dashboard contains `My Work` and `Analytics` tabs.
- Role-aware navigation changes when Reviewer is selected.

### 5.2 Undefined Behavior

The following are intentionally not defined:

- Reviewer certification.
- Reviewer approval.
- Reviewer rejection.
- Reviewer return.
- Reviewer decertification.
- Reviewer ownership transition.

No reviewer workflow button may be implemented until a business rule is supplied. Reviewer pages beyond the confirmed dashboard and confirmed navigation target must resolve according to the supported-screen inventory or the standard 404 policy.

## 6. System Admin

### 6.1 Internal Role Code

`SYSTEM_ADMIN`

### 6.2 Confirmed Access

- System Admin landing dashboard/view.
- Dependent Validation in view-only mode, unless later requirements explicitly permit edit/upload.
- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Show Cross Currency.
- Show Prior 12 Periods.

### 6.3 Restrictions

- System Admin does not automatically gain journal workflow ownership.
- System Admin journal access must be explicitly read-only or assignment-based.
- System Admin must not certify, approve, reject, or decertify solely because of the admin role.

## 7. Local Admin

### 7.1 Internal Role Code

`LOCAL_ADMIN`

### 7.2 Confirmed Operational Access

- Upload JDE journal file.
- View uploaded-file status.
- Click Parse.
- View parse results.
- View row-level parse errors.
- Retry parsing after correcting a permitted issue or re-uploading a corrected file.

### 7.3 Restrictions

- Upload does not populate journals.
- Parse failure does not populate journal headers, lines, or assignments.
- Local Admin does not gain System Admin currency/dependency access unless separately assigned.

## 8. Multiple Roles and Active Role

A user can possess multiple roles, but only one role is active in the session at a time. Selecting a role from the top-right profile menu triggers a backend request. The backend verifies that the user owns the requested role, updates the session, and issues a new access token.

Changing only browser state must never authorize a new role.

## 9. Same-User Control

The same person cannot be both Preparer and Approver for the same journal. This rule must be enforced during assignment resolution, parse validation, and workflow action validation.

## 10. Permission Enforcement

Permissions are enforced in three places:

- Frontend route and action visibility.
- Backend role authorization.
- Backend journal-assignment and status validation.

The backend remains authoritative even if the UI mistakenly displays an action.


# Role-Matrix

# Role Matrix

## 1. Purpose

This matrix consolidates confirmed Phase 1 role access and explicitly distinguishes confirmed, read-only, undefined, and prohibited behavior.

## 2. Legend

- **Allowed**: confirmed and actionable.
- **Read only**: confirmed visibility without workflow change.
- **Conditional**: allowed only with required assignment, role, and status.
- **Undefined**: no implementation until a requirement is supplied.
- **Denied**: must be blocked by UI and API.

## 3. Screen Access Matrix

| Capability | Preparer | Approver | Reviewer | System Admin | Local Admin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Login | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed |
| Landing Dashboard | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed |
| My Work tab | As shown by approved Preparer design | Allowed | Allowed | Not assumed | Not assumed |
| Analytics tab | Not assumed unless shown | Allowed | Allowed | Not assumed | Not assumed |
| To Do list | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed where supported | Allowed where supported | Allowed where supported |
| User Profile | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed | Allowed |
| Role Switch | Conditional | Conditional | Conditional | Conditional | Conditional |
| View Journal Progress | Allowed | Allowed | Only if supported by confirmed navigation | Read only if explicitly enabled | Read only if explicitly enabled |
| Journal Detail | Conditional | Conditional | Undefined | Read only if explicitly enabled | Read only if explicitly enabled |
| Upload JDE File | Denied | Denied | Denied | Denied unless separately authorized | Allowed |
| Parse JDE File | Denied | Denied | Denied | Denied unless separately authorized | Allowed |
| Dependent Validation | Denied | Denied | Denied | Read only | Denied unless separately authorized |
| Currency Settings | Denied | Denied | Denied | Allowed | Denied unless separately authorized |
| Currency Rates Information | Denied | Denied | Denied | Allowed | Denied unless separately authorized |

## 4. Journal Action Matrix

| Action | Preparer | Approver | Reviewer | System Admin | Local Admin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Edit unassigned journal | Conditional | Conditional only if assignment design permits | Undefined | Denied | Conditional only if operational assignments permit |
| Open Cert link | Conditional | Conditional | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Open View link | Conditional read only | Conditional read only | Conditional only if a supported route exists | Read only if enabled | Read only if enabled |
| Certify | Conditional | Not used as final Approver decision | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Approve | Denied | Conditional | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Reject | Denied | Conditional | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Decertify | Conditional | Denied | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Upload supporting document | Conditional | Read or upload only if approved by detail requirements | Undefined | Denied | Denied |
| Add comment | Conditional | Conditional | Undefined | Read only unless explicitly enabled | Read only unless explicitly enabled |
| View audit trail | Conditional | Conditional | Undefined | Read only if enabled | Read only if enabled |

## 5. Action-Column Resolution

The View Journal Progress grid resolves exactly one of the primary row links:

### Edit

Shown when the journal is unassigned and the active role is authorized to perform assignment/setup activity.

### Cert

Shown when the journal is assigned to an actionable Preparer or Approver context for the current user. `Cert` opens the actionable detail/certification page. The buttons inside the page are role specific.

### View

Shown when the journal has moved into another role bucket, is closed, or is otherwise read-only for the active role.

## 6. Workflow Matrix

| Current Status | Active Role | Detail Actions | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PENDING_PREPARER` | Assigned Preparer | Certify | `PENDING_APPROVER` |
| `REJECTED` | Assigned Preparer | Certify | `PENDING_APPROVER` |
| `PENDING_APPROVER` | Assigned Preparer | View, Decertify | `PENDING_PREPARER` after Decertify |
| `PENDING_APPROVER` | Assigned Approver | Approve, Reject | `CLOSED` or `REJECTED` |
| `CLOSED` | Preparer or Approver with access | View | No status change |

## 7. Enforcement Notes

- Role-code enums control workflow logic.
- Database role rows support assignment and display but do not redefine workflow behavior.
- Reviewer actions remain undefined and must not be inferred from the dashboard tabs.
- Unauthorized direct navigation returns 403 when the route exists but access is denied.
- Unknown or unsupported routes return 404.


# Application-Shell

# Application Shell

## 1. Purpose

The application shell delivers a consistent layout across all authenticated screens and coordinates global state such as user identity, active role, selected period, notifications, To Do items, navigation, and shared messages.

## 2. Layout Regions

```text
+---------------------------------------------------------------+
| Global Header                                                 |
+----------+----------------------------------------------------+
| Left     | Page Header / Tabs / Content                       |
| Nav      |                                                    |
|          |                                                    |
+----------+----------------------------------------------------+
| Global Footer                                                 |
+---------------------------------------------------------------+
```

## 3. Global Header

The header contains:

- Baxter logo only, with no application name beside it.
- Period selector.
- Notifications control.
- To Do control.
- User profile and active-role selector.

The header remains visible across authenticated screens unless a confirmed full-screen interaction intentionally replaces it.

## 4. Left Navigation

The left navigation is role aware. When the active role changes:

1. The backend validates the role change.
2. A new token is issued.
3. The user context updates.
4. The available menu updates.
5. The user is navigated to the selected role's landing dashboard.

The UI must not show unsupported menu items simply because they exist in a reference application. Only confirmed pages are displayed.

## 5. Main Content

The main content region supports:

- Dashboard tabs and widgets.
- Dense, horizontally scrollable grids.
- Column filter rows.
- Page titles and action controls.
- Slide-out panels where required by confirmed screens.
- Shared loading overlays.
- Shared empty states.
- Standard access-denied and 404 experiences.

## 6. Footer

The footer is common across all authenticated pages and uses the approved Baxter text and links. Long grids may use a sticky-page layout in which the footer follows the content rather than covering pagination.

## 7. Global Client State

The shell owns or provides access to:

- Authenticated user.
- Assigned roles.
- Active role.
- Session expiry information.
- Selected global period.
- Notification count and drawer/popover state.
- To Do count and drawer/popover state.
- Shared notification/message provider.
- Correlation ID display for errors.

## 8. Period Behavior

The default selected period is calculated once when a new authenticated session or shell state is created. It is the previous calendar month. The selected period persists while navigating between supported pages.

A role switch keeps the selected period unless a role-specific rule later requires reset. Logout clears the selected period from authenticated state.

## 9. Loading and Busy States

The shell provides a reusable loading layer:

- Page-level loading for role context and navigation.
- Widget-level loading for dashboard data.
- Grid-level loading for period data.
- Button-level busy state for workflow actions.
