- Parsing state with disabled Parse button and visible progress message.

Duplicate submissions must be prevented while an action is in progress.

## 10. Notification Layer

All modules use the shared notification provider. Pages must not call browser `alert()` or render raw exception text.

Supported severities:

```text
SUCCESS
ERROR
WARNING
INFO
```

Critical failures use a persistent popup/dialog. Routine success and information messages may use dismissible toasts.

## 11. Accessibility

- Every input has a label or accessible name.
- Interactive icons have tooltips and `aria-label` values.
- Header controls are keyboard accessible.
- Focus states remain visible.
- Navigation indicates the active item.
- Drawers and dialogs trap and restore focus correctly.
- The logo link has an accessible label such as `Go to dashboard`.

## 12. Responsive Behavior

The application is desktop-first because the journal grid contains many columns. The shell must still behave safely on smaller screens:

- Left navigation may collapse.
- Header controls may move into an overflow menu.
- Grids remain horizontally scrollable.
- Footer links wrap without overlapping content.
- No financial data is hidden merely to fit a small viewport.

## 13. Error Boundaries

The shell catches unexpected frontend rendering failures and presents a standard application error with a retry option and correlation/support context. Backend API errors remain handled by the shared API client and notification provider.


# Header-And-Footer

# Header and Footer

## 1. Header Branding

The header displays only the Baxter logo. No application title, temporary project name, vendor name, or external product name is displayed.

Logo URL:

```text
https://www.baxter.com/themes/custom/cosmos/logo.svg
```

The implementation should retrieve or package the approved logo in a way compliant with deployment and security requirements. If external asset loading is restricted in production, store an approved copy under the frontend public assets folder while preserving the source reference in controlled documentation.

## 2. Logo Interaction

The logo is an interactive link.

```text
Click logo -> navigate to the active role's landing dashboard
```

Expected route:

```text
/dashboard
```

If dashboard routing uses a role-aware route, the shell resolves the role-specific content after navigation.

## 3. Header Content Order

Recommended desktop order:

```text
Left:  Baxter Logo
Right: Period Selector | Notifications | To Do | User Profile/Role
```

No text label is displayed beside the logo.

## 4. Period Selector

- Displays `MM/YYYY`.
- Defaults to the previous month.
- Is globally available on authenticated screens where the reference shell shows period context.
- Changing the period updates global period state.
- Data pages reload using the selected period.
- Indicates loading while period-bound data is being refreshed.

## 5. Notifications Control

- Displays an unread count when data exists.
- Opens a popup, drawer, or menu consistent with the supplied UI style.
- Shows application information and workflow notifications.
- Selecting a supported journal notification navigates to the corresponding page.
- If a notification points to an unsupported route, the application displays the standard 404 page.

## 6. To Do Control

The To Do control shows pending actions or tasks assigned to the logged-in user in the active role. The list may include fields confirmed by the supplied screenshot, such as product/context, key, due information, due date, role, and status.

Selecting a supported item navigates to its actionable page.

## 7. User Profile and Role Menu

The top-right user control displays user-profile information and all roles actively assigned to the user.

Role selection behavior:

```text
Select role
  -> call switch-role API
  -> backend validates user_roles
  -> update user_session.active_role_code
  -> issue new access token
  -> refresh menu and dashboard
```

The menu must not authorize a role through frontend state alone.

## 8. Header Styling

The header follows the supplied modern enterprise style:

- White background.
- Clear border or subtle separation from page content.
- IBM Plex Sans for shell/navigation text where consistent with supplied CSS.
- Compact control spacing.
- Purple navigation accent based on the supplied UI references.
- Strong visible focus styling.

CSS and component names must remain neutral and must not include external product names.

## 9. Footer Content

The footer displays exactly:

```text
©Copyright 2026 Baxter. All rights reserved.
Cookies | Terms & Conditions | Privacy Policy
```

Links:

```text
Terms & Conditions:
https://www.baxter.com/terms-use

Privacy Policy:
https://www.baxter.com/policies-positions/global-privacy-policy
```

`Cookies` remains plain text until a URL or cookie-preference requirement is supplied.

## 10. Footer Link Behavior

- Terms and Privacy links open safely, preferably in a new tab for external-site navigation.
- Use `rel="noopener noreferrer"` when opening a new tab.
- Links are keyboard accessible.
- Visible separators are not included in the accessible link names.

## 11. Footer Placement

- Present on all authenticated pages.
- Present on Login if approved by the final screen design.
- Must not obstruct grid pagination.
- Width follows the content shell.
- On long pages, footer follows page content.
- On short pages, shell layout may hold footer near the viewport bottom without using an overlapping fixed position.

## 12. Prohibited Branding

Do not display or include external product names in:

- Header.
- Footer.
- Browser title.
- Source comments.
- CSS class names.
- Component names.
- Routes.
- API messages.
- Logs.
- Repository documentation delivered with the application.


# Navigation-Architecture

# Navigation Architecture

## 1. Purpose

Navigation is driven by the active role and the supported-screen inventory. It must reflect only confirmed Phase 1 pages and must not expose unsupported reference-application pages.

## 2. Navigation Layers

### 2.1 Global Navigation

Available through the application shell:

- Logo to Dashboard.
- Period selector.
- Notifications.
- To Do.
- User profile.
- Role selector.

### 2.2 Role Navigation

The left navigation changes after a successful backend-validated role switch.

### 2.3 Context Navigation

Journal detail may use tabs/sections for details, documents, comments, hyperlinks, certification details, and audit trail. Currency screens may use the confirmed left-panel navigation.

## 3. Supported Route Set

Recommended routes:

```text
/login
/dashboard
/journals/progress
/journals/[journalId]
/admin/journal-files
/admin/journal-files/[fileLoadId]/results
/admin/dependent-validation
/admin/currency/settings
/admin/currency/rates
/notifications
/todo
/profile
/no-access
```

The final Next.js directory structure may adapt route segments, but user-facing behavior and authorization must remain consistent.

## 4. Preparer Navigation

Display only confirmed pages:

```text
Dashboard
View Journal Progress
```

Journal detail is reached from Edit, Cert, View, To Do, or notifications rather than requiring a separate left-menu item.

## 5. Approver Navigation

```text
Dashboard
View Journal Progress
```

Dashboard contains:

```text
My Work
Analytics
```

No separate speculative Approval Queue page is added unless the dashboard or View Journal Progress route supplies that function.

## 6. Reviewer Navigation

The supplied artifacts confirm role-specific navigation and a Reviewer dashboard with My Work and Analytics tabs. Only supported Reviewer menu entries should be rendered.

Because reviewer workflow is undefined, navigation must not expose speculative reviewer action pages.

## 7. System Admin Navigation

Confirmed items:

```text
Dashboard
Dependent Validation
Currency Settings
Currency Rates Information
```

The Currency module follows the supplied left-panel style where applicable.

## 8. Local Admin Navigation

Confirmed operational items:

```text
Dashboard
Upload Journal File
```

Upload Journal File includes uploaded-file history, Parse action, parse result, and parse errors. These may be tabs or routes within the same module.

## 9. Role Switch Navigation

After a successful role switch:

1. Close profile menu.
2. Replace token/user context.
3. Rebuild left navigation.
4. Navigate to `/dashboard`.
5. Preserve selected period unless future requirements specify otherwise.
6. Load role-appropriate dashboard tabs and data.

## 10. Direct URL Handling

- Unknown route: 404.
- Known route without valid authentication: redirect to Login.
- Known route with authentication but insufficient role: show Access Denied or redirect to `/no-access`.
- Known journal route without assignment/authorization: return API 403 and show the standard access-denied popup/page.

## 11. Menu Source of Truth

For Phase 1, the supported role menu may be defined in code because screen availability and route guards are workflow/security behavior. Do not create database-configurable menus that could enable an unimplemented or unauthorized route without deployment.

Business-maintained role assignment remains in the database.

## 12. Active State and Usability

- Highlight the active menu item.
- Preserve expanded/collapsed left-navigation state within the session.
- Provide accessible menu labels and keyboard navigation.
- Use neutral component and CSS names.
- Avoid showing disabled unsupported menu entries; omit them entirely.

## 13. Breadcrumbs and Back Navigation

Journal detail should provide clear context back to View Journal Progress. Browser Back must work. If breadcrumbs are implemented, use neutral labels such as:

```text
Dashboard / View Journal Progress / Journal <ID>
```

## 14. 404 Navigation

The 404 page includes:

- `Go to Dashboard`.
- `Go Back`.

It does not render a fake page for the unsupported feature.


# Period-Management

# Period Management

## 1. Purpose

The period determines the accounting month used by dashboard widgets, View Journal Progress, currency information, and other period-bound views.

## 2. Display Format

```text
MM/YYYY
```

Examples:

```text
07/2026
12/2026
01/2027
```

## 3. Default Period Rule

The default period is always the previous calendar month.

Examples:

| Current Date | Default Period |
|---|---|
| August 20, 2026 | `07/2026` |
| January 5, 2027 | `12/2026` |
| March 1, 2027 | `02/2027` |

The calculation must handle year rollover correctly.

## 4. Calculation Rule

Pseudocode:

```text
current_date = application clock date
previous_month_date = first day of current month minus one day
selected_period = format(previous_month_date, MM/YYYY)
```

The backend clock should be the source for authoritative period defaults when time-zone consistency matters. The frontend may calculate the same value for immediate display but should reconcile it with the authenticated bootstrap/user-context response.

## 5. Global Period State

The selected period belongs to the authenticated application shell, not to an individual page.

Pages consume the same period state:

- Dashboard.
- Approver My Work.
- Approver Analytics when period dependent.
- Reviewer dashboard when period dependent.
- View Journal Progress.
- Currency Rates Information.
- To Do where the supplied UX filters tasks by period.

## 6. Persistence

Recommended Phase 1 behavior:

- Initialize from backend-provided default.
- Preserve during client-side navigation.
- Preserve during active-role switch.
- Clear on logout.
- Optionally store in session storage for browser refresh, but validate that the value is a supported period before reuse.

Do not store period in a long-lived token because the period is user context, not authorization identity.

## 7. Period Change Flow

```text
User selects period
  -> validate MM/YYYY
  -> update global period state
  -> notify subscribed pages
  -> call period-bound APIs
  -> show loading state
  -> render refreshed results
```

## 8. Backend API Contract

Period-bound endpoints accept an explicit period parameter, for example:

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/progress?period=07/2026
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=07/2026
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026
```

The backend validates:

- Format.
- Valid month range `01` through `12`.
- Valid year range.
- Access to the requested period if future period restrictions are introduced.

## 9. Error Handling

Invalid period response example:

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT",
    "message": "Select a valid period in MM/YYYY format.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [],
    "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
  }
}
```

The UI shows the centralized error popup and keeps the last valid period selected.

## 10. Journal Filtering

Period selection is the primary backend filter for View Journal Progress. Once the period dataset is loaded:

- Column filters are applied in the UI.
- Sorting is applied before pagination.
- Pagination is applied to the filtered and sorted rows.

If data volume later exceeds acceptable client limits, server-side pagination can be introduced without changing the period contract.

## 11. Currency Prior-12-Periods Rule

When `Show Prior 12 Periods` is selected, the selected period is the anchor period. The UI displays the anchor and the preceding 12 periods, following the exact behavior evidenced by the supplied screenshot.

Example anchor `07/2026`:

```text
07/2026
06/2026
05/2026
04/2026
03/2026
02/2026
01/2026
12/2025
11/2025
10/2025
09/2025
08/2025
07/2025
```

The final product owner may confirm whether the phrase means 12 periods including or excluding the selected period. Until confirmed, the UI should follow the supplied screen behavior exactly.

## 12. Accessibility

- Period control has a visible label or accessible name.
- Keyboard selection is supported.
- Loading state is announced where practical.
- Validation text is associated with the control.


# Dashboard-Architecture

# Dashboard Architecture

## 1. Purpose

The dashboard module provides the authenticated landing experience for each active role. The supplied Preparer, Approver, and Reviewer dashboard artifacts show a shared application shell with role-specific dashboard content, tab handling, widget containers, loading states, and responsive grid behavior.

Phase 1 must reproduce the confirmed dashboard behavior without copying proprietary source code or introducing undefined business capabilities.

## 2. Confirmed Dashboard Model

The dashboard is not implemented as one static page. It is composed of:

```text
Dashboard Route
  -> Role Dashboard Resolver
  -> Dashboard Header and Tabs
  -> Role-Specific Content Definition
  -> Widget Grid
  -> Widget Data Services
  -> Shared Loading, Empty, and Error States
```

The same high-level framework is used for Preparer, Approver, and Reviewer, but the visible tabs, widgets, data, and navigation targets are resolved using the active role.

## 3. Phase 1 Design Decision

The reference HTML contains evidence of widget collapse, expand, fullscreen, add/remove, reset, edit, and rearrangement capabilities. Phase 1 will use a fixed dashboard layout.

Included:

- Role-specific dashboard content.
- Confirmed tabs.
- Independent widget loading.
- Widget refresh where required.
- Collapse or fullscreen only when the approved screen requires those controls.
- Responsive dashboard grid.

Excluded:

- User-customized widget placement.
- Add/remove widget catalog.
- Dashboard layout persistence.
- Drag-and-drop rearrangement.
- User-defined widget configuration.

## 4. Route and Role Resolution

Recommended route:

```text
/dashboard
```

The route does not encode the role in the URL. The backend-authenticated user context contains the active role, and the frontend resolves the correct dashboard.

```text
PREPARER_RECONCILER -> Preparer dashboard
APPROVER            -> Approver dashboard
REVIEWER            -> Reviewer dashboard
SYSTEM_ADMIN        -> System Admin dashboard
LOCAL_ADMIN         -> Local Admin landing view, if separately required
```

An unsupported active role produces a controlled authorization/configuration error rather than displaying another role's dashboard.

## 5. Default Period

Dashboard data uses the global period selected in the application shell. On a new session, the default is the previous calendar month.

Example:

```text
Current month: August 2026
Default period: 07/2026
```

Changing the period refreshes all period-bound dashboard data while preserving the active role and selected dashboard tab.

## 6. Dashboard Tabs

Confirmed tab behavior:

- Approver: `My Work` and `Analytics`.
- Reviewer: `My Work` and `Analytics`.
- Preparer: only the tabs confirmed by the supplied Preparer artifacts should be rendered. Do not add Analytics unless the approved screen contains it.
- System Admin: do not add My Work or Analytics unless supplied requirements support those tabs.

Tab state may be stored in the URL query string or local component state. A refresh should restore a valid tab when practical.

Example:

```text
/dashboard?tab=my-work
/dashboard?tab=analytics
```

Unknown tab values fall back to the role's default tab.

## 7. Widget Contract

Each widget definition contains:

```text
widget_code
role_code
supported_tab
period_required
position
column_span
row_span
refresh_behavior
empty_state_message_code
api_endpoint
```

Widget configuration is code-controlled for Phase 1. Business data comes from APIs and PostgreSQL.

## 8. Dashboard API

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY&tab=<tab-code>
```

The server determines the role from the validated bearer token and active session. The client must not send an arbitrary role to retrieve another role's dashboard.

Example response:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "active_role": "APPROVER",
    "period": "07/2026",
    "active_tab": "MY_WORK",
    "tabs": ["MY_WORK", "ANALYTICS"],
    "widgets": []
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 9. Loading Strategy

The dashboard shell loads first. Widgets may load together or independently depending on API design.

Required states:

- Dashboard bootstrap loading.
- Widget loading.
- Widget empty state.
- Widget recoverable error.
- Whole-dashboard authorization failure.

A failed widget must not necessarily blank the entire dashboard. The failed widget shows a user-friendly error and retry option unless the failure affects authorization or session validity.

## 10. Shared Components

Recommended frontend components:

```text
DashboardPage
RoleDashboardResolver
DashboardTabs
DashboardGrid
DashboardWidgetCard
DashboardWidgetHeader
DashboardWidgetBody
DashboardWidgetLoading
DashboardWidgetEmptyState
DashboardWidgetErrorState
```

## 11. Backend Structure

```text
api/v1/controllers/dashboard_controller.py
application/services/dashboard/get_dashboard_service.py
application/services/dashboard/impl/get_dashboard_service_impl.py
domain/services/dashboard_service.py
infrastructure/services/dashboard_service_impl.py
```

The controller handles HTTP only. The application service validates the active role and orchestrates widget data. Domain/infrastructure services calculate summaries or query repositories.

## 12. Styling Standards

The supplied UI artifacts indicate:

- IBM Plex Sans in global shell and navigation areas.
- Libre Franklin in selected page/component areas.
- A deep-purple navigation family.
- White widget backgrounds.
- Compact enterprise spacing.
- Subtle borders.
- Visible purple focus states.
- Chart tooltips using dark neutral backgrounds.

The implementation should mirror this visual language through neutral design tokens rather than copying vendor CSS class names.

## 13. Accessibility

- Tabs use proper tab semantics.
- Active tab is announced.
- Widget controls have labels and tooltips.
- Charts provide accessible summaries or equivalent tabular information when practical.
- Loading and error states are announced.
- Color is not the only indicator of status.

## 14. Security

- Dashboard APIs require bearer authentication.
- Active role is read from the validated session/token.
- The response excludes data outside the active user's authorized scope.
- Analytics must apply the same role and assignment restrictions as My Work.
- Directly changing a tab or query string never expands authorization.

## 15. Audit and Logging

Viewing a dashboard does not create a journal audit event. Application logs may record dashboard retrieval failures, authorization failures, response time, and correlation ID.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Logo navigation opens `/dashboard`.
- Dashboard content matches the active role.
- Default period is previous month.
- Approver and Reviewer show My Work and Analytics tabs.
- Unsupported tabs are not displayed.
- Widget errors use modular notifications and do not expose raw exceptions.
- Role switching loads the newly selected role's dashboard.
- Dashboard data cannot be retrieved for an unassigned role.


# Preparer-Dashboard

# Preparer Dashboard

## 1. Purpose

The Preparer dashboard is the landing experience when the active role is Preparer. It provides period-aware operational visibility and routes the user to confirmed journal work without inventing unsupported pages.

## 2. Role Context

- Internal role code: `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- Display role: `Preparer`, unless the final approved label requires `Preparer/Reconciler`.
- External mapping: `BLPREPARER`.

## 3. Entry Flow

```text
Login or role switch
  -> backend validates Preparer role
  -> new/current bearer token contains PREPARER_RECONCILER
  -> navigate to /dashboard
  -> use global selected period
  -> load Preparer dashboard data
```

## 4. Confirmed Shell Elements

The page uses the standard authenticated shell:

- Baxter logo.
- Previous-month default period selector.
- Notifications.
- To Do.
- User profile and role switching.
- Preparer-specific left navigation.
- Global footer.

## 5. Dashboard Content Policy

The supplied Preparer dashboard screenshot and HTML are the visual source of truth for layout, widget arrangement, headings, density, chart presentation, and available interactions.

Phase 1 should implement only the widgets visibly confirmed in those artifacts or explicitly approved later. The architecture must not invent names, calculations, charts, or navigation targets merely because the underlying reference framework supports additional widgets.

## 6. Operational Focus

Preparer data is restricted to work visible to or assigned to the current user and role. Dashboard links may route to:

- View Journal Progress.
- An assigned journal detail page.
- To Do items.
- A supported notification target.

Unsupported reference pages do not appear in the dashboard or navigation.

## 7. Journal Counts and Status Summaries

Where the supplied dashboard displays journal or work counts, the calculation must use the selected period and current user context.

Typical source constraints:

```text
assigned_user_id = current_user.user_id
role_code = PREPARER_RECONCILER
journal_period = selected_period
```

Do not expose another user's assigned journals.

## 8. Preparer Work States

The dashboard may summarize confirmed workflow states relevant to the Preparer:

- Pending Preparer.
- Rejected and returned to Preparer.
- Pending Approver, visible for View and Decertify.
- Closed, read-only where access is permitted.

Names and grouping in the UI must match the approved screenshots and workflow status catalog.

## 9. Dashboard-to-Workbench Navigation

Selecting a supported journal metric or work item opens View Journal Progress with period and optional filters applied.

Example:

```text
/dashboard
  -> click pending item
  -> /journals/progress?period=07/2026&status=PENDING_PREPARER
```

The destination revalidates access rather than trusting dashboard parameters.

## 10. Dashboard-to-Journal Navigation

Selecting an individual assigned task opens the journal using the action-resolution rule:

- `Cert` for actionable Preparer work.
- `View` after the journal has moved to another role bucket.
- `Edit` only for an authorized unassigned journal.

Inside journal detail:

- Pending Preparer or Rejected: show Certify.
- Pending Approver for the same Preparer: show View and Decertify.
- Closed: read only.

## 11. Data API

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=07/2026
```

The backend resolves the active role and returns only Preparer dashboard content.

A dedicated API may be used where separate caching or query behavior is required:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard/preparer?period=07/2026
```

Even with a role-specific URL, the role is validated from the session.

## 12. Empty State

When no work exists for the selected period, show a neutral message such as:

```text
No assigned journal work was found for the selected period.
```

Do not display zero-value charts that imply missing data unless the approved screen does so.

## 13. Error State

API failure response includes an enum-based error code and correlation ID. The widget or page shows a proper popup/error state. Raw exceptions must never appear.

Example message code:

```text
DASHBOARD_PREPARER_LOAD_FAILED
```

## 14. Audit

Viewing the Preparer dashboard does not create a journal audit event. Certify and Decertify actions occur only inside journal detail and always create audit records.

## 15. Permissions

Allowed:

- View authorized dashboard data.
- Open assigned or permitted journal records.
- Certify in a valid Preparer state.
- Decertify while pending Approver.

Denied:

- Approve.
- Reject as Approver.
- View System Admin configuration.
- View journals assigned to another user without explicit read permission.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Preparer role opens the correct dashboard.
- Selected period defaults to previous month.
- Data is scoped to the logged-in Preparer.
- Dashboard links open supported routes.
- No speculative widgets or pages are shown.
- Certify and Decertify are not executed directly from dashboard cards.
- Errors use the shared notification framework.


# Approver-Dashboard

# Approver Dashboard

## 1. Purpose

The Approver dashboard provides an approval-focused landing experience based on the supplied Approver dashboard artifacts. It contains two confirmed tabs: **My Work** and **Analytics**.

## 2. Role Context

- Internal role: `APPROVER`.
- External mapping: `BLAPPROVER`.
- The active-role token and server session must both identify the Approver role.

## 3. Entry Flow

```text
Login or role switch
  -> validate APPROVER assignment
  -> issue/update token
  -> navigate to /dashboard
  -> open default tab My Work
  -> load selected-period Approver data
```

## 4. Tabs

### 4.1 My Work

My Work is the default Approver tab. It shows work currently assigned to the logged-in Approver and routes to actionable journal detail.

Data scope:

```text
assigned_user_id = current_user.user_id
role_code = APPROVER
assignment_status is actionable
journal_period = selected_period
```

### 4.2 Analytics

Analytics is a confirmed tab. It presents only analytics supported by the supplied screenshots/HTML or later approved calculations.

Analytics requirements:

- Remain scoped to data the Approver is authorized to see.
- Use the selected period when the widget is period dependent.
- Never provide a bypass to journal assignment security.
- Use chart legends, labels, and tooltips consistent with the supplied dashboard style.

Do not invent analytical KPIs not visible or explicitly documented.

## 5. My Work Interactions

Selecting an assigned journal opens the actionable journal detail screen. In View Journal Progress, the row action may be displayed as `Cert`, but the Approver decisions inside the detail page are:

- Approve.
- Reject.

`Cert` is the grid navigation label, not an instruction to implement a separate Approver Certify transition.

## 6. Approval Behavior

### Approve

```text
PENDING_APPROVER -> CLOSED
```

The action:

- Validates ownership and status.
- Creates the certification/action record.
- Creates the audit record.
- Flushes all changes.
- Commits atomically.
- Shows a success popup.

### Reject

```text
PENDING_APPROVER -> REJECTED
```

The action:

- Requires configured reason and/or comments according to the approved detail screen.
- Returns the journal to the Preparer queue.
- Creates certification/rejection and audit records.
- Commits atomically.
- Shows a success popup.

## 7. Dashboard API

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY&tab=MY_WORK
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY&tab=ANALYTICS
```

Example My Work response content:

```json
{
  "active_role": "APPROVER",
  "period": "07/2026",
  "active_tab": "MY_WORK",
  "widgets": [],
  "work_items": []
}
```

## 8. To Do Integration

Approver To Do items show actionable assignments for the logged-in Approver. Selecting an item navigates to the supported journal detail page. The API revalidates assignment and status.

## 9. Period Behavior

Changing the global period reloads both My Work and period-dependent Analytics data. The active tab remains selected.

## 10. Empty States

My Work:

```text
No approval work was found for the selected period.
```

Analytics:

```text
No analytics data is available for the selected period.
```

Messages should use centralized info codes.

## 11. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
DASHBOARD_APPROVER_LOAD_FAILED
DASHBOARD_ANALYTICS_LOAD_FAILED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
```

The UI displays centralized notification components and preserves the last successfully loaded tab where practical.

## 12. Security

- The server derives role from the token/session.
- My Work includes only assigned Approver journals.
- Analytics applies the same authorization scope.
- A user who also owns the Preparer role cannot approve a journal prepared by that same user.
- Direct URL access to an unassigned journal returns 403.

## 13. Audit

Dashboard views do not create journal audit events. Approve and Reject always create audit events in the workflow transaction.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- My Work and Analytics tabs are visible.
- My Work is the default tab.
- Period defaults to previous month.
- Assigned work is scoped to the current Approver.
- `Cert` opens detail; detail shows Approve and Reject.
- Approve closes the journal.
- Reject returns the journal to Preparer.
- No reviewer or admin actions appear.


# Reviewer-Dashboard

# Reviewer Dashboard

## 1. Purpose

This document defines only the Reviewer dashboard behavior confirmed by the supplied Reviewer HTML and screenshots. The Reviewer business workflow has not been provided and must not be inferred.

## 2. Confirmed Facts

- Reviewer is a selectable user role.
- Selecting Reviewer changes the left navigation and dashboard context.
- Reviewer dashboard contains two tabs:
  - My Work.
  - Analytics.
- The dashboard uses the same shared widget and tab framework as the other role dashboards.

## 3. Explicitly Undefined

Phase 1 architecture does not define Reviewer:

- Certify.
- Approve.
- Reject.
- Return.
- Decertify.
- Reassign.
- Close.
- Journal ownership transition.

No Reviewer workflow API, button, status transition, or audit action should be implemented without a later requirement.

## 4. Dashboard Entry

```text
User selects Reviewer role
  -> backend validates REVIEWER role assignment
  -> backend issues a new Reviewer-role token
  -> frontend rebuilds Reviewer navigation
  -> navigate to /dashboard
  -> load My Work tab
```

## 5. My Work Tab

The My Work tab is confirmed as a dashboard tab, but the meaning of Reviewer work is not sufficiently defined.

Implementation rule:

- Render only work-list elements and fields clearly present in the supplied Reviewer dashboard artifacts.
- Do not attach unconfirmed journal actions.
- If an item points to a route without an approved page or workflow, route to the standard 404 page rather than inventing functionality.
- If only dashboard visuals are required for the pilot, use approved seed data that reflects the supplied screen without implying an operational workflow.

## 6. Analytics Tab

The Analytics tab is confirmed, but calculations must be based on visible/approved dashboard content only.

- Do not expose journals outside Reviewer authorization.
- Do not create analytical drill-down routes unless confirmed.
- Unsupported drill-downs show 404.
- Chart data and labels must match the supplied screenshot.

## 7. Period Behavior

Reviewer dashboard uses the global selected period where the supplied dashboard indicates period-bound content. Default is previous month.

## 8. Navigation

The Reviewer left menu must reproduce only confirmed supported items. Menu labels visible in the screenshot may be displayed only when the corresponding target page is implemented or intentionally routes to the standard 404 policy accepted for the pilot.

Preferred Phase 1 behavior is to omit unsupported menu items rather than advertise unavailable pages.

## 9. API Design

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY&tab=MY_WORK
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY&tab=ANALYTICS
```

The server returns Reviewer dashboard data only when the active role is Reviewer.

No Reviewer journal-action endpoints are introduced by this dashboard document.

## 10. Empty and Information States

Examples:

```text
No Reviewer work is available for the selected period.
No Reviewer analytics data is available for the selected period.
```

Use centralized message codes rather than hardcoded component text.

## 11. Error Handling

Possible errors:

```text
DASHBOARD_REVIEWER_LOAD_FAILED
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED
```

Raw errors must never appear in the UI.

## 12. Security

- Reviewer role must be assigned and active.
- Dashboard data is role scoped.
- Reviewer role does not inherit Approver or Preparer actions.
- A user with multiple roles must explicitly switch roles to change context.

## 13. Audit

Viewing Reviewer dashboard content does not create journal audit records. If Reviewer actions are later defined, corresponding audit events must be designed at that time.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Reviewer role opens the Reviewer dashboard.
- My Work and Analytics tabs are available.
- My Work is the default tab unless the approved screenshot shows otherwise.
- No unconfirmed Reviewer workflow action is displayed.
- Unsupported routes render 404.
- Dashboard errors use the shared notification framework.


# System-Admin-Dashboard

# System Admin Dashboard

## 1. Purpose

The System Admin dashboard provides access only to the System Admin capabilities confirmed by the supplied screenshots and requirements. No System Admin HTML dashboard was supplied, so the page must remain intentionally simple and must not invent analytics or operational widgets.

## 2. Confirmed Capabilities

- Dependent Validation.
- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Cross-currency view.
- Prior 12 periods view.

## 3. Dependent Validation Access

The current requirement states that System Admin can only view dependent validation.

Therefore Phase 1 behavior is:

- Display dependent mapping data.
- First logical column represents the Preparer identifier coming from JDE.
- Display the dependent Approver mapping information shown in the approved screen.
- Do not provide add/edit/delete/upload controls unless a later requirement explicitly authorizes them.

## 4. Dashboard Design

Because no dashboard HTML exists, use a minimal role landing page aligned with the common application shell.

Recommended content:

- A page heading only if shown by the approved design language.
- Navigation cards or links for the three confirmed areas.
- Optional informational text explaining the role's available functions.

Do not create unsupported System Admin metrics, charts, task summaries, or widget personalization.

## 5. Navigation Targets

```text
Dependent Validation      -> /admin/dependent-validation
Currency Settings         -> /admin/currency/settings
Currency Rates Information -> /admin/currency/rates
```

## 6. Currency Navigation

The currency screens follow the supplied left-panel arrangement. The System Admin dashboard routes to the currency area, after which the currency left panel identifies the confirmed screens.

## 7. Role Restrictions

System Admin does not automatically receive:

- Journal Certify.
- Journal Approve.
- Journal Reject.
- Journal Decertify.
- Local Admin file upload/parse.

Those capabilities require separate assigned roles and explicit active-role switching.

## 8. Dashboard API

The System Admin landing page may be static except for role validation. If counts or configuration summaries are later approved, use:

```http
GET /api/v1/dashboard?period=MM/YYYY
```

For Phase 1, period may be displayed globally but not required for non-period System Admin navigation.

## 9. Error Handling

- Unauthorized role: 403 and Access Denied.
- Unsupported admin route: 404.
- Failed configuration retrieval: user-friendly error popup with correlation ID.

## 10. Audit

Viewing the System Admin dashboard does not create journal audit events. Viewing dependent validation may be application logged. Any future configuration change must create an appropriate audit record.

## 11. Acceptance Criteria

- Only confirmed System Admin functions appear.
- Dependent Validation is view-only.
- Currency Settings and Currency Rates Information are reachable.
- No speculative System Admin widgets are shown.
- Journal workflow actions are denied under System Admin role.
- Unsupported admin pages return 404.


# ToDo-Module

# To Do Module

## 1. Purpose

The To Do module shows pending actions or assigned tasks for the authenticated user in the active role. It is accessed from the global header and uses the supplied To Do screenshot as the presentation reference.

## 2. Scope

Confirmed behavior:

- Header To Do control.
- Count or indicator of pending items.
- List of pending actions assigned to the current user.
- Role-sensitive content.
- Navigation to a supported actionable page.

The module must not become a generalized task-management platform in Phase 1.

## 3. Confirmed Display Fields

The screenshot indicates fields such as:

- Product or functional area.
- Key or work-item identifier.
- Due information.
- Due date.
- Role.
- Status.

Final labels, ordering, widths, and values follow the supplied screen.

## 4. Data Sources

For journal work, To Do items can be derived from active journal assignments rather than requiring a separate editable task framework.

Example derivation:

```text
journal_assignment.assigned_user_id = current_user
journal_assignment.role_code = active_role
journal_assignment.assignment_status = actionable
journal_header.status = role-actionable status
```

If non-journal tasks are shown in the supplied pilot data, introduce a task read model or table only when required by those confirmed fields.

## 5. Role Behavior

### Preparer

May see:

- Journals pending Preparer certification.
- Rejected journals returned for review and re-certification.

### Approver

May see:

- Journals pending Approver decision.

### Reviewer

Display only items supported by supplied Reviewer artifacts. Do not attach undefined reviewer actions.

### System Admin

May see confirmed administrative information only if supplied. Do not invent currency or dependency tasks.

### Local Admin

May see uploaded files requiring Parse or parse failures only if supported by the approved To Do design.

## 6. Navigation Rule

Selecting an item:

1. Closes the To Do popup/drawer.
2. Navigates to the item's supported route.
3. Revalidates authorization through the destination API.

Examples:

```text
Preparer journal -> /journals/<journalId>
Approver journal -> /journals/<journalId>
Parse item       -> /admin/journal-files
```

If the item points to an unsupported route, the application displays 404.

## 7. API

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/todo?period=MM/YYYY
```

The backend derives user and active role from the bearer token/session.

Example response:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "count": 2,
    "items": [
      {
        "item_id": "<id>",
        "item_type": "JOURNAL_APPROVAL",
        "key": "<journal-id>",
        "due_text": "Due",
        "due_date": "2026-07-31",
        "role": "APPROVER",
        "status": "PENDING_APPROVER",
        "target_route": "/journals/<journal-id>"
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 8. Sorting

Recommended order:

1. Overdue items.
2. Earliest due date.
3. Most recently assigned item.

If the supplied screenshot indicates another ordering, follow the screenshot.

## 9. Period Behavior

The To Do list may use the global selected period when the work is period-specific. The header count must match the same filter applied to the displayed list.

## 10. Empty State

```text
No pending items are assigned to you for the selected period.
```

Use an `INFO` message severity, not an error.

## 11. Error Handling

Example code:

```text
TODO_LIST_LOAD_FAILED
```

The module shows a reusable error state or popup. Raw backend errors are not displayed.

## 12. Security

- Require bearer token and active session.
- Return only current-user items for the active role.
- Ignore or reject arbitrary user ID or role parameters from the client.
- Destination route repeats authorization checks.

## 13. Audit

Opening or viewing To Do does not create a journal audit event. Selecting an item may be application logged for troubleshooting but does not change workflow state.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Header shows correct To Do count.
- List contains only active-user, active-role items.
- Fields align with the supplied screenshot.
- Selecting a supported item navigates correctly.
- Reviewer actions are not inferred.
- Empty and error states are user friendly.


# Notifications

# Notifications

## 1. Purpose

The Notifications module provides application information and workflow alerts through the global header. It is separate from transient success/error popups, although both use centralized message definitions and severity enums.

## 2. Notification Categories

Confirmed or supportable categories include:

- Workflow assignment notification.
- Journal rejected and returned to Preparer.
- Journal moved to Approver.
- Journal closed after approval.
- Journal decertified and pulled back.
- Journal-file upload completed.
- Parse completed successfully.
- Parse failed with errors.
- Session-expiry information.
- Application information message.

Do not create email, SMS, or push-notification behavior in Phase 1 unless explicitly approved.

## 3. Difference Between Header Notifications and Popups

### Header Notifications

Persistent or retrievable events that remain visible until read/dismissed according to policy.

### Popup/Toast/Dialog Messages

Immediate feedback for the current action, such as:

- Certified successfully.
- Approval failed.
- Parse failed.

A workflow action may create both an audit event and a header notification, while the current user also receives an immediate success popup.

## 4. Header Interaction

- Display unread count.
- Open a compact list, popup, or drawer consistent with the supplied shell.
- Show newest events first.
- Allow supported notification selection.
- Mark as read when opened or explicitly selected, based on final UX behavior.
- Provide a clear empty state.

## 5. Notification Data Model

If persistence is required, recommended fields are:

```text
notification_id
user_id
role_code
notification_type
message_code
message_text
severity
related_entity_type
related_entity_id
target_route
is_read
created_at
read_at
expires_at
```

Message text may be generated from a centralized catalog using message code and metadata.

## 6. Enum Model

```text
NotificationSeverity:
SUCCESS
ERROR
WARNING
INFO

NotificationType:
WORKFLOW_ASSIGNMENT
JOURNAL_REJECTED
JOURNAL_APPROVED
JOURNAL_DECERTIFIED
FILE_UPLOAD_COMPLETED
FILE_PARSE_COMPLETED
FILE_PARSE_FAILED
SESSION_EXPIRING
APPLICATION_INFO
```

Use enums rather than ad hoc strings.

## 7. API

Recommended endpoints:

```http
GET  /api/v1/notifications
POST /api/v1/notifications/{notificationId}/read
POST /api/v1/notifications/read-all
```

The server determines the current user from the token. The client must not request another user's notifications.

Example response:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "unread_count": 1,
    "items": [
      {
        "notification_id": "<id>",
        "notification_type": "JOURNAL_REJECTED",
        "message_code": "JOURNAL_REJECTED_NOTIFICATION",
        "message": "A journal was rejected and returned for review.",
        "severity": "WARNING",
        "target_route": "/journals/<journal-id>",
        "is_read": false,
        "created_at": "2026-08-20T10:00:00Z"
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 8. Workflow Integration

Notifications are created after the corresponding business transaction succeeds. Avoid notifying users about a workflow transition that later rolls back.

Recommended sequence:

```text
Business updates
  -> audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
  -> publish/create post-commit notification
```

If notification persistence is part of the same required transaction, its failure policy must be explicitly documented. Journal audit remains mandatory; notification delivery should not normally reverse a valid financial workflow action.

## 9. Navigation

Selecting a notification navigates only to a supported route. The destination page performs independent authorization.

- Unauthorized known route: Access Denied.
- Unknown/unsupported route: 404.
- Deleted/unavailable related record: user-friendly Not Found message.

## 10. Empty State

```text
You have no notifications.
```

## 11. Error Handling

Example codes:

```text
NOTIFICATION_LIST_LOAD_FAILED
NOTIFICATION_MARK_READ_FAILED
```

The header remains usable even if notification retrieval fails. Show a centralized error message with correlation ID.

## 12. Accessibility

- Notification control has an accessible name.
- Unread count is announced meaningfully.
- Drawer/menu supports keyboard navigation.
- Severity is not represented by color alone.
- Time values have accessible labels.

## 13. Security and Privacy

- Notifications are user scoped.
- Message text must not reveal journal information to unauthorized roles.
- Target routes are validated server-side.
- Sensitive data is not embedded in client-visible notification metadata unless required.

## 14. Audit and Logging

A header notification is not a substitute for the journal audit trail. The system logs notification generation and delivery failures using correlation IDs. Reading a notification need not create a journal audit event.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Unread count and list are consistent.
- Notifications are scoped to the authenticated user.
- Supported workflow events create appropriate notifications.
- Immediate action feedback uses shared popup/toast/dialog components.
- Raw errors are never shown.
- Selecting a notification cannot bypass authorization.


# View-Journal-Progress

# View Journal Progress

## 1. Purpose

View Journal Progress is the primary journal operations workbench. It provides period-based visibility across journal records and presents the correct row-level navigation action according to assignment ownership, active role, and workflow status.

This page is not a separate approval queue or generic task list. Preparer and Approver users use the same journal progress workbench with role-aware data and actions.

## 2. Route

```text
/journals/progress
```

Recommended query parameters:

```text
period=MM/YYYY
status=<status-code>
```

The selected period comes from the global application-shell period context.

## 3. Authorization

The page requires:

- Valid JWT bearer token.
- Active server-side session.
- Active role with journal-progress access.
- Dataset restricted according to the current user, active role, assignments, and permitted read-only visibility.

The client must not send a user ID to expand scope. The backend derives the user and active role from the validated request context.

## 4. Data Loading

Recommended endpoint:

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/progress?period=07/2026
```

The backend loads the selected-period dataset. The frontend may apply column-level filtering, sorting, and pagination locally after the period dataset is loaded, provided the data volume remains acceptable.

Processing order:

```text
Backend period and authorization filtering
  -> frontend column filters
  -> frontend sorting
  -> frontend pagination
```

If volume later exceeds the client threshold, the same screen can move to server-side filtering and pagination without changing the page's business behavior.

## 5. Grid Behavior

The grid is horizontally scrollable and supports the supplied View Journal Progress column set.

Required behaviors:

- Sticky or pinned high-value columns where supported by the approved screen.
- Resizable columns.
- Sortable columns.
- Column visibility control if shown in the reference page.
- Pagination.
- Empty state.
- Loading state.
- Error state.
- Action column.
- Status, comment, and document indicators.
- Numeric alignment for monetary values.
- Date formatting consistent with source and display requirements.

AG Grid is recommended because of the large column count and enterprise grid interaction requirements.

## 6. Filter Row

Each filterable column has a filter textbox directly below that column's header.

Rules:

- Filter input width equals the current column width.
- Filters are aligned with their columns.
- Filtering starts only after more than two characters are entered.
- Clearing the input removes the filter.
- Multiple filters combine using logical AND.
- Filtering occurs before pagination.
- Header controls such as Action, Comment, and Documents may use specialized filtering instead of free text if required by the approved screen.

## 7. Default Period

The page initially uses the previous calendar month.

Example:

```text
Current month: August 2026
Default selected period: 07/2026
```

Changing the global period reloads the progress dataset and resets the page number to the first page. Column visibility and column widths may remain unchanged during the same session.

## 8. Action Column

The Action column displays exactly one navigation link based on the centralized action-resolution service:

- `Edit` when the journal is unassigned and the current user is authorized to edit or assign it.
- `Cert` when the journal is in an actionable Preparer or Approver bucket for the current user.
- `View` when the journal has moved to another role bucket, is closed, or is otherwise read-only for the current role.

The grid does not directly execute Certify, Approve, Reject, or Decertify. Those actions exist inside the journal detail page.

## 9. Row Navigation

### Edit

```text
Edit -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=edit
```

The detail API independently validates that the journal is unassigned and the current role may edit it.

### Cert

```text
Cert -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=action
```

The detail page resolves the valid button set:

- Preparer: Certify.
- Approver: Approve and Reject.

### View

```text
View -> /journals/<journalId>?mode=view
```

The detail page is read-only except for the specific Preparer Decertify case while the journal is pending Approver.

## 10. Status and Indicators

The progress grid presents workflow and supporting-data indicators, including:

- Status.
- Comment availability.
- Document availability.
- Final Certification State.
- Completed flag.
- Posting Status.
- Header Status display.

Indicator values are view-model fields derived by the backend. The frontend must not infer financial status from unrelated fields.

## 11. Currency and Totals

The grid displays source and USD values where available:

- Total Debit.
- Total Credit.
- Conversion rate to USD.
- Total Debit USD.
- Total Credit USD.

Currency values use decimal-safe backend calculations and locale-consistent display formatting. The UI does not recalculate authoritative financial totals.

## 12. API Response

Example shape:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "period": "07/2026",
    "rows": [
      {
        "journal_id": "JRN001",
        "status": "PENDING_PREPARER",
        "action": "CERT",
        "action_label": "Cert",
        "is_read_only": false
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

The backend returns the resolved action code and label. The UI does not duplicate the workflow rules in multiple grid components.

## 13. Empty State

```text
No journals were found for the selected period and filters.
```

If no period data exists before local filters are applied, use an informational empty state. Empty results are not errors.

## 14. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_PROGRESS_LOAD_FAILED
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
SESSION_EXPIRED
```

The shared notification module presents a proper popup or error state. Raw exceptions and stack traces are prohibited.

## 15. Accessibility

- Grid supports keyboard navigation.
- Action links have descriptive accessible labels including the journal identifier.
- Filter inputs have accessible names based on their column.
- Status icons include text or accessible labels.
- Horizontal scrolling does not prevent keyboard access.

## 16. Performance

- Retrieve only fields needed by the grid.
- Use indexed period, status, assignment-user, and journal identifiers.
- Avoid loading document binary content in the progress response.
- Return document and comment counts/flags instead.
- Use memoized column definitions.
- Debounce filter input after the minimum character rule.

## 17. Audit

Viewing, filtering, sorting, or paging does not create journal audit events. Opening a row may be application logged for diagnostics but does not change workflow status.

## 18. Acceptance Criteria

- Previous month is selected by default.
- Complete approved column set is available.
- Filter textboxes align below their columns.
- Filtering starts after more than two characters.
- Edit, Cert, and View resolve correctly.
- Detail actions do not execute from the grid.
- Unauthorized journals cannot be opened through URL manipulation.
- Grid errors use centralized messages and correlation IDs.


# View-Journal-Progress-Column-Matrix

# View Journal Progress Column Matrix

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the complete business column inventory supplied for the View Journal Progress grid. It distinguishes displayed operational fields, JDE/source fields, derived fields, indicators, financial values, and duplicate business labels.

## 2. Implementation Rule

The source CSV contains duplicate labels such as `Team` and `Preparer`. The API and frontend must use unique technical field names while preserving the approved user-facing labels.

Example:

```text
team_assignment       -> display label Team
team_source           -> display label Team
preparer_display_name -> display label Preparer
preparer_source_id    -> display label Preparer
```

Duplicate labels must not produce duplicate JSON keys.

## 3. Column Matrix

### 3.1 Workflow and Operational Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Batch ID | `batch_id` | Journal/header or source record | Text | Yes | Yes | Business batch identifier shown by the progress view |
| Status | `status_display` | Workflow status mapping | Enum/Text | Yes | Yes | User-facing label derived from internal status |
| Comment | `has_comments` | Journal comment count | Boolean/Icon | Yes | Yes | Display indicator, do not load comment text in grid |
| Documents | `has_documents` | Journal document count | Boolean/Icon | Yes | Yes | Display indicator, do not load binary content |
| ID | `journal_display_id` | Journal business ID | Text/Number | Yes | Yes | Distinct from internal database primary key |
| Template | `template_name` | Journal header | Text | Yes | Yes | Default may be Generic |
| Team | `team_assignment` | Assignment/team mapping | Text | Yes | Yes | First Team occurrence |
| Preparer | `preparer_display_name` | Assigned Preparer user | Text | Yes | Yes | User-friendly name |
| Approver | `approver_display_name` | Assigned Approver user | Text | Yes | Yes | May show Unassigned |
| Reviewer | `reviewer_display_name` | Reviewer assignment if present | Text | Yes | Yes | Reviewer workflow remains undefined |
| Final Certification State | `final_certification_state` | Workflow/certification view model | Text | Yes | Yes | Display-only state summary |
| Completed | `is_completed` | Derived from terminal state | Boolean | Yes | Yes | Do not infer solely in UI |
| Posting Status | `posting_status` | Journal header/source | Text | Yes | Yes | Display status from supplied data |
| Document ID | `source_document_id` | Source or journal header | Text | Yes | Yes | Not the supporting document table ID |
| Journal Master | `journal_master` | Source/progress data | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve source semantics |
| Automated Journals | `automated_journal_flag` | Source/progress data | Boolean/Text | Yes | Yes | Display per approved screen |
| Matched Variance | `matched_variance` | Source/derived | Decimal/Text | Yes | Yes | Confirm formatting from source data |

### 3.2 Financial Summary Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Total Debit | `total_debit` | Aggregated journal lines or source total | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| Total Credit | `total_credit` | Aggregated journal lines or source total | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| BL Currency Rate to USD | `currency_rate_to_usd` | Currency-rate lookup | Decimal | Yes | Yes | UI label may be revised to remove external product terminology in final delivery |
| Total Debit - USD | `total_debit_usd` | Authoritative backend calculation | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |
| Total Credit - USD | `total_credit_usd` | Authoritative backend calculation | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Right aligned |

### 3.3 Journal Source and Batch Columns

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Team | `team_source` | Source/JDE | Text | Yes | Yes | Second Team occurrence |
| Company Code | `company_code` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve leading zeros |

| Document Type | `document_type` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve source code |
| Document Number | `document_number` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Preserve leading zeros |

| Explanation 1 | `explanation_1` | JDE source | Text | Yes | Yes | Truncate in grid with tooltip if needed |
| Total Credit (Auto-populated) | `total_credit_auto` | Backend/source derived | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Authoritative value |
| Total Debit (Auto-populated) | `total_debit_auto` | Backend/source derived | Decimal | Yes | Yes | Authoritative value |

| Header Status (Display) | `header_status_display` | Source/derived | Text | Yes | Yes | Example validation/status message |

## 4. Additional Action Column

The UI also requires the role-resolved `Action` column even though it is not listed as a source-data field in the CSV.

| Display Column | Technical Field | Source / Derivation | Data Type | Filter | Sort | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Action | `resolved_action` | Backend action resolver | Enum/Link | Optional | Optional | Edit, Cert, or View |

## 5. Column Groups

Recommended column selector groupings:

```text
Workflow
Assignments
Financial Summary
Source Journal
Batch and Ledger
Invoice
Intercompany
Regional
System/Derived
```

Grouping improves usability without removing any approved columns.

## 6. Data Formatting

- IDs and codes are strings to preserve leading zeros.
- Monetary values use decimal types, not binary floating point.
- Rates use sufficient decimal scale.
- Dates are normalized in API responses and formatted for display.
- Boolean indicators use approved labels/icons and accessible text.
- Empty source values display blank, not misleading zero, unless zero is a valid numeric value.

## 7. Branding Cleanup

Source labels containing an external product name must be replaced with neutral, approved labels in the delivered UI while retaining neutral technical fields such as `external_journal_id` and `currency_rate_to_usd`.

## 8. Acceptance Criteria

- Every supplied CSV column has a unique technical field.
- Duplicate display labels do not create duplicate JSON keys.
- Codes preserve leading zeros.
- Numeric and date fields sort correctly.
- Action is derived, not read from source data.
- The UI can expose the full column inventory through horizontal scrolling and/or a column selector.


# Action-Resolution-Rules

# Action Resolution Rules

## 1. Purpose

The action resolver determines the link shown in the View Journal Progress Action column and the valid workflow buttons shown inside Journal Detail.

The rule is centralized in backend code. It must not be duplicated independently across pages.

## 2. Action Types

```text
EDIT
CERT
VIEW
```

User-facing labels:

```text
EDIT -> Edit
CERT -> Cert
VIEW -> View
```

## 3. Inputs

The resolver evaluates:

- Authenticated user ID.
- Active role.
- User's active role assignments.
- Journal status.
- Journal assignments.
- Assignment status.
- Whether the journal is unassigned.
- Whether the current user owns the actionable assignment.
- Whether the journal has moved to another role bucket.
- Whether the journal is closed.
- Preparer/Approver same-user restriction.

## 4. Edit Rule

Return `EDIT` when:

- Journal is unassigned.
- Current active role is authorized to edit/assign the unassigned journal.
- Journal is not closed or otherwise locked.

`Edit` opens the journal in assignment/edit mode. Edit authorization must be confirmed again by the Journal Detail API.

## 5. Cert Rule

Return `CERT` when:

- Current user owns the actionable assignment.
- Active role is Preparer or Approver.
- Journal is in an actionable status for that role.

Examples:

```text
Assigned Preparer + PENDING_PREPARER -> Cert
Assigned Preparer + REJECTED -> Cert
Assigned Approver + PENDING_APPROVER -> Cert
```

`Cert` is a navigation label. Inside Journal Detail:

- Preparer receives `Certify`.
- Approver receives `Approve` and `Reject`.

## 6. View Rule

Return `VIEW` when:

- Journal belongs to another role bucket.
- Current user does not own the actionable assignment but has permitted read access.
- Journal is closed.
- Current role has historical/read-only visibility.
- An assigned Preparer views a journal currently pending Approver.

Special case:

```text
Preparer + PENDING_APPROVER -> View detail with Decertify available
```

The row link remains `View`, because the primary ownership bucket is Approver, while the detail page may expose the controlled Decertify action to the original Preparer.

## 7. Priority

Recommended resolution order:

```text
1. Validate page visibility
2. If unassigned and editable -> EDIT
3. If current user owns actionable Preparer/Approver assignment -> CERT
4. If read access exists -> VIEW
5. Otherwise -> no action / forbidden
```

## 8. Detail Button Resolution

Separate resolver output:

```text
can_edit
can_certify
can_approve
can_reject
can_decertify
is_read_only
```

Example:

```json
{
  "grid_action": "VIEW",
  "can_certify": false,
  "can_approve": false,
  "can_reject": false,
  "can_decertify": true,
  "is_read_only": false
}
```

## 9. Same-User Restriction

The same user cannot be both Preparer and Approver for the same journal. If invalid data exists, the resolver must not expose Approve. The backend returns a business-rule error and logs the issue.

## 10. Reviewer

Reviewer action resolution is undefined. Do not return CERT or workflow buttons for Reviewer until the business flow is provided.

## 11. System Admin and Local Admin

Admin roles do not receive workflow actions solely because of admin status. Read-only access, if enabled, returns View.

## 12. Backend Interface

```text
IJournalActionResolver
JournalActionResolverImpl
```

Recommended method contract:

```text
resolve(user_context, journal, assignments) -> JournalActionAccessDto
```

## 13. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_ACTION_NOT_ALLOWED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
```

## 14. Audit

Action resolution itself does not create audit events. Successful workflow actions do.

## 15. Acceptance Tests

- Unassigned authorized journal returns Edit.
- Assigned Preparer journal returns Cert.
- Assigned Approver journal returns Cert.
- Journal in another role bucket returns View.
- Preparer viewing pending Approver journal receives View plus Decertify in detail.
- Closed journal returns View.
- Reviewer receives no invented workflow action.
- Unauthorized user receives no action and cannot open the journal.


# Journal-Detail

# Journal Detail

## 1. Purpose

Journal Detail displays all information required to review a journal and perform authorized workflow actions. The page is opened from View Journal Progress, To Do, or a supported notification.

## 2. Route

```text
/journals/<journalId>
```

Optional UI mode query:

```text
mode=edit
action
view
```

The backend remains authoritative and does not trust the requested mode.

## 3. Page Sections

The detail page includes approved sections such as:

- Journal summary/header.
- Journal line grid.
- Documents.
- Comments.
- Hyperlinks where included in the approved page.
- Certification/action panel.
- Audit Trail or Certification Details.

Sections may be tabs, panels, or a two-column layout according to the supplied screenshots.

## 4. Header Information

Display authoritative journal values, including identifiers, status, period, source details, assignments, totals, currency, and posting/header status where applicable.

## 5. Journal Lines

The line grid uses journal-line data from the parsed source file. It is read-only for workflow review unless a confirmed edit requirement exists.

- Preserve source row number.
- Use decimal-safe amount values.
- Show source and USD amount where available.
- Support sorting and filtering if shown.
- Do not change parsed source data during certification.

## 6. Role-Aware Actions

### Preparer

Valid actions by status:

```text
PENDING_PREPARER -> Certify
REJECTED -> Certify
PENDING_APPROVER -> Decertify
CLOSED -> none, View only
```

### Approver

```text
PENDING_APPROVER -> Approve, Reject
Other states -> View only
```

### Reviewer

No workflow actions are defined.

### Admin

No workflow actions solely due to admin role.

## 7. Certification Panel

The panel shows the applicable certification statement from configuration, comments/reason fields when required, and authorized action buttons.

Buttons are disabled while an API call is in progress.

## 8. Data API

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}
```

Response includes:

```text
journal header
lines or line endpoint reference
assignments
resolved access/actions
document summary
comment summary
certification history
audit summary
```

Large line data may use:

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/lines
```

## 9. Read-Only Mode

Read-only mode:

- Disables editable controls.
- Hides unauthorized workflow buttons.
- Allows permitted document download/preview.
- Allows audit viewing.
- Does not rely on disabled HTML controls as the only security layer.

## 10. Success Handling

After a successful workflow action:

- Show centralized success popup/toast.
- Refresh journal status and resolved access.
- Refresh Audit Trail.
- Update or invalidate View Journal Progress cache.
- Navigate only when the approved workflow calls for it.

## 11. Error Handling

Possible codes:

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

Display user-friendly messages with correlation ID. No raw exception is rendered.

## 12. Concurrency

Workflow commands must lock or version-check the journal before state transition. If another user has already acted, return a conflict/business error and refresh the page.

Recommended response status:

```text
409 Conflict
```

## 13. Accessibility

- Page heading identifies the journal.
- Tabs/panels are keyboard accessible.
- Action buttons have clear labels.
- Confirmation dialogs manage focus.
- Validation messages are associated with fields.
- Status is conveyed through text and not color alone.

## 14. Audit

View does not create a journal audit event. Certify, Approve, Reject, Decertify, document upload, comment addition, and hyperlink addition create the applicable audit records.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Detail displays authoritative journal data.
- Buttons reflect active role, ownership, and status.
- Preparer can Certify or Decertify only in valid states.
- Approver can Approve or Reject only in valid state.
- Reviewer actions are absent.
- Unauthorized URL access is denied.
- Workflow updates and audit records are atomic.


# Documents-Module

# Documents Module

## 1. Purpose

The Documents module manages supporting evidence attached to a journal and makes authorized documents available for workflow review.

## 2. Capabilities

- Upload supporting document.
- List documents.
- Download document.
- Preview supported document types where available.
- Display metadata.
- Mark inactive/remove only if explicitly approved.

## 3. Authorization

Document access requires:

- Valid bearer token and active session.
- Access to the parent journal.
- Current role permission for the requested operation.

A document ID alone never grants access.

## 4. Upload Flow

```text
Select file
  -> client validates basic constraints
  -> upload API validates journal access
  -> validate name, size, MIME type and extension
  -> calculate hash
  -> persist metadata and content
  -> create audit record
  -> flush and commit
  -> return success message
```

## 5. Storage

Current schema stores file content in PostgreSQL `BYTEA`. The repository/service boundary must allow future replacement with Amazon S3 without changing journal workflow code.

## 6. Metadata

Required fields:

```text
document_id
journal_id
original_file_name
file_extension
mime_type
file_size_bytes
file_hash
description
uploaded_by
uploaded_role
uploaded_at
status
```

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents/{documentId}/download
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/documents/{documentId}/preview
```

## 8. Validation

- Empty file rejected.
- File size must be within configured limit.
- Unsupported extension/MIME combination rejected.
- File name sanitized for display and response headers.
- Hash may be used for duplicate detection.
- Journal must exist and be accessible.
- Upload must be allowed in the current state/role.

## 9. UI

Document panel shows:

- File name.
- Description.
- Size.
- Uploaded by.
- Uploaded role.
- Uploaded time.
- Download icon.
- Preview icon only when supported.

View Journal Progress receives only document count/indicator.

## 10. Audit Events

```text
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED, optional application/security log
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_PREVIEWED, optional application/security log
```

Upload audit is part of the business transaction.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
DOCUMENT_UPLOAD_FAILED
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_TYPE_NOT_SUPPORTED
DOCUMENT_SIZE_EXCEEDED
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
DOCUMENT_PREVIEW_NOT_AVAILABLE
```

## 12. Security

- Do not expose storage paths.
- Use safe content-disposition headers.
- Prevent path traversal.
- Validate MIME type.
- Consider malware scanning before production.
- Log download/preview failures with correlation ID.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Authorized user can upload permitted document.
- Document list refreshes after commit.
- Approver can review permitted evidence.
- Unauthorized document access returns 403/404 appropriately.
- Upload creates audit event.
- Raw storage details are never exposed.


# Comments-Module

# Comments Module

## 1. Purpose

The Comments module records journal-specific collaboration and workflow context. Comments are visible to authorized users across workflow stages.

## 2. Capabilities

- Add comment.
- List comments chronologically or newest first according to approved UI.
- Display author, active role, and timestamp.
- Retain prior comments after status transitions.

Editing or deleting posted comments is not included unless explicitly approved.

## 3. Authorization

- User must have access to the journal.
- Active role is recorded with the comment.
- Read-only journal access may allow comment reading but not necessarily comment creation.
- Reviewer comment behavior remains undefined unless the approved screen expressly shows it.

## 4. Data Model

```text
comment_id
journal_id
comment_text
role_code
created_by
created_at
updated_at
```

## 5. Add Flow

```text
Enter comment
  -> validate non-empty and length
  -> validate journal access
  -> save comment
  -> create audit event
  -> flush and commit
  -> show success message
```

## 6. API

```http
GET  /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/comments
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/comments
```

Request:

```json
{
  "comment_text": "<comment>"
}
```

The backend derives author and active role from request context.

## 7. Rejection Comments

Approver rejection uses the workflow action contract and records rejection reason/comments in the certification/action record. It may also create a visible journal comment if the business design requires that behavior. Do not create duplicate comments unless explicitly intended.

## 8. Decertification Comments

Decertification requires the configured reason/comment policy. The workflow action stores this in certification/audit metadata. A separate comment row is optional only when approved.

## 9. UI

- Multiline comment input.
- Character limit/help text.
- Add button.
- Busy state during save.
- Comment history with author, role, and timestamp.
- Safe text rendering.
- No raw HTML execution.

## 10. Audit Event

```text
JOURNAL_COMMENT_ADDED
```

The audit event includes comment ID and metadata but should not duplicate sensitive text unnecessarily.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
COMMENT_REQUIRED
COMMENT_TOO_LONG
COMMENT_ADD_FAILED
COMMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 12. Security

- Escape/render as text.
- Apply length limit.
- Prevent cross-site scripting.
- Do not trust client-supplied author or role.
- Preserve immutable authorship.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Authorized user can add a valid comment.
- Comment persists with user and active role.
- Comment is visible to authorized subsequent roles.
- Audit event is created.
- Empty/oversized comments show proper field errors.
- Rejection workflow does not create accidental duplicate messages.


# Audit-Trail

# Audit Trail

## 1. Purpose

The Audit Trail provides immutable traceability for journal workflow and supporting actions. Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify must always be recorded.

## 2. Mandatory Workflow Events

```text
PREPARER_CERTIFIED
APPROVER_APPROVED
APPROVER_REJECTED
PREPARER_DECERTIFIED
```

Additional journal events:

```text
JOURNAL_FILE_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_PARSE_STARTED
JOURNAL_PARSE_SUCCEEDED
JOURNAL_PARSE_FAILED
JOURNAL_DOCUMENT_UPLOADED
JOURNAL_COMMENT_ADDED
JOURNAL_HYPERLINK_ADDED
```

## 3. Required Audit Fields

```text
audit_id
journal_id
file_load_id
action_type
old_status
new_status
performed_by
performed_role
message
metadata_json
created_at
```

## 4. Audit Questions

Every workflow event must answer:

- Who performed the action?
- Which active role was used?
- Which journal was affected?
- What action occurred?
- What was the previous status?
- What is the new status?
- When did it occur?
- What reason/comment metadata was supplied?

## 5. Atomicity Rule

```text
No workflow action is complete unless the audit record is successfully written.
```

Required transaction sequence:

```text
Lock/load journal
  -> validate ownership and transition
  -> update journal and assignment
  -> create certification/action record
  -> create audit record
  -> flush all changes
  -> commit
```

If audit insert or flush fails, rollback the full action.

## 6. Event Details

### Certify

```text
Old: PENDING_PREPARER or REJECTED
New: PENDING_APPROVER
Role: PREPARER_RECONCILER
```

### Approve

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: CLOSED
Role: APPROVER
```

### Reject

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: REJECTED
Role: APPROVER
Metadata: reason code, reason text, comments
```

### Decertify

```text
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: PENDING_PREPARER
Role: PREPARER_RECONCILER
Metadata: reason/comments and assignment changes
```

## 7. Audit UI

Journal Detail includes an Audit Trail or Certification Details section.

Columns:

```text
Action
Status From
Status To
Performed By
Performed Role
Performed At
Reason
Comments
```

Default order is newest first. Use pagination when needed.

## 8. API

```http
GET /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/audit
```

Access requires authorization to the journal.

## 9. Immutability

- No normal update/delete API.
- Corrections are represented by new events.
- Database permissions should prevent application users from modifying history.
- Retention follows corporate requirements when supplied.

## 10. Metadata

Use structured JSON for supplementary values. Do not hide essential searchable fields only inside JSON.

Example:

```json
{
  "assignment_id": "ASN001",
  "certification_id": "CRT001",
  "reason_code": "MISSING_SUPPORT",
  "screen_id": "journal-detail",
  "action_source": "UI"
}
```

## 11. Error Handling

If audit logging fails:

```text
Action could not be completed because audit logging failed. Please retry or contact support.
```

API code:

```text
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
```

## 12. Security

- Audit access is journal-authorized.
- Sensitive metadata is minimized.
- Correlation IDs link application logs to API failures but need not replace audit IDs.
- Audit records do not contain bearer tokens or password data.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Every mandatory action produces one audit event.
- Event contains before/after status.
- Event identifies user and active role.
- Audit failure rolls back the workflow action.
- UI shows the event after success.
- Audit history cannot be edited through the application.


# Journal-Status-Matrix

# Journal Status Matrix

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the Phase 1 journal status model and allowed transitions. Status rules are implemented in code using enums.

## 2. Status Enum

```text
UPLOADED
PARSING
PARSE_FAILED
PENDING_PREPARER
PENDING_APPROVER
REJECTED
CLOSED
```

File-load status and journal status should remain separate enums. `UPLOADED`, `PARSING`, and `PARSE_FAILED` primarily belong to the file-load lifecycle, while journal business records begin after successful parse.

## 3. Journal Business Statuses

### PENDING_PREPARER

Journal is assigned to Preparer and awaits certification.

### PENDING_APPROVER

Preparer certified the journal; Approver owns the active decision. Original Preparer may View and Decertify.

### REJECTED

Approver rejected the journal. It is returned to the Preparer for correction/review and re-certification.

### CLOSED
