Approver approved the journal. Workflow is complete for Phase 1 and the journal is read-only.

## 4. Transition Matrix

| From | Action | Role | To |
|---|---|---|---|
| PENDING_PREPARER | Certify | Assigned Preparer | PENDING_APPROVER |
| REJECTED | Certify | Assigned Preparer | PENDING_APPROVER |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Approve | Assigned Approver | CLOSED |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Reject | Assigned Approver | REJECTED |
| PENDING_APPROVER | Decertify | Original Assigned Preparer | PENDING_PREPARER |

## 5. Grid Action Matrix

| Status / Ownership | Grid Action |
|---|---|
| Unassigned and editable | Edit |
| Assigned actionable Preparer | Cert |
| Assigned actionable Approver | Cert |
| Moved to another role bucket | View |
| Closed | View |

## 6. Detail Action Matrix

| Status | Preparer | Approver | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|
| PENDING_PREPARER | Certify | View if authorized | Undefined |
| REJECTED | Certify | View | Undefined |
| PENDING_APPROVER | View, Decertify | Approve, Reject | Undefined |
| CLOSED | View | View | Undefined |

## 7. Assignment Effects

### Certify

- Complete/deactivate Preparer actionable assignment as appropriate.
- Activate/create Approver assignment.

### Reject

- Return/activate Preparer assignment.
- Complete/deactivate Approver actionable assignment.

### Approve

- Complete Approver assignment.
- Close journal.

### Decertify

- Reactivate Preparer assignment.
- Remove/deactivate pending Approver action.

Exact assignment status enum values must remain consistent across code and schema.

## 8. Invalid Transitions

Examples:

- Approve from PENDING_PREPARER.
- Reject from CLOSED.
- Decertify from REJECTED.
- Certify from CLOSED.
- Approver decision by an unassigned user.

Return:

```text
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
```

## 9. Concurrency

Status transitions require row locking or optimistic version validation. A stale client receives 409 Conflict and refreshes the current status.

## 10. Reviewer

No Reviewer transition is included. A future Reviewer flow requires a separate architecture update.

## 11. Acceptance Criteria

- Only listed transitions succeed.
- Assignment changes match the transition.
- Grid and detail actions match status and ownership.
- Invalid transitions return structured errors.
- Audit records capture old and new status.


# Certification-Workflow

# Certification Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Certification workflow allows the assigned Preparer to certify a journal and move it to the Approver bucket.

## 2. Eligible States

```text
PENDING_PREPARER
REJECTED
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- Current user owns the Preparer assignment.
- User is not the resolved Approver for the same journal.
- Session and token are valid.

## 4. Entry

View Journal Progress displays `Cert`. Selecting it opens Journal Detail. The detail page displays `Certify` when all rules pass.

## 5. Preconditions

- Journal exists.
- Correct current status.
- Preparer assignment is actionable.
- Required supporting documents exist if header validation requires them.
- Required comments/explanations are complete.
- Debit/credit and other configured validation rules pass.
- Approver assignment/resolution is valid.
- Preparer and Approver are different users.

## 6. Transaction

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> revalidate status and assignment
  -> validate certification prerequisites
  -> update status to PENDING_APPROVER
  -> update Preparer assignment
  -> activate/create Approver assignment
  -> insert journal_certification record
  -> insert PREPARER_CERTIFIED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

Any failure rolls back all changes.

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/certify
```

Request may contain:

```json
{
  "comments": "<optional-or-required-comment>",
  "certification_statement_id": "<statement-id>"
}
```

The backend derives user and role.

## 8. Success Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Certified successfully.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_APPROVER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 9. Post-Success UI

- Show success popup/toast.
- Refresh journal detail.
- Show View mode.
- Show Decertify to the original Preparer.
- Refresh Audit Trail.
- Invalidate progress and To Do data.
- Approver receives actionable work/notification after commit.

## 10. Audit

```text
Action: PREPARER_CERTIFIED
Old: PENDING_PREPARER or REJECTED
New: PENDING_APPROVER
```

Audit failure rolls back certification.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
REQUIRED_DOCUMENT_MISSING
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 12. Concurrency

A second Certify submission must not create duplicate certifications. Disable the button and enforce status/locking in the backend.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Only assigned Preparer can certify.
- PENDING_PREPARER and REJECTED can certify.
- Status becomes PENDING_APPROVER.
- Approver work becomes active.
- Audit event exists.
- Failure leaves journal unchanged.


# Approval-Workflow

# Approval Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Approval workflow enables the assigned Approver to approve or reject a journal currently in the Approver bucket.

## 2. Eligible State

```text
PENDING_APPROVER
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `APPROVER`.
- Current user owns the active Approver assignment.
- Current user is not the journal Preparer.
- Session and token are valid.

## 4. Entry

View Journal Progress displays `Cert` for an actionable Approver journal. Selecting it opens Journal Detail, which displays:

- Approve.
- Reject.

There is no separate confirmed Approver Decertify action.

## 5. Approve Flow

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate PENDING_APPROVER and assignment
  -> validate segregation of duties
  -> update status to CLOSED
  -> complete Approver assignment
  -> insert approval certification/action record
  -> insert APPROVER_APPROVED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

### Success

```text
Approved successfully.
```

The journal becomes read-only.

## 6. Reject Flow

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate PENDING_APPROVER and assignment
  -> validate rejection reason/comments
  -> update status to REJECTED
  -> complete/deactivate Approver assignment
  -> reactivate Preparer assignment
  -> insert rejection certification/action record
  -> insert APPROVER_REJECTED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

### Success

```text
Rejected successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

## 7. APIs

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/approve
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/reject
```

Reject request:

```json
{
  "reason_code": "MISSING_SUPPORT",
  "comments": "Please attach the required support."
}
```

## 8. Audit

Approve:

```text
APPROVER_APPROVED
PENDING_APPROVER -> CLOSED
```

Reject:

```text
APPROVER_REJECTED
PENDING_APPROVER -> REJECTED
```

Audit failure rolls back the decision.

## 9. Notifications and To Do

After commit:

- Approve removes active work from Approver and closes the journal.
- Reject removes active Approver work and creates/reactivates Preparer work.
- Relevant users receive notifications according to the notification module.

## 10. Error Codes

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
REJECTION_REASON_REQUIRED
REJECTION_COMMENT_REQUIRED
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 11. Concurrency

Approve and Reject are mutually exclusive. The first committed action changes the status. A stale second action receives 409 Conflict.

## 12. UI Confirmation

Approve and Reject should use confirmation dialogs where shown/appropriate. Reject dialog contains reason and comments fields. The UI never displays raw backend errors.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Only assigned Approver can act.
- Approve closes journal.
- Reject returns journal to Preparer.
- Reason/comments are validated.
- Audit record is mandatory.
- Same person cannot prepare and approve.
- Failure rolls back all updates.


# Decertification-Workflow

# Decertification Workflow

## 1. Purpose

Decertification allows the original assigned Preparer to pull a certified journal back from the Approver bucket before the Approver has approved or rejected it.

## 2. Eligible State

```text
PENDING_APPROVER
```

## 3. Eligible User

- Active role is `PREPARER_RECONCILER`.
- Current user is the journal's assigned/original Preparer.
- Approver has not completed a decision.
- Journal is not CLOSED or REJECTED.

## 4. Entry

In View Journal Progress, the journal is in another role bucket, so the row action is `View` for the Preparer. Journal Detail shows `Decertify` when the rule is satisfied.

## 5. Preconditions

- Journal exists.
- Status remains PENDING_APPROVER.
- Preparer ownership is valid.
- Approver assignment is still pending/actionable.
- Required reason/comment policy is satisfied.

## 6. Transaction

```text
Begin transaction
  -> lock journal
  -> validate Preparer and PENDING_APPROVER
  -> update status to PENDING_PREPARER
  -> reactivate Preparer assignment
  -> deactivate/cancel pending Approver assignment
  -> insert decertification action record
  -> insert PREPARER_DECERTIFIED audit record
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

Any failure rolls back the status and assignment changes.

## 7. API

```http
POST /api/v1/journals/{journalId}/decertify
```

Request:

```json
{
  "reason_code": "<configured-code>",
  "comments": "<reason for pulling back>"
}
```

## 8. Success Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_DECERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Decertified successfully and returned to Preparer queue.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_PREPARER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

## 9. Post-Success UI

- Show success popup.
- Refresh journal detail.
- Show Certify again when prerequisites are met.
- Remove journal from active Approver work.
- Add/reactivate Preparer To Do item.
- Refresh Audit Trail.

## 10. Audit

```text
Action: PREPARER_DECERTIFIED
Old: PENDING_APPROVER
New: PENDING_PREPARER
```

Metadata includes reason/comments and assignment-status changes.

## 11. Error Codes

```text
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
DECERTIFICATION_REASON_REQUIRED
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 12. Race Condition

If the Approver approves or rejects before the Decertify transaction obtains the lock, Decertify fails with 409 Conflict and the UI refreshes the latest state.

## 13. Restrictions

- Approver cannot decertify in Phase 1.
- Reviewer decertification is undefined.
- Decertification after CLOSED is prohibited.
- Decertification does not delete previous certification history.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Original Preparer can decertify only while pending Approver.
- Grid link remains View before opening detail.
- Status returns to PENDING_PREPARER.
- Approver work is removed/deactivated.
- Audit event is mandatory.
- Previous certification remains historically visible.
- Failure leaves all state unchanged.


# Currency-Architecture

# Currency Architecture

## 1. Purpose

The Currency module provides System Admin access to currency configuration and period-based conversion-rate information. The architecture is based on the supplied currency screenshots, the currency-conversion workbook used as seed data, and the existing PostgreSQL currency tables.

## 2. Confirmed Scope

Phase 1 contains:

- Currency Settings.
- Currency Rates Information.
- Cross-currency display.
- Historical-rate display.
- Show Prior 12 Periods.
- SQL/workbook-based seed loading.

The module must not add unsupported pages or speculative rate-maintenance workflows.

## 3. Role and Authorization

The confirmed role is `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.

Required controls:

- Valid bearer token.
- Active server-side session.
- Active role `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.
- Explicit permission for currency screens.

Other roles do not gain access because the user can view journal rates elsewhere. Direct access by an unauthorized role returns 403.

## 4. Routes

```text
/admin/currency/settings
/admin/currency/rates
```

Unsupported currency routes return the standard 404 page.

## 5. Currency Area Layout

The supplied Currency Setup screenshot shows a left-panel pattern for currency configuration. The delivered UI should preserve the approved left-panel/content relationship while using neutral component and CSS names.

Recommended structure:

```text
Currency Area
  -> Currency Left Navigation
  -> Currency Settings
  -> Currency Rates Information
```

Only supported entries are displayed.

## 6. Frontend Modules

```text
modules/currency/
  pages/
    CurrencySettingsPage.tsx
    CurrencyRatesInformationPage.tsx
  components/
    CurrencySidePanel.tsx
    CurrencyRateGrid.tsx
    CrossCurrencyToggle.tsx
    PriorPeriodsToggle.tsx
    CurrencyFilters.tsx
  hooks/
    useCurrencySettings.ts
    useCurrencyRates.ts
  services/
    currencyService.ts
  types/
    currency.types.ts
  styles/
```

## 7. Backend Modules

```text
api/v1/controllers/admin_currency_controller.py
application/services/currency/
  get_currency_settings_service.py
  get_currency_rates_service.py
  import_currency_rates_service.py
  impl/
domain/services/currency_rate_service.py
infrastructure/database/repositories/currency_conversion_repository_impl.py
```

Controllers handle HTTP only. Application-service implementations orchestrate authorization, validation, repository access, and audit behavior.

## 8. Database Model

The existing Phase 1 model includes:

```text
currency_conversion_rate
currency_rate_load
```

Core rate fields:

```text
rate_id
from_currency
to_currency
conversion_rate
effective_from
effective_to
source_system
source_file_name
load_batch_id
is_active
created_at
updated_at
```

The model supports direct and cross-currency pairs through `from_currency` and `to_currency`.

## 9. Period Model

Currency screens use the global `MM/YYYY` period. The default is the previous month. The selected period is the anchor for current-rate and historical-rate retrieval.

## 10. Rate Precision

- Store rates using fixed decimal precision.
- Current schema uses `NUMERIC(18,8)`.
- Do not use binary floating-point values for authoritative rate calculations.
- Display precision follows the workbook/screenshot and must not silently truncate significant digits.

## 11. Rate Selection

For a requested pair and period, select the active rate whose effective date range covers the period anchor date.

```text
from_currency = requested source
and to_currency = requested target
and effective_from <= period end/anchor
and (effective_to is null or effective_to >= period start/anchor)
and is_active = true
```

If multiple rates overlap, treat the configuration as invalid and return a controlled error rather than choosing arbitrarily.

## 12. API Overview

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=MM/YYYY
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/import
```

The import endpoint is included only for controlled administration or deployment use. If no upload control is approved in the screen, seed/import remains an operational process.

## 13. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_RATE_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_RATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_FAILED
```

## 14. Audit and Logging

Viewing rates is application logged when needed but does not create journal audit events. Import or configuration changes create currency administration audit records. Journal conversions retain the rate used on the journal so later rate changes do not rewrite historical financial values.

## 15. Non-Functional Requirements

- Rate retrieval must be deterministic.
- Large historical grids remain responsive.
- All rate values are decimal safe.
- APIs include correlation IDs.
- No spreadsheet formula is treated as authoritative unless imported as a resolved value and validated.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- System Admin can open both confirmed currency screens.
- Global period defaults to previous month.
- Direct rates display correctly.
- Cross-currency mode displays the approved pair view.
- Prior 12 periods displays the approved history structure.
- Unauthorized roles receive 403.
- Missing or overlapping rates produce structured errors.


# Currency-Settings

# Currency Settings

## 1. Purpose

Currency Settings defines the supported currency configuration displayed through the confirmed currency setup screen and left-panel navigation.

## 2. Scope Control

The screenshot confirms the Currency Settings area, but not every reference-system setting is automatically in scope. Implement only fields and controls visible in the approved screen or required for the Phase 1 rate model.

## 3. Access

- Active role: `SYSTEM_ADMIN`.
- Other roles: denied.
- Direct navigation without authorization: 403.

## 4. Route

```text
/admin/currency/settings
```

## 5. Page Layout

```text
Global application shell
  -> Currency left panel
  -> Currency Settings content
  -> Save/Cancel controls only when edit behavior is approved
```

The left panel must match the supplied visual hierarchy and highlight the active currency page.

## 6. Configuration Concepts

Supported configuration may include:

- Currency code.
- Display name.
- Active/inactive indicator.
- Default target/reporting currency.
- Rate type or frequency when present in approved data.
- Decimal/display precision when supplied.

Do not invent settlement, hedging, consolidation, or accounting-policy settings.

## 7. Currency Code Rules

- Required.
- Uppercase.
- Normally three characters when using standard currency codes.
- Unique in the active currency catalog.
- Preserve approved non-standard codes if they exist in source data.
- Cannot be changed in a way that breaks historical rate references.

## 8. Active Status

Deactivation prevents new configuration use but does not delete historical rates or journal conversion history.

```text
Deactivate != Delete
```

## 9. API

If Phase 1 is view-only:

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
```

If approved editing is implemented:

```http
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/settings
PUT  /api/v1/admin/currency/settings/{currencyCode}
```

The architecture must not expose write endpoints before the screen's edit controls and permissions are approved.

## 10. Validation

- Currency code required.
- Duplicate code rejected.
- Default target currency must exist and be active.
- Precision values must be within configured bounds.
- Deactivation must not remove historical records.

## 11. UI Behavior

- Dense enterprise form or grid consistent with screenshot.
- Labels above or beside controls as shown.
- Save disabled when no changes exist.
- Cancel restores server values.
- Loading and error states use shared UI components.
- Success and error messages use centralized enums/catalog.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_SETTING_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_CODE_REQUIRED
CURRENCY_CODE_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_TARGET_INVALID
CURRENCY_SETTING_UPDATE_FAILED
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 13. Audit

When writes are enabled, record:

```text
CURRENCY_SETTING_CREATED
CURRENCY_SETTING_UPDATED
CURRENCY_SETTING_DEACTIVATED
```

Audit metadata includes before/after values, actor, role, and timestamp. Viewing settings does not create a business audit event.

## 14. Database Direction

If a dedicated currency-master table is introduced, keep it separate from rate history.

Recommended fields:

```text
currency_code
currency_name
is_active
display_precision
created_at
updated_at
```

The current Phase 1 schema can derive available currencies from rate data until a dedicated master is required.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Active currency navigation is clear.
- Only approved fields appear.
- No unsupported settings are invented.
- Historical rates survive deactivation.
- Unauthorized roles cannot view or modify settings.
- Errors include user-friendly messages and correlation IDs.


# Currency-Rates-Information

# Currency Rates Information

## 1. Purpose

Currency Rates Information displays period-based currency conversion rates using the layout and controls shown in the supplied screenshots.

## 2. Route

```text
/admin/currency/rates
```

## 3. Access

System Admin only for the confirmed administration screen.

## 4. Screen Structure

The page contains:

- Currency-area left panel.
- Page title/information area.
- Period context.
- Rate filters.
- `Show Cross Currency` selection.
- `Show Prior 12 Periods` selection.
- Rate information grid.

Labels and layout follow the screenshots rather than a generic form design.

## 5. Current-Period View

Default behavior:

- Global period is previous month.
- Cross-currency option is off unless the approved screenshot shows another default.
- Prior-12-period option is off.
- Display current/selected period rates.

## 6. Grid Columns

The exact labels follow the screenshot and workbook. The logical fields include:

```text
rate_type
from_currency
to_currency
selected_period
conversion_rate
source
status
```

Historical mode adds period columns or period rows according to the approved screenshot.

## 7. Filters

Confirmed logical filters may include:

- Rate type.
- Currency/from currency.
- Convert to/to currency.
- Period.
- Custom frequency only if visible and supported.

Do not add unsupported filter controls.

## 8. API

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_cross_currency=false&show_prior_periods=false
```

Optional pair filters:

```text
fromCurrency=USD
toCurrency=INR
rateType=<type>
```

## 9. Response Model

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "period": "07/2026",
    "show_cross_currency": false,
    "show_prior_periods": false,
    "periods": ["07/2026"],
    "rates": [
      {
        "from_currency": "EUR",
        "to_currency": "USD",
        "rate": "1.09000000"
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "<correlation-id>"
}
```

Rate values are serialized as decimal-safe strings or JSON decimal-compatible values.

## 10. Missing Rates

A missing rate is displayed as blank or an approved missing-data indicator, not zero. Zero is a valid number only if the business source explicitly supplies it.

The API may return warnings:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
```

## 11. Sorting and Pagination

- Sort by rate type, source currency, and target currency by default.
- Use pagination when rate volume requires it.
- Prior-period columns remain aligned during horizontal scrolling.
- Currency codes remain visible through pinned columns if the approved grid supports pinning.

## 12. Precision and Formatting

- Preserve up to eight decimal places from the database.
- Do not apply currency monetary symbols to conversion rates.
- Do not use percentage formatting.
- Trim only insignificant trailing zeros when consistent with the screenshot.

## 13. Loading and Error States

- Changing period or options shows grid loading.
- Failed retrieval shows centralized error state.
- Last valid selection remains visible when practical.

Error codes:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

## 14. Accessibility

- Toggle controls have explicit labels.
- Grid headers identify period and pair.
- Missing rate indicators include accessible text.
- Horizontal grid remains keyboard accessible.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Page matches the confirmed left-panel and rate-grid structure.
- Selected period is displayed.
- Direct rates load by default.
- Cross-currency and prior-period options alter the view independently.
- Missing values are not shown as zero.
- Rate precision is preserved.


# Cross-Currency-Rules

# Cross-Currency Rules

## 1. Purpose

Cross-currency mode displays conversion relationships between currencies beyond a simple source-to-reporting-currency list. The supplied cross-currency screenshot is the UI source of truth.

## 2. Terms

```text
Direct rate: A -> B stored explicitly.
Inverse rate: B -> A calculated as 1 / direct rate.
Cross rate: A -> C calculated through an approved pivot currency B.
```

## 3. Phase 1 Pivot

The existing seed data primarily contains currency-to-USD rates. Therefore USD is the natural Phase 1 pivot unless the workbook/configuration explicitly defines another reporting currency.

Do not hardcode USD inside UI components. Treat the pivot/reporting currency as configuration.

## 4. Calculation

Assume rates are stored as:

```text
1 unit of FROM currency = rate units of TO currency
```

If both currencies have rates to the same pivot:

```text
A -> Pivot = rate_A
C -> Pivot = rate_C
A -> C = rate_A / rate_C
```

Example:

```text
EUR -> USD = 1.09
INR -> USD = 0.012
EUR -> INR = 1.09 / 0.012
```

All calculations use decimal arithmetic.

## 5. Direct-Rate Priority

Resolution order:

```text
1. Explicit direct rate for pair and period
2. Approved inverse of explicit reverse pair
3. Cross rate through configured pivot
4. Missing-rate result
```

This order avoids replacing an approved direct rate with a calculated value.

## 6. Same-Currency Rule

```text
A -> A = 1
```

Same-currency rates may be stored or derived. Display exactly `1` using approved precision.

## 7. Inverse Rule

```text
inverse_rate = 1 / direct_rate
```

A direct rate of zero cannot be inverted and is a configuration error.

## 8. Period Consistency

All component rates used in one cross-rate calculation must belong to the same selected period/effective date context. Never combine rates from different periods.

## 9. Rounding

- Calculate with database/application decimal precision.
- Round only for final display or persisted derived rate.
- Do not round component rates before division.
- Display precision follows configured rate precision, up to the schema scale.

## 10. Missing Component Rate

If either pivot component is missing:

- Do not calculate the cross rate.
- Display missing/blank indicator.
- Include a warning code.
- Do not substitute a previous period unless an explicit fallback rule is approved.

## 11. API

Cross mode request:

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_cross_currency=true
```

The response identifies whether each rate is:

```text
DIRECT
INVERSE
CROSS
IDENTITY
MISSING
```

## 12. Auditability

For journal calculations, store the actual rate used and its source/method so historical journal totals remain explainable.

Recommended metadata:

```text
rate_method
pivot_currency
component_rate_ids
calculated_at
```

The information screen may calculate cross rates on retrieval without persisting every matrix cell.

## 13. Error Codes

```text
CROSS_CURRENCY_COMPONENT_MISSING
CROSS_CURRENCY_ZERO_RATE
CROSS_CURRENCY_INVALID_PAIR
CROSS_CURRENCY_CALCULATION_FAILED
```

## 14. Security

Only authorized System Admin users access the administration cross-currency screen. Journal-processing services may call the rate service internally without exposing admin endpoints.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Explicit direct rate has priority.
- Same-currency result equals one.
- Inverse uses decimal division.
- Cross rate uses one configured pivot and same-period components.
- Missing components do not produce fabricated values.
- Response identifies calculation method.


# Historical-Rates-And-Prior-12-Periods

# Historical Rates and Prior 12 Periods

## 1. Purpose

This document defines historical currency-rate retrieval and the `Show Prior 12 Periods` behavior shown in the supplied screenshot.

## 2. Anchor Period

The globally selected period is the anchor period.

Example:

```text
Selected period: 07/2026
```

## 3. Period Sequence

The UI displays the selected period and prior periods in descending order.

Example sequence:

```text
07/2026
06/2026
05/2026
04/2026
03/2026
02/2026
01/2026
12/2025
11/2025
10/2025
09/2025
08/2025
07/2025
```

The supplied screenshot determines whether the selected period plus 12 prior periods, or 12 total periods including the selected period, is displayed. The implementation must match the screenshot exactly.

## 4. Year Rollover

Period generation must correctly cross calendar years.

```text
01/2027 -> 12/2026 -> 11/2026 ...
```

## 5. Data Retrieval

For each pair and each displayed period:

- Resolve the effective rate for that period.
- Preserve the rate source and method.
- Return missing rather than carrying forward unless a fallback rule is approved.

## 6. API

```http
GET /api/v1/admin/currency/rates?period=07/2026&show_prior_periods=true
```

Response includes an ordered periods array and rate values keyed by period.

Example:

```json
{
  "periods": ["07/2026", "06/2026", "05/2026"],
  "rates": [
    {
      "from_currency": "EUR",
      "to_currency": "USD",
      "period_values": {
        "07/2026": "1.09000000",
        "06/2026": "1.08000000",
        "05/2026": null
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

## 7. Grid Presentation

The screenshot indicates an expanded period view. Recommended behavior:

- Currency-pair columns remain pinned.
- Period columns scroll horizontally.
- Newest/anchor period appears first.
- Header labels use `MM/YYYY`.
- Missing values remain blank or show an approved unavailable indicator.
- Tooltips can display rate source and effective dates.

## 8. Cross-Currency Combination

`Show Cross Currency` and `Show Prior 12 Periods` may be selected together if the approved UI permits it.

In combined mode:

- Calculate/resolve every pair independently for every period.
- Do not reuse the anchor-period cross rate for older periods.
- Return rate method per period.

## 9. Performance

- Query the full effective-date range in one repository call where practical.
- Index currency pair and effective dates.
- Build the period matrix in the application service.
- Avoid one database query per cell.
- Cache read-only period results where safe and invalidate after rate import.

## 10. Historical Integrity

Changing a current rate must not overwrite historical effective periods. Import should insert new effective-dated records or close previous ranges according to approved rules.

## 11. Missing and Duplicate Rates

- Missing rate: null plus warning metadata.
- Overlapping active rates: configuration error.
- Duplicate pair/effective start: rejected by unique constraint or validation.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
HISTORICAL_RATE_LOAD_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
```

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- Period sequence matches screenshot.
- January rollover works.
- Pair columns remain readable during horizontal scroll.
- Historical values use their own period rates.
- Missing values are not carried forward without approval.
- Combined cross/historical mode is period correct.


# Currency-Import-Workflow

# Currency Import Workflow

## 1. Purpose

The Currency Import workflow loads validated conversion-rate seed or operational data into PostgreSQL. The supplied currency workbook is the Phase 1 source for seed data.

## 2. Deployment Versus UI Import

Two modes are distinguished:

### Seed/Deployment Import

- Workbook is converted into reviewed SQL seed data.
- SQL file is version controlled.
- Loaded using `psql` during database setup.
- This is the required Phase 1 baseline.

### Administrative UI Import

- Implement only if a visible upload control and business requirement are approved.
- Uses the same validation and transaction service.

Do not expose a speculative upload screen solely because the database has a load table.

## 3. Recommended Files

```text
backend/db/seed/003_seed_currency_rates.sql
backend/db/source/currency_conversion_source.xls
backend/db/README.md
```

The source workbook may be retained outside the deployable repository if licensing, confidentiality, or size policies require it. The SQL seed remains the executable artifact.

## 4. Import Data Contract

Each rate row resolves to:

```text
rate_id
rate_type, if supplied
from_currency
to_currency
conversion_rate
effective_from
effective_to
source_system
source_file_name
load_batch_id
is_active
```

## 5. Validation Rules

### File-Level

- Expected workbook/sheet exists.
- Required columns exist.
- File is readable.
- No unsupported formula/error cells in required fields.

### Row-Level

- Source currency required.
- Target currency required.
- Currency codes normalized to uppercase.
- Conversion rate required and greater than zero unless a documented exception exists.
- Effective-from date required.
- Effective-to is optional but cannot precede effective-from.
- Duplicate pair/effective-from rejected.
- Overlapping active ranges rejected.
- Same-currency rate must equal one.

## 6. Transaction Behavior

A controlled import is atomic for the batch:

```text
Begin transaction
  -> create currency_rate_load record
  -> validate all rows
  -> insert/update rate records according to approved mode
  -> update valid/invalid counts
  -> mark load successful
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

If any blocking error occurs:

```text
Rollback rate changes
Persist failure/load-error summary separately
Show/import-log error result
```

Do not partially load business rate data when atomic mode is selected.

## 7. Import Modes

Recommended modes:

```text
INSERT_ONLY
REPLACE_BATCH
```

`UPSERT` should not be used casually because it can overwrite historical data. Any update mode must preserve prior values and effective history.

## 8. SQL Seed Execution

The deployment guide uses direct `psql` execution. Example final path:

```powershell
& "C:\Program Files\PostgreSQL\18\bin\psql.exe" -U postgres -h localhost -d journal_certification -f .\db\seed\003_seed_currency_rates.sql
```

Paths must match the repository exactly.

## 9. Administrative API

Only when approved:

```http
POST /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/import
GET  /api/v1/admin/currency/rates/imports/{loadBatchId}
```

## 10. Load Tracking

The `currency_rate_load` record stores:

```text
load_batch_id
source_type
source_file_name
file_content, optional
total_rows
valid_rows
invalid_rows
status
error_message
loaded_by
loaded_at
processed_at
```

For AWS deployment, source files may move to S3 while metadata remains in PostgreSQL.

## 11. Audit

Administration audit events:

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_STARTED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_SUCCEEDED
CURRENCY_RATE_IMPORT_FAILED
```

Audit metadata includes batch ID, source file, row counts, and actor. Do not store sensitive workbook content in logs.

## 12. Error Codes

```text
CURRENCY_IMPORT_FILE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_COLUMN_MISSING
CURRENCY_IMPORT_RATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_DATE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_DUPLICATE
CURRENCY_IMPORT_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_IMPORT_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

## 13. Post-Import Behavior

After successful import:

- Invalidate currency-rate caches.
- Refresh Currency Rates Information.
- Preserve historical journal rates already stored on journals.
- Show success message with batch counts.

## 14. Security

- Import requires System Admin or deployment-level database authorization.
- Uploaded workbook type and size are validated.
- No macro execution.
- No formula execution by backend import code.
- Store secrets outside files and source control.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Seed SQL can build currency rates from scratch.
- Invalid rows block an atomic import.
- No partial rate data remains after failure.
- Duplicate and overlapping rates are rejected.
- Same-currency rates equal one.
- Import produces a load record and administration audit event.
- Currency screens show new rates only after commit.


# Dependency-Validation

# Dependency Validation

## Purpose
Dependency Validation is the authoritative routing configuration used during journal parsing and assignment resolution.

## Business Objective
- Resolve Preparer ownership
- Resolve Approver ownership
- Support journal routing
- Prevent invalid assignments
- Enforce segregation of duties

## Source System
The first key value originates from the JDE Preparer identifier in the uploaded file.

## Core Data
- Preparer Identifier
- Preparer User
- Approver User
- Effective Dates
- Active Flag

## Processing
Upload -> Validate -> Store Mapping -> Journal Parse Uses Mapping

## Validation Rules
- Preparer required
- Approver required
- Active mapping required
- Same-user Preparer/Approver prohibited
- Duplicate active mappings prohibited

## Security
System Admin access only.

## Acceptance Criteria
Every parsed journal resolves to a valid Preparer and Approver mapping.


# Dependency-Mapping-Upload

# Dependency Mapping Upload

## Purpose
Provides controlled loading of dependency mapping data.

## Supported Inputs
- Excel
- CSV

## Upload Lifecycle
Select File -> Validate Structure -> Validate Rows -> Save Batch -> Activate Mapping

## Mandatory Columns
- Preparer ID
- Preparer Name
- Approver ID
- Approver Name
- Status

## Row Validations
- Required values present
- Approver exists
- Preparer exists
- No duplicate active mapping
- Preparer != Approver

## Errors
- Missing Column
- Invalid User
- Duplicate Mapping
- Invalid Status

## Audit
- Upload Started
- Upload Completed
- Upload Failed


# Journal-Assignment-Engine

# Journal Assignment Engine

## Purpose
The Assignment Engine determines ownership during parsing and workflow transitions.

## Inputs
- Parsed JDE Journal
- Dependency Validation Data
- User Master
- Role Assignments

## Primary Flow
Read Journal -> Resolve Preparer -> Resolve Approver -> Create Assignments -> Persist Journal

## Assignment Rules
### Preparer Assignment
Resolved from dependency mapping.

### Approver Assignment
Resolved from dependency mapping.

### Same User Check
A user cannot be both Preparer and Approver.

## Workflow Ownership
PENDING_PREPARER -> Preparer Assignment Active
PENDING_APPROVER -> Approver Assignment Active
REJECTED -> Preparer Assignment Active
CLOSED -> No Active Assignment

## Failure Handling
Any resolution failure causes parse rollback.

## Audit
Assignment creation is logged.


# Approver-Resolution-Logic

# Approver Resolution Logic

## Purpose
Defines how an Approver is identified for a journal.

## Resolution Sequence
1. Read JDE Preparer Identifier
2. Lookup Dependency Mapping
3. Locate Active Mapping
4. Resolve Approver User
5. Validate User Role
6. Create Assignment

## Validation Rules
- Mapping must exist
- Approver must be active
- Approver role must be assigned
- Approver cannot equal Preparer

## Errors
- APPROVER_NOT_FOUND
- DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
- INVALID_APPROVER_ROLE
- PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER

## Parse Impact
Unable to resolve Approver = Parse Failure = Transaction Rollback

## Acceptance Criteria
Every journal has one valid Approver before commit.


# JDE-Integration-Model

# JDE Integration Model

## Purpose
Defines inbound integration between JDE journal extracts and the Journal Certification Platform.

## Source
JDE generated journal file.

## Flow
JDE Export -> Upload -> Parse -> Validate -> Assignment Resolution -> Persist -> Workflow

## Import Stages
### Upload
Stores file metadata and content.

### Parse
Validates entire file.

### Persistence
Creates journal headers, journal lines, assignments and audit records.

## Atomic Processing Rule
If any row fails a critical validation:
- Rollback transaction
- No journal created
- No assignments created

## Dependency Validation Usage
JDE Preparer Identifier is used to resolve Preparer and Approver ownership.

## Future Enhancements
- S3 ingestion
- API integration
- Scheduled imports

## Acceptance Criteria
Uploaded JDE data can be parsed and routed to the correct users through dependency mappings.


# Security-Architecture

# Security Architecture

## Purpose
Defines authentication, authorization, session control, RBAC, auditability, and API protection.

## Principles
- Zero trust authorization
- JWT-based authentication
- Server-side session validation
- RBAC authorization
- Journal ownership enforcement
- Least privilege access

## Layers
Frontend Route Guards -> API Authorization -> Business Authorization -> Data Access Authorization

## Controls
- HTTPS only
- JWT bearer tokens
- Refresh token support
- Password hashing
- Correlation IDs
- Audit logging

## Security Events
Login, Logout, Role Switch, Certify, Approve, Reject, Decertify, Upload, Parse.


# Authentication-Flow

# Authentication Flow

## Login
User -> Login API -> Credential Validation -> Session Creation -> JWT Issued.

## Flow
1. Submit credentials
2. Validate user
3. Validate active account
4. Create session
5. Issue access token
6. Return user roles

## Logout
Invalidate session and refresh token.

## Failure Codes
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_ACCOUNT_DISABLED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED


# JWT-And-Session-Management

# JWT and Session Management

## Access Token
Contains:
- user_id
- username
- active_role
- session_id
- issued_at
- expiry

## Session Table
Tracks active sessions.

## Validation
Every API validates:
- JWT signature
- Expiration
- Session existence
- Session status

## Expiration
Expired token -> 401 Unauthorized.

## Security
Never store passwords in JWT.


# Role-Switching

# Role Switching

## Purpose
Users may own multiple roles but only one active role.

## Flow
Select Role -> API Call -> Validate Ownership -> Update Session -> Issue New JWT.

## Rules
- Role must exist in user_roles.
- Frontend cannot grant roles.
- Backend is authoritative.

## Result
Navigation, Dashboard and Permissions refresh.


# Authorization-Matrix

# Authorization Matrix

## Roles
PREPARER_RECONCILER
APPROVER
REVIEWER
SYSTEM_ADMIN
LOCAL_ADMIN

## Key Permissions
Preparer:
- Certify
- Decertify

Approver:
- Approve
- Reject

Reviewer:
- Dashboard only (workflow TBD)

System Admin:
- Currency
- Dependency Validation

Local Admin:
- Upload
- Parse

## Enforcement
UI + API + Business Rule validation.


# Journal-Ownership-Security

# Journal Ownership Security

## Principle
Authentication does not equal ownership.

## Ownership Checks
User must:
- Hold role
- Own assignment
- Be in valid status

## Preparer Rules
Can act only on assigned journals.

## Approver Rules
Can approve/reject only assigned journals.

## Segregation of Duties
Preparer cannot approve same journal.

## Errors
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION

## Audit
All workflow changes are audited.


# Error-Handling-Standards

# Error Handling Standards

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the mandatory error-handling standard for the frontend, FastAPI backend, database operations, workflow transactions, file parsing, authentication, and administration modules.

The application must never display raw exceptions, stack traces, SQL errors, framework messages, or unhandled promise rejections to users. Every failure is translated into a stable application error code and a user-friendly message.

## 2. Core Principles

```text
Technical details belong in protected server logs.
User-facing responses contain safe and actionable messages.
Business failures are not treated as unexpected system failures.
Every failed API response follows one response contract.
Every error response contains a correlation ID.
Workflow and parse failures must not leave partial business data.
```

## 3. Error Categories

### 3.1 Validation Errors

Used when request fields, uploaded rows, periods, identifiers, comments, files, or business inputs are invalid.

Examples:

```text
VALIDATION_ERROR
INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT
FILE_INVALID_COLUMN_COUNT
REJECTION_REASON_REQUIRED
COMMENT_REQUIRED
```

Recommended HTTP status: `400 Bad Request` or `422 Unprocessable Entity`, applied consistently.

### 3.2 Authentication Errors

Used when credentials, access tokens, refresh tokens, or sessions are invalid.

```text
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED
AUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN_INVALID
```

Recommended HTTP status: `401 Unauthorized`.

### 3.3 Authorization Errors

Used when the user is authenticated but lacks the required active role, permission, assignment, or ownership.

```text
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
```

Recommended HTTP status: `403 Forbidden`.

### 3.4 Not Found Errors

Used when a supported resource does not exist.

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_SETTING_NOT_FOUND
DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
```

Recommended HTTP status: `404 Not Found`.

Unknown or unsupported frontend routes use the standard Page Not Found screen and must not be confused with resource-level API failures.

### 3.5 Business Rule Errors

Used when the request is structurally valid but violates workflow or financial rules.

```text
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
```

Recommended HTTP status: `409 Conflict` for state conflicts, or `422 Unprocessable Entity` for other business constraints.

### 3.6 Concurrency Errors

Used when the record changed after the client loaded it or another user completed the action first.

```text
JOURNAL_CONCURRENT_UPDATE
RESOURCE_VERSION_CONFLICT
```

Recommended HTTP status: `409 Conflict`.

The UI must refresh the latest record and explain that the data changed.

### 3.7 Infrastructure and Transaction Errors

Used for database, storage, network, or unexpected server failures.

```text
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
FILE_STORAGE_FAILED
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR
```

Recommended HTTP status: `500 Internal Server Error` or `503 Service Unavailable` when a dependency is temporarily unavailable.

## 4. Backend Exception Hierarchy

Recommended structure:

```text
ApplicationException
  -> ValidationException
  -> AuthenticationException
  -> AuthorizationException
  -> NotFoundException
  -> BusinessRuleException
  -> ConflictException
  -> ParseException
  -> TransactionException
  -> InfrastructureException
```

Every application exception carries:

```text
error_code
safe_message
severity
http_status
details
```

Technical exceptions are logged and translated by global exception handlers.

## 5. Global FastAPI Exception Handling

Recommended file:

```text
backend/app/api/exception_handlers.py
```

Handlers must cover:

- Application exceptions.
- Pydantic/request validation errors.
- Authentication and authorization failures.
- SQLAlchemy/database exceptions.
- File and storage exceptions.
- Unhandled exceptions.

Unknown exceptions are converted to `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`. The response must not expose the exception class, SQL statement, file path, or stack trace.

## 6. Transaction Error Handling

Workflow commands and parsing use explicit transaction boundaries.

```text
Begin transaction
  -> validate
  -> modify business records
  -> create required audit event
  -> flush
  -> commit
```

On any failure:

```text
rollback
log technical failure with correlation ID
return structured API error
show proper UI popup/dialog
```

Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify must roll back when audit logging fails.

## 7. Parse Error Handling

Journal parsing validates the complete file before committing business data.

A critical error causes:

- Parse batch status `PARSE_FAILED`.
- No journal headers committed.
- No journal lines committed.
- No assignments committed.
- Row-level validation details returned or stored in the parse-error area.
- Error popup explaining that no journal data was created.

Example summary:

```text
Parse failed. No journal data was created.
```

## 8. Frontend Error Handling

All API calls use a shared API client and shared error mapper.

The frontend must not use:

```text
window.alert
raw response dumps
component-specific copies of common error messages
```

The frontend must:

- Map the standardized API error response.
- Show the correct notification severity.
- Display field-level errors beside the associated fields.
- Display row-level parse errors in the parse-error panel.
- Display correlation ID for support-worthy failures.
- Redirect to Login for expired sessions.
- Display Access Denied for 403.
- Preserve valid user input when retry is possible.

## 9. Popup and Message Behavior

### Success

Use an auto-dismiss toast for routine completion.

### Information

Use a toast, banner, or inline callout.

### Warning

Use a persistent notification when user attention is required.

### Error

Use a sticky toast or dialog. Critical transaction, parse, and authorization errors should remain visible until dismissed.

## 10. Retry Rules

Automatic retry may be used for idempotent GET requests and transient network failures. Do not automatically retry workflow commands such as Approve, Reject, Certify, Decertify, Parse, or Upload without an idempotency design.

## 11. Error Details

`details` may include safe structured information:

```text
field
row_number
error_code
message
rejected_value, only when safe
```

Do not include credentials, tokens, password hashes, SQL, local paths, or confidential payloads.

## 12. Logging Rule

Every server failure is logged with:

- Correlation ID.
- Timestamp.
- Endpoint.
- User ID where available.
- Active role where available.
- Safe entity identifiers.
- Error code.
- Technical exception and stack trace in protected logs.

## 13. Acceptance Criteria

- All failed APIs use the standard contract.
- Raw exceptions never reach the UI.
- Every error has an enum code.
- Every error response has a correlation ID.
- Workflow failures roll back.
- Parse failures create no journal business data.
- 401, 403, 404, 409, 422, and 500 behaviors are consistent.
- Shared notification components render user-friendly messages.


# API-Response-Contracts

# API Response Contracts

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the common JSON envelopes returned by all REST APIs. Consistent envelopes allow the frontend API client, notification framework, error mapper, and logging tools to behave uniformly.

## 2. General Rules

- JSON property naming uses `snake_case` unless the frontend contract later standardizes on camelCase through a serialization layer.
- Success and failure envelopes are mutually exclusive.
- Every response includes `correlation_id`.
- Timestamps use ISO 8601 UTC.
- Financial decimals are serialized without binary floating-point loss.
- Error codes, message codes, and severity values are enums.

## 3. Success Envelope

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "Certified successfully.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "journal_id": "JRN001",
    "status": "PENDING_APPROVER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000001"
}
```

`message` may be omitted for read-only GET operations when no user notification is required.

## 4. Error Envelope

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION",
    "message": "This journal action is not allowed in the current status.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [],
    "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000002"
  }
}
```

The top-level and nested correlation-ID convention must be chosen once. Recommended implementation keeps `correlation_id` at the top level for both success and error responses. If retained inside `error` for compatibility, do not duplicate mismatched values.

## 5. Validation Error Details

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "VALIDATION_ERROR",
    "message": "Please correct the highlighted fields and try again.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [
      {
        "field": "period",
        "error_code": "INVALID_PERIOD_FORMAT",
        "message": "Select a valid period in MM/YYYY format."
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000003"
}
```

## 6. Parse Error Response

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "error_code": "FILE_PARSE_FAILED",
    "message": "Parse failed. No journal data was created.",
    "severity": "ERROR",
    "details": [
      {
        "row_number": 12,
        "field": "preparer_adid",
        "error_code": "PREPARER_ADID_NOT_FOUND",
        "message": "The Preparer identifier was not found in the user master."
      }
    ]
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000004"
}
```

For large error lists, return a summary and a paged parse-error endpoint rather than an oversized response.

## 7. Paged Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "items": [],
    "pagination": {
      "page": 1,
      "page_size": 25,
      "total_items": 143,
      "total_pages": 6,
      "has_previous": false,
      "has_next": true
    }
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000005"
}
```

Phase 1 local grid pagination may not require this contract for period-loaded datasets, but server-side administrative and error lists can use it.

## 8. HTTP Status Mapping

```text
200 OK                  Successful read or command response
201 Created             Resource created
202 Accepted            Asynchronous processing accepted, if used
204 No Content          Successful command with no response body, used sparingly
400 Bad Request         Invalid request syntax or field validation
401 Unauthorized        Authentication/token/session failure
403 Forbidden           Role, permission, or ownership failure
404 Not Found           Supported resource does not exist
409 Conflict            Status/concurrency/business-state conflict
413 Payload Too Large   Upload exceeds configured limit
415 Unsupported Media Type
422 Unprocessable Entity Business/input validation, if standardized
500 Internal Server Error
503 Service Unavailable Temporary dependency outage
```

## 9. Authentication Responses

Login success:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {
    "access_token": "<token>",
    "token_type": "Bearer",
    "expires_in": 3600,
    "user": {},
    "roles": ["PREPARER_RECONCILER", "APPROVER"],
    "active_role": "PREPARER_RECONCILER"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000006"
}
```

Refresh tokens should be returned/stored according to the secure cookie strategy described in the security architecture. Do not expose raw server-side token hashes.

## 10. File Upload Response

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": {
    "message_code": "FILE_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY",
    "message": "File uploaded successfully. Click Parse to process the file.",
    "severity": "SUCCESS"
  },
  "data": {
    "file_load_id": "LOAD001",
    "status": "UPLOADED"
  },
  "correlation_id": "CORR-20260820-000007"
}
```

## 11. Request Correlation Header

The server returns:

```http
X-Correlation-ID: CORR-20260820-000007
```

The response-body value and header value must match.

## 12. Schema Organization

Recommended backend schemas:

```text
ApiSuccessResponse[T]
ApiErrorResponse
ApiMessage
ApiError
ApiErrorDetail
PaginationMetadata
```

## 13. Frontend Behavior

The shared API client:

- Reads HTTP status.
- Parses the standard envelope.
- Maps error code and severity.
- Captures correlation ID.
- Triggers centralized session handling for 401.
- Throws/returns a typed application error, not an arbitrary transport exception.

## 14. Acceptance Criteria

- Success and error shapes are consistent across controllers.
- Correlation header and body match.
- Validation details are structured.
- Decimals and dates are serialized safely.
- HTTP status matches the error category.
- Frontend does not need endpoint-specific parsing for common failures.


# Message-Catalog-And-Enums

# Message Catalog and Enums

## 1. Purpose

The message catalog centralizes all user-facing success, error, warning, and information messages. Enums provide stable codes, while display text can be maintained without changing business logic in multiple locations.

## 2. Design Principles

```text
Code decides which message code applies.
Catalog resolves the user-facing text.
UI renders the server-provided safe message and severity.
Repeated messages are not hardcoded in pages or services.
```

## 3. Severity Enum

```text
SUCCESS
ERROR
WARNING
INFO
```

## 4. Error Code Enum

Recommended groups:

### Authentication and Authorization

```text
AUTH_INVALID_CREDENTIALS
AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED
AUTH_SESSION_EXPIRED
AUTH_FORBIDDEN
ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED
```

### Journal

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND
JOURNAL_NOT_ASSIGNED_TO_USER
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION
JOURNAL_CONCURRENT_UPDATE
JOURNAL_PREPARER_APPROVER_SAME_USER
CERTIFICATION_PREREQUISITE_FAILED
DECERTIFICATION_NOT_ALLOWED
```

### File and Parse

```text
FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED
FILE_PARSE_FAILED
FILE_INVALID_FORMAT
FILE_INVALID_COLUMN_COUNT
PREPARER_ADID_NOT_FOUND
APPROVER_ADID_NOT_FOUND
DEPENDENCY_MAPPING_NOT_FOUND
```

### Documents and Comments

```text
DOCUMENT_NOT_FOUND
DOCUMENT_TYPE_NOT_SUPPORTED
DOCUMENT_SIZE_EXCEEDED
DOCUMENT_ACCESS_FORBIDDEN
COMMENT_REQUIRED
COMMENT_TOO_LONG
```

### Currency

```text
CURRENCY_RATE_NOT_FOUND
CURRENCY_RATE_OVERLAP
CURRENCY_IMPORT_FILE_INVALID
CURRENCY_IMPORT_TRANSACTION_FAILED
```

### Infrastructure

```text
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED
DATABASE_TRANSACTION_FAILED
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR
SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE
```

## 5. Success Code Enum

```text
LOGIN_SUCCESSFUL
ROLE_SWITCHED_SUCCESSFULLY
FILE_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY
FILE_PARSED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_CERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_APPROVED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_REJECTED_SUCCESSFULLY
JOURNAL_DECERTIFIED_SUCCESSFULLY
DOCUMENT_UPLOADED_SUCCESSFULLY
COMMENT_ADDED_SUCCESSFULLY
CURRENCY_IMPORT_SUCCESSFUL
```

## 6. Information Code Enum

```text
NO_RECORDS_FOUND
NO_TODO_ITEMS
NO_NOTIFICATIONS
PARSING_IN_PROGRESS
SESSION_EXPIRING_SOON
PREVIEW_NOT_AVAILABLE
FILTER_MINIMUM_CHARACTERS_REQUIRED
```

## 7. Warning Code Enum

```text
UNSAVED_CHANGES
MISSING_OPTIONAL_RATE
JOURNAL_STATUS_CHANGED
SESSION_EXPIRING_WARNING
```

## 8. Backend Catalog

Recommended file:

```text
backend/app/core/message_catalog.py
```

Example conceptual mapping:

```text
JOURNAL_NOT_FOUND -> Journal was not found.
JOURNAL_INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION -> This journal action is not allowed in the current status.
AUDIT_LOGGING_FAILED -> Action could not be completed because audit logging failed. Please retry or contact support.
INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR -> Something went wrong. Please try again or contact support.
```

## 9. Message Parameters

Messages may use safe named parameters:

```text
File {file_name} uploaded successfully.
Showing {start}-{end} of {total}.
```

Do not format messages using untrusted HTML. Parameters are escaped and rendered as text.

## 10. Localization Readiness

Even if Phase 1 uses English only:

- Codes remain language neutral.
- Avoid concatenating sentence fragments in components.
- Keep display text in catalogs/resources.
- Dates and numbers use formatting utilities.

## 11. Frontend Message Mapping

The frontend may maintain a limited fallback catalog for network errors and cases where no API response is available. The server message is authoritative for business failures.

Recommended files:

```text
notification.types.ts
notificationMapper.ts
fallbackMessageCatalog.ts
```

## 12. Naming Standards

- Codes use uppercase `SNAKE_CASE`.
- Codes describe the condition, not the current UI component.
- Do not include external product names.
- Do not reuse one code for unrelated conditions.
- Codes are append-only where external clients may depend on them.

## 13. Governance

Adding a message requires:

1. Add/update enum.
2. Add catalog text.
3. Map HTTP status and severity.
4. Add tests.
5. Update API documentation where externally visible.

## 14. Testing

Tests validate:

- Every enum has a catalog entry.
- No duplicate codes.
- Required placeholders are supplied.
- No message exposes secrets or technical details.
- Error codes map to expected HTTP status.

## 15. Acceptance Criteria

- Common messages are not duplicated across pages.
- Every API failure uses an enum code.
- Every success command can return a success code.
- Catalog text is safe and actionable.
- External product names are absent.
- Fallback messages exist for network and unknown failures.


# Notification-Framework

# Notification Framework

## 1. Purpose

The notification framework provides one modular way to display immediate success, error, warning, and information feedback throughout the application.

This framework is distinct from persistent header notifications. Immediate notifications describe the result of the action currently performed by the user.

## 2. Supported Presentation Types

### Toast

Used for routine success and information messages.

### Sticky Toast

Used for recoverable errors or warnings requiring attention.

### Modal Dialog

Used for critical transaction failures, parse failures, authorization issues requiring explanation, or detailed row-level errors.

### Inline Banner

Used for page-level information, warnings, or partial-data states.

### Field-Level Message

Used for request validation near the invalid control.

## 3. Severity Rules

```text
SUCCESS -> success toast, normally auto-dismiss
INFO    -> toast or inline banner
WARNING -> persistent toast or banner
ERROR   -> sticky toast or modal based on impact
```

## 4. Shared Frontend Structure

```text
frontend/modules/shared/notifications/
  components/
    AppNotificationProvider.tsx
    NotificationToast.tsx
    NotificationDialog.tsx
    NotificationBanner.tsx
    ValidationSummary.tsx
  hooks/
    useNotification.ts
  services/
    notificationMapper.ts
  types/
    notification.types.ts
  styles/
```

## 5. Notification Object

```text
code
message
severity
details
correlation_id
auto_dismiss
duration_ms
action_label
action_callback
```

## 6. API Integration

The shared API client maps standardized success/error envelopes to typed notification objects.

```text
API response
  -> parse response contract
  -> map code/severity/details
  -> return typed result
  -> page/service triggers appropriate presentation
```

Not every successful GET should show a toast. Command responses can request success feedback; background reads remain quiet unless they fail.

## 7. Workflow Messages

### Certify

```text
Certified successfully.
```

### Approve

```text
Approved successfully.
```

### Reject

```text
Rejected successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

### Decertify

```text
Decertified successfully and returned to Preparer queue.
```

### Action Failure

```text
Action failed. No changes were saved.
```

## 8. Parse Messages

### Upload

```text
File uploaded successfully. Click Parse to process the file.
```

### Parsing

```text
Parsing is in progress.
```

### Success

```text
File parsed successfully.
```

### Failure

```text
Parse failed. No journal data was created.
```

Parse failure uses a dialog or page panel with row-level details and correlation ID.

## 9. Session Messages

### Expired

```text
Your session has expired. Please log in again.
```

The notification is shown before redirecting to Login where technically practical.

### Forbidden

```text
You are not authorized to perform this action.
```

## 10. Deduplication

The provider prevents duplicate notifications caused by repeated renders or multiple interceptors. A notification may be deduplicated using:

```text
code + correlation_id + entity_id
```

## 11. Queue Behavior

- Limit visible toasts.
- Queue additional messages.
- Critical errors take priority.
- A modal is never hidden behind a toast.
- Role switch and navigation clear obsolete transient notifications where appropriate.

## 12. Accessibility

- Use suitable ARIA live regions.
- Errors remain visible long enough to read.
- Dialog focus is trapped and restored.
- Close buttons have labels.
- Severity is conveyed by text/icon, not color only.
- Correlation ID can be copied.

## 13. Styling

Notifications follow the enterprise theme and use neutral component names. Do not copy proprietary class names. Use consistent colors, typography, spacing, and iconography.

## 14. Persistent Header Notifications

Persistent workflow notifications are stored and retrieved through the separate Notifications module. The immediate framework may also show a toast when a persistent notification is generated.

## 15. Testing

- Success toast appears once.
- Error modal shows details and correlation ID.
- 401 triggers session handling.
- 403 triggers access-denied message.
- Field validation maps to controls.
- Notifications are keyboard accessible.
- No `window.alert` is used.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Every module uses the shared provider.
- Messages come from standard API contracts/catalogs.
- Raw exceptions never appear.
- Critical errors remain visible.
- Duplicate messages are controlled.
- Persistent and transient notifications are clearly separated.


# Correlation-Id-And-Logging

# Correlation ID and Logging

## 1. Purpose

Correlation IDs connect frontend errors, API requests, backend logs, database failures, and support investigations without exposing internal technical details to users.

## 2. Correlation ID Lifecycle

1. Accept a valid incoming `X-Correlation-ID` from a trusted gateway/client, or generate a new ID.
2. Store it in request context.
3. Include it in every log entry for that request.
4. Return it in the response header.
5. Return it in the standard response body.
6. Display it in support-worthy error notifications.

## 3. Format

Use a globally unique, non-sensitive value such as a UUID or approved platform format.

Example:

```text
CORR-20260820-8f17a84d
```

Do not encode user IDs, email addresses, tokens, or business-sensitive values in the correlation ID.

## 4. FastAPI Middleware

Recommended middleware responsibilities:

- Read/generate correlation ID.
- Attach ID to request state/context variable.
- Set response header.
- Record request start/end.
- Record duration and status code.
- Clear context after request completion.

## 5. Frontend Handling

The API client reads correlation ID from the body or `X-Correlation-ID` header and attaches it to the typed error.

Error dialog example:

```text
Something went wrong. Please try again or contact support.
Correlation ID: CORR-20260820-8f17a84d
```

Provide a copy action when practical.

## 6. Structured Logging

Use structured JSON logs in AWS environments. Recommended fields:

```text
timestamp
log_level
message
correlation_id
request_method
request_path
http_status
duration_ms
user_id
active_role
session_id, hashed or safe reference
entity_type
entity_id
error_code
exception_type
service_name
environment
```

## 7. Sensitive Data Rules

Never log:

- Passwords.
- Password hashes.
- Access tokens.
- Refresh tokens.
- Authorization headers.
- Full session secrets.
- File binary content.
- Sensitive document content.
- Entire request bodies by default.
- Database connection strings with passwords.

Mask or omit sensitive fields.

## 8. Log Levels

```text
DEBUG -> local diagnostics, disabled/restricted in production
INFO  -> successful lifecycle events and operational milestones
WARNING -> recoverable abnormal conditions
ERROR -> failed operation requiring investigation
CRITICAL -> broad outage or integrity threat
```

Business validation failures usually log at INFO or WARNING, not ERROR, unless they indicate invalid system configuration.

## 9. Workflow Logging

Certify, Approve, Reject, and Decertify produce:

- Immutable journal audit event for business history.
- Application log entry for technical operations.

Application logs do not replace audit records.

## 10. Parse Logging

Log:

- File load ID.
- File name where safe.
- Row counts.
- Parse start/end.
- Validation summary.
- Transaction commit/rollback.
- Error code and correlation ID.

Do not log every raw source row in production.

## 11. AWS CloudWatch Direction

For AWS deployment:

- Send backend structured logs to CloudWatch Logs.
- Configure retention according to corporate policy.
- Create metrics/alarms for 5xx rates, authentication failures, parse failures, and latency.
- Use correlation ID in CloudWatch search and support runbooks.

## 12. Frontend Logging

Frontend logging is limited and sanitized. Browser console output must not contain tokens or sensitive journal data. Production client errors may be sent to an approved monitoring platform with correlation ID and safe metadata.

## 13. Audit Versus Application Log

```text
Audit Trail -> Who changed business state, what changed, before/after status.
Application Log -> How the request executed and why a technical failure occurred.
```

Audit data is immutable and business-facing. Application logs are operational and access controlled.

## 14. Error Handler Integration

Global exception handlers:

- Log technical exception with stack trace server-side.
- Use the current correlation ID.
- Map to safe error code/message.
- Return the standard response contract.

## 15. Operational Search Examples

Support can search by:

```text
correlation_id
journal_id
file_load_id
error_code
user_id
request_path
```

Access to logs must be restricted and monitored.

## 16. Acceptance Criteria

- Every API response contains one correlation ID.
- Header and body IDs match.
- Every request log contains the ID.
- Error popups show the ID for significant failures.
- Sensitive values are masked or omitted.
- Workflow audit and technical logs remain separate.
- AWS logging is structured and searchable.
